# Architecture Decision Record — Gma's Helper

> ⬆ Strategy sits above this ledger in [STRATEGY.md](./STRATEGY.md) — the one-page thesis on why the product wins and the priority between the derivation engine and reach. This file remains the canonical record of engineering decisions.

## Overview

Gma's Helper (working title; BMad project name: "Happy") is a single-page web app that shows active cannabis happy-hour deals within a user-set road-distance radius from the user's location. Each listing shows miles to the shop and a gas-cost-vs-savings calculation. No browsing, no discovery — just "is this deal worth the drive, right now?"

**Status:** LIVE in production at gmaslist.com (Render). All numeric epics (1–4, 6) shipped and deployed, and the Tier-1 derivation engine shipped through Epics 1–3 (honest cross-store relational facts). 21 WA stores in the dataset, all geocoded; deals arrive via ADR-034 push-ingest (GitHub Actions hourly cron → `/api/ingest`), server read-only. The original scrape-at-request and national-MPG-default paths were superseded (ADR-034, ADR-055). Current frontier per STRATEGY.md: AI-search / crawler visibility to earn reach, while the engine's price-history facts accrue on their own. *(The prior status through 2026-06-13 — "code-complete, data-complete for only 1 of 4 sources" — is obsolete; its detail is preserved in the change log.)*
**Owner:** Erik (solo founder), Marysville WA area.

---

## Architecture Decisions

### ADR-001: Lightweight Single-Page Web App (no native app)
**Status:** Accepted
**Date:** 2026-06-08
**Context:** Cannabis apps face app-store gatekeeping — Google Play bans cannabis-sale-facilitating apps outright (enforced as of 2025-2026); Apple allows licensed dispensary apps but with geo-restrictions. Texas's App Store Accountability Act (effective 2026-01-01) adds age-verification burdens.
**Decision:** Build as a lightweight browser-based single-page web app. No native mobile app, no app-store presence.
**Rationale:** Sidesteps Google's outright ban and emerging age-verification law entirely. Accessible on any device via browser.
**Consequences:** No push notifications, no app-store discovery. Must be mobile-responsive. Hosting/deployment simpler than native.
**Testing:** N/A at this stage.

### ADR-002: Road-Distance Routing (not straight-line radius)
**Status:** Superseded by ADR-011
**Date:** 2026-06-08
**Context:** User explicitly specified 50-road-miles from zip 98270 (Marysville, WA), excluding destinations requiring ferry crossings (Olympic Peninsula, Bremerton).
**Decision:** Use driving-route distance, not straight-line/as-the-crow-flies radius math.
**Rationale:** Straight-line radius would include unreachable destinations across Puget Sound. Road-distance accurately reflects "is this worth the drive."
**Consequences:** Requires a routing/driving-distance API (meaningfully costlier and more complex than a naive geo-radius filter). Need to evaluate API options (Google Maps Routes API, OSRM, etc.) during architecture phase.
**Testing:** Validate by comparing selected WA dispensary addresses against expected include/exclude results.
**Superseded by:** ADR-011 (hardcoded JSON lookups eliminate the routing API entirely for R&D).

### ADR-003: Gas-Cost Calculator — fueleconomy.gov API + Hardcoded National MPG
**Status:** Accepted (refined by ADR-013); **default-MPG half retired by ADR-055** (2026-06-26) — vehicle-precision mode via fueleconomy.gov retained, no national-average default
**Date:** 2026-06-08 / Refined 2026-06-09
**Context:** Core differentiator is the gas-cost-vs-savings comparison. Need vehicle MPG data.
**Decision:** Default MPG is hardcoded at 28 (US fleet average) — see ADR-013. Optional precision mode via fueleconomy.gov public API (Year/Make/Model cascading dropdowns, persisted in browser localStorage). fueleconomy.gov API returns XML; client must send `Accept: application/json` header.
**Rationale:** fueleconomy.gov is a free US government API with proper endpoints intended for third-party use — no scraping, no cost, no ToS risk. The default path requires zero user setup.
**Consequences:** Dependency on fueleconomy.gov availability for precision mode. Fallback: show error in dropdown, Gas Cost falls back to nationalMpg silently.
**Testing:** Verify fueleconomy.gov API returns expected MPG values for known Year/Make/Model combinations. Verify localStorage persistence across sessions.

### ADR-004: Scrape/Aggregate for Deal Sourcing
**Status:** Accepted (with known open risk)
**Date:** 2026-06-08
**Context:** No centralized real-time deal feed exists for cannabis dispensaries. Deals live on individual dispensary websites, SMS clubs, and Instagram — fragmented.
**Decision:** Scrape and aggregate from publicly available dispensary websites and menus. No manual curation, no dispensary self-submission at launch.
**Rationale:** Only feasible solo-founder approach at launch. Keeps the system independent of dispensary cooperation.
**Consequences:** Open risks — (1) ToS/legal exposure from scraping dispensary sites; (2) operational fragility when sites change layout; (3) data freshness challenges. These must be addressed in architecture work: monitoring for breakage, stale-data fallbacks, legal/ethics position on scraping cannabis retail sites specifically.
**Testing:** Accuracy check — small test group (Erik, wife, possibly a friend) verifies displayed deals match real-world deals when they walk in the door.

### ADR-006: Own-Site Crawling Only for v1 (no aggregator scraping)
**Status:** Accepted
**Date:** 2026-06-08
**Context:** Pre-PRD crawl spike (2026-06-08) confirmed ~60% of area dispensaries serve deal data as plain crawlable HTML. Weedmaps/Leafly deal detail requires JS rendering or private API access. Dispensaries using third-party menu subdomains exclusively have no crawlable own-site deal data.
**Decision:** v1 scrapes own-site HTML only. Weedmaps/Leafly deferred to Phase 2. Dispensaries without crawlable own-site deal data excluded from v1 Coverage Zone.
**Rationale:** Simplest viable approach for R&D validation. Avoids JS rendering dependency and aggregator ToS exposure.
**Consequences:** Coverage is ~60% of area dispensaries. Remaining ~40% excluded until Phase 2. Each new dispensary requires a manually written parser.
**Testing:** Spike confirmed Wild Seed Wellness, Remedy Tulalip, Hangar 420 are crawlable. Parser accuracy validated by test-group trips.

### ADR-007: Happy Hours Primary, Daily Deals Secondary
**Status:** Accepted
**Date:** 2026-06-08
**Context:** Spike found two deal archetypes: time-windowed Happy Hours (e.g., Remedy Tulalip 7–8am, Hangar 420 9pm–close) and all-day Daily Deals (e.g., Wild Seed Wellness brand deals).
**Decision:** Feed surfaces Happy Hours first (sorted by time remaining), then Daily Deals (sorted by discount % descending). Both types are shown; Happy Hours are the priority signal.
**Rationale:** Time-windowed deals are higher-urgency; they expire. Daily Deals fill the feed when no Happy Hours are active.
**Consequences:** Scraper must classify deals by type. Happy Hours without parseable time windows fall back to Daily Deal classification.
**Testing:** Verify sort order and classification in feed during R&D.

### ADR-008: Fixed Origin Point at Zip 98270 for v1 (no user GPS)
**Status:** Accepted (assumption — not explicitly confirmed by Erik)
**Date:** 2026-06-08
**Context:** All Road Distance and Gas Cost calculations require a starting point. Browser geolocation would improve accuracy for users not in Marysville but adds complexity.
**Decision:** Origin fixed at zip 98270 centroid for v1. No browser GPS/geolocation used.
**Rationale:** All R&D test users are in Marysville. Eliminates geolocation permission UX and simplifies routing calls. Revisit if concept validates beyond the local test group.
**Consequences:** Gas Cost estimates are inaccurate for users not in Marysville. Acceptable at R&D scale.
**Testing:** N/A — decision is a simplification for R&D.

### ADR-009: Side-by-Side Discount Display (not computed Net Savings dollar figure)
**Status:** Accepted
**Date:** 2026-06-08
**Context:** PRD authoring revealed deals are published as percentages (e.g., "35% off"), not dollar amounts. Computing a dollar Net Savings requires knowing the user's intended spend, which is unknown.
**Decision:** v1 shows "X% off — $Y to get there" side by side (Discount Display) rather than a collapsed Net Savings number.
**Rationale:** Side-by-side is honest — no invented basket size. SM-3 in the PRD validates whether users find this sufficient to make a go/no-go decision.
**Consequences:** The "is it worth it?" verdict is less instant than a single number. If SM-3 fails (users want a dollar figure), Phase 2 options are: fixed assumed basket size, or user-entered intended spend.
**Testing:** SM-3 — test group confirms Discount Display was sufficient for trip decisions.

### ADR-010: Lean R&D Stack (Vite + React + Express + data.json)
**Status:** Accepted
**Date:** 2026-06-09
**Context:** Architecture phase — need a full-stack for R&D validation. Priorities: zero infrastructure cost, zero setup friction, maximum iteration speed for solo founder.
**Decision:** Frontend: Vite 6.3 + React + TypeScript + Tailwind CSS v4. Backend: Express v5 + Node.js + TypeScript (tsx). Storage: data.json flat file. Scraper: Axios 1.17 + Cheerio 1.2. Scheduler: setInterval in Express process.
**Rationale:** Every choice eliminates a dependency that would slow R&D validation. No database migrations, no cloud scheduler, no infrastructure provisioning.
**Consequences:** data.json will not scale past ~50 dispensaries without migration to SQLite. setInterval is not production-grade scheduling. Both acceptable for R&D phase.
**Testing:** R&D validation trip accuracy (SM-1, SM-2).

### ADR-011: Hardcoded Road Distance Lookups (no routing API for v1)
**Status:** Accepted — supersedes ADR-002 for R&D
**Date:** 2026-06-09
**Context:** ADR-002 flagged routing API as required. Architecture phase evaluated cost vs. R&D scale (~20–50 dispensaries, static locations, fixed origin at 98270).
**Decision:** Pre-compute road distance (via Google Maps, once) from zip 98270 to each dispensary and hardcode in `data.json` as `distanceMiles`. No routing API call at runtime.
**Rationale:** Dispensary locations are static. Zero API cost, zero latency, zero failure mode. Ferry exclusion handled at data-entry time (excluded dispensaries never added).
**Consequences:** Adding a new dispensary requires a one-time manual distance lookup. Not suitable if user-variable origin points are added later.
**Testing:** Spot-check computed distances against Google Maps for 3–5 dispensaries.

### ADR-012: EIA API for Gas Price (daily ≤24h refresh)
**Status:** Accepted
**Date:** 2026-06-09
**Context:** PRD OQ-1 — needed a reliable public source for US average gas price. Options: EIA (US govt), GasBuddy (unofficial API, fragile), AAA (no public API, requires scraping).
**Decision:** EIA (Energy Information Administration) public API. Refreshed daily (≤24h) in `refreshGasPrice.ts`. API key stored in `.env` as `EIA_API_KEY`.
**Rationale:** Free, official, stable. Daily (≤24h) cadence is sufficient — gas price fluctuations don't flip go/no-go decisions. GasBuddy/AAA scraping would be a maintenance liability.
**Consequences:** Gas price may be up to 24h stale. Acceptable given SM-2's 15% accuracy margin.
**Testing:** SM-2 — gas cost within 15% of actual trip fuel cost.

### ADR-013: Hardcoded National Average MPG (28 for R&D)
**Status:** ~~Accepted~~ **Superseded by ADR-055** (2026-06-26) — the hardcoded `nationalMpg` default was removed; MPG now comes only from the user's selected vehicle
**Date:** 2026-06-09
**Context:** PRD required a daily-refreshed national-average MPG figure. No reliable live API found for a single fleet-average number (fueleconomy.gov endpoints are vehicle-specific, not fleet-average).
**Decision:** Hardcode `nationalMpg: 28` in `data.json` meta. Update manually if fleet average shifts significantly.
**Rationale:** US fleet average consistently ~28–30 MPG. Satisfies SM-2 (15% accuracy margin). Eliminates a live API dependency.
**Consequences:** Inaccurate for heavy truck owners; precision mode (ADR-003) mitigates this. If users complain math feels off, add a simple "Your MPG" text input later.
**Testing:** SM-2 — gas cost within 15% of actual trip fuel cost.

### ADR-014: data.json + logs.json Flat File Storage
**Status:** Accepted
**Date:** 2026-06-09
**Context:** Need persistent storage for dispensary config, cached deals, gas price meta, and scraper run logs.
**Decision:** Two JSON files: `data.json` (frontend-served data) and `logs.json` (operator-only scraper log). Writes are atomic (write to `data.tmp.json`, rename). `logs.json` never served to frontend.
**Rationale:** Zero cost, zero setup, zero migrations. Sufficient for R&D scale. Separation of concerns: scraper log bloat doesn't affect frontend payload.
**Consequences:** Not suitable for concurrent writes (single-process Node.js avoids this at R&D scale). Upgrade path: SQLite when dispensary count exceeds ~50.
**Testing:** Verify atomic write prevents partial data from being served during scraper runs.

### ADR-015: Single GET /api/data Endpoint with Server-Side Active Deal Filtering
**Status:** Accepted
**Date:** 2026-06-09
**Context:** Need API design for frontend to consume deal data. Active deal status depends on Pacific Time — timezone logic must be centralized.
**Decision:** Single `GET /api/data` endpoint returns `{ meta, dispensaries[] }` with active deals pre-filtered. Server enforces `TZ=America/Los_Angeles` as the first line of `index.ts`.
**Rationale:** Timezone logic in one place (server). Frontend stays stateless — client-side filter is distance only.
**Consequences:** All time-zone sensitive logic couples to the server. Acceptable — this is a feature, not a bug.
**Testing:** Verify Happy Hours that have expired do not appear in API response. Verify Daily Deals expire at Pacific midnight.

### ADR-016: Axios + Cheerio Scraper with Python Microservice Upgrade Path
**Status:** Accepted (refined by ADR-017)
**Date:** 2026-06-09
**Context:** Need to scrape dispensary HTML for deal data. Most target sites serve plain HTML; some require JS rendering (Dutchie iFrame menus specifically).
**Decision:** Axios + Cheerio for plain HTML scrapers. Upgrade individual scrapers to call the Python Scraper microservice (ADR-017) if a site requires a real browser runtime.
**Rationale:** Cheerio is lightweight and fast for plain HTML. The Python Scraper handles browser complexity without bringing Playwright into the Node.js process.
**Consequences:** Sites that load deals via JavaScript will fail silently (empty `Deal[]` returned) until upgraded to call the Python service.
**Testing:** Verify each dispensary scraper returns expected Deal[] before adding to production data.json.

### ADR-017: Python Scraper Microservice for Dutchie/iFrame Dispensaries
**Status:** Accepted
**Date:** 2026-06-09
**Context:** Dutchie iFrame menus cannot be scraped by Axios+Cheerio — menu data is loaded by JavaScript inside an iFrame and is invisible to standard HTTP clients. Happy Time (the primary target dispensary) uses Dutchie for all three WA locations (Mt Vernon, Pullman, Yakima). A working Python Playwright microservice already exists at `C:\Users\erikc\Dev\Scraper` (FastAPI on port 8000), tested against live Dutchie menus.
**Decision:** Dutchie dispensary scraper files in `server/scrapers/` call `POST http://localhost:8000/scrape` on the Python Scraper service via a shared `server/utils/scraperClient.ts` wrapper. The TypeScript scraper file transforms raw GraphQL JSON (`intercepted[].data`) → `Deal[]`. The scraper contract (`export default async function scrape(): Promise<Deal[]>`) is unchanged — `runScrapers.ts` has no knowledge of which tier a scraper uses.
**Rationale:** The Python Scraper encapsulates browser automation, playwright-stealth fingerprint spoofing, and GraphQL network interception. Isolating this complexity in a separate service means Dutchie API changes, stealth patches, and browser engine upgrades never touch the Node.js codebase. The Happy scraper files stay thin — they own only the business logic (GraphQL → Deal transform).
**Consequences:** Dutchie scrapers require the Python service on port 8000. Local dev: start Python service alongside Node.js. Production: both processes must be deployed (Docker Compose at `C:\Users\erikc\Dev\Scraper\docker-compose.yml`). If the service is unavailable, `scraperClient.ts` catches the error and the scraper returns `[]` — existing stale-handling in `runScrapers.ts` applies.
**Testing:** Verify Happy Time Mt Vernon `scrape()` returns non-empty `Deal[]` with Scraper service running. Verify `[]` returned (no throw) when service is down.

### ADR-018: Build-Time Data Copy Script for dist/
**Status:** Accepted
**Date:** 2026-06-09
**Context:** Code review of Story 1.3 found that `tsc` only compiles `.ts` files — `server/data/data.json` and `server/data/logs.json` were never copied to `dist/`, so `dataRoute.ts`'s `__dirname`-relative `DATA_PATH` resolved to a non-existent file in production builds, causing `/api/data` to return 500.
**Decision:** Added `server/scripts/copyData.mjs` (Node `cpSync`/`mkdirSync`, cross-platform) that copies `server/data/` → `dist/server/data/`, run via `server/package.json`'s `build` script (`tsc && node scripts/copyData.mjs`).
**Rationale:** Keeps `__dirname`-relative paths in `dataRoute.ts` valid at the same relative depth in both dev (tsx) and built (`dist/`) environments, without changing the route code itself.
**Consequences:** Any future non-`.ts` runtime asset under `server/` must be added to `copyData.mjs` or it will be silently missing from `dist/`.
**Testing:** Verified end-to-end — built the project, ran `dist/server/index.js` with `NODE_ENV=production`, confirmed `GET /api/data` returns 200 with correct data (previously 500/ENOENT).

### ADR-019: Root package.json start/build Scripts Aligned to dist/server/ Output
**Status:** Accepted
**Date:** 2026-06-09
**Context:** Following ADR-015's `rootDir: ".."` change, `tsc` output moved from `server/dist/index.js` to `server/dist/server/index.js`, but the root `package.json`'s `start` script (`node server/dist/index.js`) and `build` script (bare `cd server && npx tsc`, skipping `copyData.mjs`) were not updated — found during Story 1.3 code review.
**Decision:** Root `start` → `node server/dist/server/index.js`. Root `build` → `npm run build --prefix client && npm run build --prefix server` (delegates to server's own build script, which includes `copyData.mjs`).
**Rationale:** Single source of truth for the server build process lives in `server/package.json`; the root scripts should delegate rather than duplicate/diverge.
**Consequences:** None — root scripts now match actual build output paths.
**Testing:** Verified `npm run build` (root) → `npm start` (root) → `GET /api/data` returns 200.

### ADR-020: Overnight Deal Time-Window Handling in filterActiveDeals
**Status:** Accepted
**Date:** 2026-06-09
**Context:** Story 1.3 code review found `isDealActive` only handled same-day windows (`nowMinutes >= start && nowMinutes < end`). Happy Hour deals spanning midnight (e.g., `22:00`–`02:00`, per ADR-007) would never be active, since `end < start` makes the comparison always false.
**Decision:** `isDealActive` now detects overnight windows (`endMinutes <= startMinutes`) and treats them as active if (today is in `daysValid` AND now ≥ startTime) OR (yesterday is in `daysValid` AND now < endTime).
**Rationale:** `daysValid` lists the day the deal *starts*; the post-midnight portion of an overnight deal belongs to the previous day's entry, not the calendar day it's currently active on.
**Consequences:** None — same-day window logic unchanged; only the `end <= start` branch is new.
**Testing:** Added 3 unit tests to `filterActiveDeals.test.ts` covering: active before midnight, active after midnight (previous day in `daysValid`), and expired the next morning. Full suite: 13/13 passing.

### ADR-021: Age Gate — Single-Button Design, Strict Boolean Check, Dialog A11y
**Status**: Superseded by ADR-036 (2026-06-19) — the strict-boolean check and dialog a11y carry forward; the single-button, no-decline design is replaced by a two-option gate with returnable decline + "Remember me." (ADR-035 first added the decline affordance; ADR-036 made it returnable and added remember-me.)
**Date**: 2026-06-10
**Context**: Story 2.1 implemented the 21+ age gate (`AgeGate.tsx` wrapping App content, persisted via generic `useLocalStorage` hook under `gma_age_confirmed`). Adversarial code review (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter + Acceptance Auditor) found no AC violations but surfaced hardening issues and one product decision.
**Decision**: (1) Single-button gate ("I am 21 or older" only, no under-21 decline path) is the intended design — explicitly dismissed in review. (2) Gate check is strict `ageConfirmed === true`, not truthiness — `JSON.parse(item) as T` in the generic hook is an unchecked cast, so corrupted/hand-edited localStorage values (e.g. `1`, `"yes"`) must not open the gate. (3) Overlay carries `role="alertdialog"`, `aria-modal`, a labelled heading, and moves focus to the confirm button on mount; button contrast raised to `bg-green-700` for WCAG AA. (4) `setValue` wrapped in `useCallback` for stable identity.
**Rationale**: WA-compliance gate is a click-through attestation, not security; one button keeps it frictionless. The strict check costs nothing and closes the only code path where garbage storage data bypasses the gate. Dialog semantics make the takeover screen non-broken for screen-reader/keyboard users.
**Consequences**: Deferred to `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/deferred-work.md`: no cross-tab storage sync, no functional-update form on `setValue`, hook ignores `key` changes after mount. None affect the single-key, single-tab R&D use case. Confirmation never expires (persists in localStorage indefinitely) — required by AC3.
**Testing**: 18 client tests passing (parameterized gate-bypass cases, storage-throws branches, focus/dialog-role assertions, setValue identity). `tsc -b` and lint clean.
**Update (2026-06-18, compliance-launch-gate → ADR-035)**: Decision (1) revised. The single-button design is **superseded** — a secondary ghost "I am under 21" action plus a terminal dead-end screen ("This site is for adults 21 and over.") was added per the regulated-content review's defensibility finding. The decline is session-only state (never persisted, never sets `gma_age_confirmed`) so a reload re-shows the normal gate; the affirm path, strict boolean check, dialog a11y, and focus-on-mount are unchanged. See ADR-035.

### ADR-022: Deal Feed — Hook-Only Data Access, Pure Sort Utility, Pinned en-US Timestamp Format
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-06-10
**Context**: Story 2.2 added the deal feed: `useDeals` hook (sole fetch path), `sortDeals` pure utility, `formatLastUpdated`, and `DealFeed` owning loading/error/empty/populated states, mounted under the header inside `AgeGate`.
**Decision**: (1) `useDeals` fetches `/api/data` once on mount with `AbortController` cleanup; abort on unmount is swallowed (`err.name === 'AbortError'`) and never sets state — non-OK status and network failure set `error` (string), leave `data` null, no throw. (2) `sortDeals(dispensaries, now)` takes `now: Date` as a parameter (never reads the clock itself) and sorts via tiered keys: timed Happy Hours by minutes-until-end ascending — negative deltas wrap +1440 for overnight windows (e.g. 22:00–02:00, mirroring server-side ADR-020) — then null-window Happy Hours (stable input order), then Daily Deals by `discountPct` descending. (3) `formatLastUpdated` pins `toLocaleString('en-US', …)` → "Jun 10, 7:45 AM" so output doesn't drift with host locale; invalid ISO → empty string. (4) Error state shows a fixed friendly message — raw error text/status never rendered; timestamp footer renders in populated AND empty states only.
**Rationale**: Passing `now` in keeps the sort pure and unit-testable without fake timers. Pinning en-US makes tests deterministic across machines and matches the spec's example format. Hook-only data access enforces the architecture rule (zero `fetch()` in components) ahead of Stories 2.3–2.6 building on the same hook.
**Consequences**: Single fetch per mount — no polling/refresh yet (stale handling is Story 2.6). Minimal deal rows (name — description — % off) are placeholder UI replaced by `DealCard` in Story 2.3. Within-tier order of null-window Happy Hours is input order (unspecified by spec).
**Testing**: 38 client tests passing (18 pre-existing + 20 new: hook success/500/network-reject/abort-on-unmount, all I/O-matrix sort cases incl. overnight wrap, format valid/invalid, four DealFeed render states + sort order + timestamp presence, App feed-region render with stubbed fetch). `tsc -b` and lint clean.

### ADR-023: Deal Cards — Single Time-Window Predicate, 60s Clock, Server-Mirror for Degenerate Shapes
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-06-10
**Context**: Story 2.3 replaced the 2.2 placeholder rows with `DealCard` and added a live countdown plus client-side expiry. Adversarial review caught the implementation inventing "end-only deal = since-midnight window" semantics that contradicted the server (which treats ANY null time as day-long-active), and three subsystems (display, expiry, sort) routing degenerate time shapes inconsistently.
**Decision**: (1) One predicate, `hasValidTimedWindow(deal)` (both times present AND parseable), drives expiry, countdown, and sort-tier routing — only fully-valid timed windows compete in the urgency tier, get a countdown, or expire client-side. (2) Every other shape (null, partial, malformed times) mirrors the server's null-time behavior: always shown, never client-expired, sorted with the all-day tier. (3) A single `useNow` hook (60s `setInterval`, cleanup on unmount) is the only clock; the expiry filter runs BEFORE `sortDeals` so expired deals never reach the overnight-wrap heuristic. (4) `DealCard` is purely presentational — window/countdown strings computed in `DealFeed`, passed as props. (5) Window copy ladder: "9:00 PM – 11:30 PM" / "9:00 PM – close" / "Until 11:30 PM" (end-only, display-only) / "Active today".
**Rationale**: Splitting time policy across display/expiry/sort caused the exact disagreements review found (a malformed-start deal sorting as urgent while rendering as malformed and living forever). A single predicate makes disagreement structurally impossible. Mirroring the server for degenerate shapes keeps client and server answering "is this active?" identically — the client tick only ever removes what the server would also drop.
**Consequences**: Deferred (see `deferred-work.md`): long-open tabs resurrect stale deals when their clock window next matches (no refetch is a frozen 2.3 boundary — needs monotonic removal or polling, Epic 3 candidate); no `visibilitychange` resync on mobile; server accepts zero-length windows (`start === end` = 24h-active) — scraper validation in Epic 4 should normalize. Countdown can lag up to ~59s (mount-anchored tick, minute precision) — accepted for R&D.
**Testing**: 82 client tests passing (77 from implementation + 5 review regressions: server-mirror end-only, `hasValidTimedWindow` shapes, "– close"/"Until X" feed derivations, malformed-start tiering). `tsc -b` and lint clean.

### ADR-024: Gas Cost — Pure Formula Module with Shared Validity Predicate, Number-Only MPG Contract
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-06-10
**Context**: Story 2.4 delivered the product's core differentiator (ADR-009 side-by-side Discount Display: "35% off — $1.80 to get there"). Review found the MPG-validity rule duplicated between `DealFeed` and `gasCost.ts`, and no guard against a non-finite product reaching display.
**Decision**: (1) `roundTripGasCost(distanceMiles, gasPrice, mpg)` in `client/src/utils/gasCost.ts` is the ONLY home of the formula `(distanceMiles × 2) × (gasPrice / mpg)`; returns null on any non-positive/non-finite input OR non-finite product — callers render the discount alone, never a broken fragment. (2) `isPositiveFinite` is exported and reused by `DealFeed` for the vehicle-MPG fallback so the validity rule has one definition. (3) `gma_vehicle_mpg` contract: the stored value must be a JSON **number**; strings (even `"20"`), booleans, ≤ 0, and garbage silently fall back to `meta.nationalMpg` — locked by test so Epic 3's writer (Story 3.2) must serialize a number.
**Rationale**: Money math scattered across components is how display and calculation drift; null-propagation beats defensive formatting (`$NaN`/`$Infinity` can never render). The number-only localStorage contract is asserted now, while the reader is built, so the Epic 3 writer has a failing test to satisfy rather than a silent mismatch.
**Consequences**: Distance 0 and sub-cent costs are treated as "no gas line" — irrelevant for hardcoded R&D distances (all > 1 mile). `toFixed(2)` half-cent float rounding accepted (estimate with SM-2's 15% margin). Cross-tab MPG staleness inherits the known `useLocalStorage` limitation (deferred since 2.1).
**Testing**: 97 client tests passing (+15 this story: formula matrix math $1.46/$2.05/$3.63, full invalid-input sweep, overflow guard, vehicle/garbage/JSON-string MPG fallbacks, per-dispensary distances, gasPrice-0 degradation). `tsc -b` and lint clean.

### ADR-025: Distance Filter — Whole-Mile Validation with Fallback-Not-Clamp, Shared Range Constants
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-06-10
**Context**: Story 2.5 added the distance slider (1–50, default 25, `gma_distance_miles`). The stored value arrives through the JSON-parsing `useLocalStorage` hook, so anything can come back; review found the 1/50/25 literals duplicated between the validator and the slider markup, and fractional values (e.g. `12.5`) slipping past a finite-only check despite the "whole miles" rule.
**Decision**: (1) Stored value counts ONLY as an integer within [1, 50]; anything else (garbage, fractional, out-of-range, JSON string) silently falls back to 25 — fallback, not clamp, mirroring the 2.4 MPG precedent: an out-of-contract value is untrusted entirely rather than "repaired". (2) `MIN/MAX/DEFAULT_DISTANCE_MILES` are exported from `DistanceFilter.tsx` as the single home of the range — `DealFeed`'s validator imports them so validator and UI cannot drift. (3) Filtering is `distanceMiles <= max` (inclusive) on the in-memory array, before the expiry filter and sort — zero network on change. (4) `DistanceFilter` is purely presentational (`value` + `onChange`); `DealFeed` owns the state; slider renders only in the data-loaded branch but stays visible when filtering empties the feed.
**Rationale**: Clamping garbage (e.g. 75 → 50) launders bad data into a plausible-looking preference the user never set; falling back to the default is honest and matches the established MPG rule, keeping one validation philosophy across all `gma_` keys. Constants exported from the component keep the contract next to the markup that enforces it.
**Consequences**: Deferred (see `deferred-work.md`): API payload `distanceMiles` is still trusted blindly (`null` coerces to 0 and passes; `NaN`/missing silently drops the dispensary) — belongs at the `useDeals` boundary, Epic 4 hardening candidate; filtered-to-empty shows the generic "No active deals right now" copy (filter-aware copy is Ask-First, needs Erik's call). Per-drag localStorage writes accepted at R&D feed size (no debounce).
**Testing**: 116 client tests passing (+19 this story: matrix sweep incl. garbage ×7 and fractional, inclusive boundary, persisted boundary values 1/50, fetch-spy zero-network proof, singular "mile"). `tsc -b` and lint clean. Three-layer adversarial review, patch-level findings only.

### ADR-026: Stale Source Indicator — Single Strict Predicate, Radius-Independent Count, Fail-Open by Decision
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-06-10
**Context**: Story 2.6 closed Epic 2: dispensaries with `stale: true` (failed scrape) must vanish from the feed, replaced by a non-intrusive "N source(s) unavailable" line — never inflate the feed with stale data. The count's relationship to the 2.5 distance filter and the handling of out-of-contract `stale` values needed decisions.
**Decision**: (1) One strict predicate (`dispensary.stale === true`, ADR-021 boolean precedent) drives BOTH feed omission and the count, so they cannot disagree. (2) The count derives from the full API array, deliberately independent of the distance filter — a stale source 40 miles out still counts at any slider setting (epics AC: "derived from the API response"). (3) Out-of-contract `stale` values (`"true"`, `1`, `null`, missing) fail open to "fresh" — locked by test. Proper handling of malformed payloads belongs at the `useDeals` boundary (deferred, batched with Epic 4 hardening), not in per-consumer equality semantics. (4) `StaleIndicator` is presentational (`count` prop), integer-guarded (`!Number.isInteger(count) || count <= 0` → null, so "NaN sources unavailable" is unrenderable), `role="status"` for screen readers, rendered outside the rows ternary so it coexists with the empty state.
**Rationale**: Splitting omission and count across two checks is how "feed shows 4, indicator says 3" bugs happen. Fail-open keeps the trust decision in one future boundary layer instead of strewing defensive coercions through UI predicates; review confirmed the alternative (treating garbage as stale) silently hides dispensaries on scraper hiccups — worse for a feed whose value is completeness honesty.
**Consequences**: A user with a narrow radius may see "1 source unavailable" for a source outside their radius — accepted overstatement, matches the AC. Null elements inside `dispensaries[]` crash at first property access (pre-existing class, deferred to the `useDeals` boundary item). Indicator never updates mid-session (no refetch by design).
**Testing**: 131 client tests passing (+15 this story: matrix sweep incl. exact AC2 2-of-4 case, radius independence, garbage ×3 + missing property, NaN/negative/zero render-nothing, singular/plural). `tsc -b` and lint clean. Three-layer adversarial review, patch-level findings only.

### ADR-027: EIA Gas Price Refresh — WA Weekly Series, Fail-Safe Contract, fsync'd Atomic Writes
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-06-11
**Context**: Story 3.1 (FR-7) replaces the static `meta.gasPrice` seed with a live EIA feed ≤ 24h stale. The architecture left EIA endpoint specifics open and required `atomicWrite.ts` (introduced here, reused by Epic 4's scrapers).
**Decision**: (1) EIA v2 `petroleum/pri/gnd`, weekly, `duoarea=SWA` (Washington state), product `EPMR` (regular gasoline), latest row only — state-level price beats the national average for Marysville trip math. (2) `refreshGasPrice()` is fail-safe by contract: on ANY failure (missing key → warn+skip, network/non-2xx, malformed body, unusable price) `data.json` stays byte-for-byte unchanged, the reason is logged via `err.message` only (the axios config carries the API key — never serialized), and the promise resolves; a gas-price hiccup can never crash the server. (3) Value validation: `typeof number|string` gate before `Number(...)` coercion (blocks `true` → 1, `[4.2]` → 4.2), then finite > 0. (4) `atomicWriteJson`: write sibling tmp → `fsyncSync` → `renameSync`; orphan tmp removed on rename failure. Documented single-writer-only — Story 4.1 must add writer serialization + unique tmp names before scraper reuse (blocker noted in deferred-work). (5) `index.ts`: fire-and-forget at boot + 24h `setInterval`; no retry/backoff by decision (next daily run is the retry).
**Rationale**: Per-request `readFileSync` in `dataRoute` means a renamed file is the only publication mechanism needed — but without fsync a power loss could publish a truncated `data.json` and 500 every request, so durability is part of "atomic" here. Fail-safe-keep-last-value matches the epic rule that a slightly stale price beats no price.
**Consequences**: Refresh exactly at the 24h boundary ("≤ 24h under normal operation"). A failed boot refresh waits a day (accepted). `npm run build` reverts dist data to seed (deferred, ADR-018 interaction). Price plausibility bounds deferred pending Erik's call. `.env` with a real `EIA_API_KEY` still needs to be created on this machine — until then the server warns and keeps the seed value.
**Testing**: 37 server tests passing (+19 this story: full failure matrix vs temp data file with deep-equal untouched assertions, key-leak probe, exotic-coercion rejections, atomicity contract incl. rename-failure cleanup). Client suite untouched at 131. Both tscs clean, client lint clean. Three-layer adversarial review; auditor found zero violations.

### ADR-028: Vehicle Precision Mode — Controlled Selector, Hook-Only fueleconomy.gov Access, First-Trim Policy
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-06-11
**Context**: Story 3.2 replaces the national-average 28 MPG with the user's actual vehicle MPG via fueleconomy.gov Year→Make→Model dropdowns, persisted in localStorage — the last lever for the 15% gas-cost accuracy bar.
**Decision**: (1) All fueleconomy.gov access lives in `useFuelEconomy.ts` (browser-direct, no proxy per architecture); every request sends `Accept: application/json` (API defaults to XML) and normalizes the single-entry quirk where `menuItem` arrives as a bare object instead of an array. (2) Menus carry no MPG, so resolution takes two extra calls: `/menu/options` (each entry = a trim, `value` = vehicle id) then `/vehicle/{id}` for `comb08` — **trim policy: take the first option** (deterministic, no fourth dropdown). (3) `comb08` validation mirrors ADR-027's gate: `typeof number|string` before `Number(...)`, then `isPositiveFinite` (shared with ADR-024). (4) `useVehicleMpg.ts` is the single home for the `gma_vehicle_mpg`/`gma_vehicle_label` pair over `useLocalStorage`; the read path validates, so consumers only ever see a usable MPG (finite > 0) or null. (5) `VehicleSelector` is fully controlled — persisted state in via props, completed selection out via `onMpgChange(mpg, label)`; it never touches storage, so DealFeed's single `useVehicleMpg` instance re-renders every card in the same pass (no reload, no lifted state). (6) Failure UX split: explicit `role="alert"` error inside the panel only; gas math silently keeps `meta.nationalMpg` — the feed never errors from this feature.
**Rationale**: Hook-only data access and `gma_`-prefixed keys follow the locked architecture rules; the controlled component keeps recalculation a pure React render concern; first-trim keeps the UI at three dropdowns (a trim picker is Ask-First scope).
**Consequences**: First-trim MPG can differ from the user's actual trim (accepted within the 15% bar). Three-layer adversarial review passed all 5 ACs with zero spec violations but left 7 patch action items open in the story file — notably two major async findings (cascade menu fetches and `resolveMpg` have no stale-response invalidation; the slowest network response can persist an abandoned vehicle). Story stays `in-progress` until patched. Deferred to `deferred-work.md`: loading indicator/fetch timeout, mid-cascade retry path, empty-menu messaging.
**Testing**: 174 client tests passing (+43 this story: hook cascade/header/normalization/comb08-exotica matrices, selector cascade enablement + failure paths, DealFeed recalculation + corrupt-storage fallback). 37 server tests unchanged, tsc and lint clean. Committed and pushed at `b34689b`.

### ADR-029: Dutchie Scraper Integration — Thin HTTP Client, Shared Store-Agnostic Transform, Graceful Deferred-ID Degradation
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-06-13
**Context**: Story 4.3 wires the three Dutchie/iFrame dispensaries (`the-joint-everett`, `jet-cannabis-everett`, `kush21-everett-evergreen`) to the existing Python Scraper microservice (ADR-017). Live end-to-end verification was deferred by Erik — no real GraphQL capture exists yet, and the `jet`/`kush21` embed ids are unresolved.
**Decision**: (1) `server/utils/scraperClient.ts` is the single HTTP boundary — `postScrape()` POSTs to `:8000/scrape` and returns `intercepted[]` only on `success:true` + array, else `[]` on every failure (down / non-200 / `success:false` / timeout / malformed); never throws. (2) Because every Dutchie store serves an identical GraphQL shape, the `GetSpecialMenuCards` → `Deal[]` transform lives once in `server/scrapers/_dutchie.ts` (not per-store); store files are thin (embed id + delegate). (3) Classification reuses the Epic 4 rules: time-of-day window → `happy_hour` 24h, else `daily`; `discountPct` number-or-null; `daysValid` full lowercase names / `everyday`. (4) Fixture-as-contract: `__fixtures__/dutchie-specials.json` is synthesized from the documented schema and is the single reconciliation point for the deferred live pass; transform field access is defensive so a live-shape delta degrades to skipped cards / `[]`, not a crash. (5) Graceful deferred-id degradation: `jet`/`kush21` ship with `STORE_ID=''` + a guard returning `[]` (→ stale) until their embed ids are discovered live; `the-joint-everett` ships with the confirmed id `689cd028ea84b6a605458416`.
**Rationale**: One HTTP boundary keeps `runScrapers` tier-agnostic (ADR-017) and store files trivial. A shared transform avoids triplicating GraphQL logic across identical stores. Registering all three now (even the two unresolved) makes the engine's stale path document the gap honestly rather than hiding it behind a missing registry entry.
**Consequences**: Two of three Dutchie stores report `stale` until the live pass resolves their embed ids and reconciles the fixture against a real capture — the discount unit (fraction-vs-percent) and the time-window field path are the named risks (see `deferred-work.md`). No new dependency (reuses `axios`); no `data.json` schema change (embed id hardcoded per store, mirroring `remedy-tulalip`).
**Testing**: 73 server tests passing (+20 this story: transform classification matrix incl. malformed-card skip + day-vocabulary lock; client failure matrix down/non-200/`success:false`/timeout/malformed → `[]`). `tsc --noEmit` clean. Three-layer adversarial review run inline — no intent_gap/bad_spec/patch; 2 defers logged. Live E2E deferred by Erik.

### ADR-030: Dutchie Live-Pass Reconciliation — Real GetSpecialMenuCards Shape, Specials-Op Wait, Text-Parsed Discount/Days
**Status**: Accepted (amends ADR-029)
**Date**: 2026-06-14
**Context**: The Story 4.3 live pass (spec `spec-4-3-live-pass`) ran the integration against the live Python service for the first time. The synthesized fixture and `_dutchie.ts` assumptions (ADR-029 item 4) proved wrong on **every field** — so the transform returned `[]` for all three stores and the integration was non-functional against live data. Erik authorized reworking the frozen transform (the "surface and stop" boundary was renegotiated with his explicit approval) and chose parse-days-if-detected-else-everyday.
**Decision**: (1) **Real shape**: specials live at `data.getSpecialMenuCards.menuCards` (not `specialMenuCards.specials`); each card carries free display text only — `menuDisplayName` + `menuDisplayDescription`, with **no numeric discount field and no structured day/time field**. (2) `discountPct` ← first `(\d+)%` parsed from name+description (whole-number percent confirmed live; no fraction risk). (3) `daysValid` ← weekday names parsed from the display text when present, else `everyday` (kush21 "…- Monday & Friday"); full lowercase, filterActiveDeals vocabulary. (4) `type` is always `daily` — `recurringSchedule` was `null` on every live card; **happy_hour/timed-special support is deferred** until a real timed sample exists (shape unverifiable, not invented). (5) **Wait on the specials op**: `dutchieRequest.wait_for_pattern` changed from `dutchie\.com/graphql` to `GetSpecialMenuCards` — large menus (kush21, 150+ specials) emit it late, and waiting on the first generic GraphQL call ended collection before specials arrived (silent zero deals). (6) Both embed ids resolved as cName slugs on the standard embed URL: `jet`→`thc-connection`, `kush21`→`kush21-everett` (kush21 also runs a Dutchie-Plus custom domain `everettshop.kush21.com`, but the standard URL works, so no custom-domain handling needed). Fixture replaced with a sanitized real capture.
**Rationale**: Defensive field access meant the wrong-shape delta degraded to `[]` (stale) rather than a crash — exactly as designed — but it also masked that the integration produced nothing until reconciled live. Parsing discount/days from text is fragile but is the only signal Dutchie exposes; deferring happy_hour avoids fabricating an unverified schema.
**Consequences**: All three Dutchie stores now flow live deals (the-joint 1, jet 2, kush21 4). Free-text day parsing can mis-key on unusual copy (accepted by Erik); note Dutchie also day-gates some specials server-side (the Mon/Fri card was absent on a Sunday), so the parser may rarely fire in practice. Timed `happy_hour` deals from Dutchie are unsupported until a live sample defines `recurringSchedule`.
**Testing**: Live E2E verified all 5 spec capabilities (service health, both ids resolved, shape reconciled, 3/3 stores fresh with contract-valid deals + logs `ok`, graceful degradation with deals preserved on service-down). `tsc --noEmit` clean; 86 server tests pass (`_dutchie.test.ts` reworked to the real shape — 14 tests).

### ADR-005: Non-Intrusive Ads Only
**Status:** Accepted
**Date:** 2026-06-08
**Context:** Need revenue mechanism. User experience is paramount.
**Decision:** Banner/sidebar ad placements only. No pop-ups, no interstitials, nothing that blocks content. If a design choice makes the page harder to use in exchange for ad placement, the ad loses.
**Rationale:** The product's value is instant, unobstructed deal information. Any ad that competes with that destroys the reason users came.
**Consequences:** Revenue ceiling is lower than aggressive ad products. Acceptable — revenue goals are intentionally modest.
**Testing:** N/A at R&D stage.

### ADR-031: Single-Service Deploy — Express Serves the Built Client, Render Blueprint
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-06-14
**Context**: Securing `gmaslist.com` (Porkbun) required a public host to point at — the app was local-only. The repo is two packages (Vite/React `client` + Express `server`); a static host alone can't run the Express scraper API, and a split client/API deploy needs a subdomain and CORS. R&D wants the simplest path to one public origin.
**Decision**: (1) **One Node web service serves both.** In `NODE_ENV=production`, `server/index.ts` mounts `express.static(client/dist)` plus an SPA fallback so the same origin serves the React app and `/api/*`. Dev behavior (Vite proxy, localhost CORS) is unchanged. (2) **Path resolved from the module URL** (`fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)` → `../../../client/dist`) so it's independent of CWD — compiled entry sits at `server/dist/server/index.js`. (3) **SPA fallback uses a RegExp** `^(?!\/api).*`, not the bare `'*'` string — Express 5 (path-to-regexp v8) rejects `'*'`, and the negative-lookahead keeps unmatched `/api` paths returning a real 404 instead of HTML. (4) **`render.yaml` Blueprint**, free plan: build forces `--include=dev` (else `NODE_ENV=production` skips vite/tsc and the build fails), then builds client+server; `npm start` runs the compiled server. `PORT` already honored; Node pinned to 22.
**Rationale**: Single service = one origin, one TLS cert, one deploy, no CORS/subdomain — least to maintain for a solo MVP. Render free tier is fine for R&D (sleeps after idle; upgrade to keep warm before public launch).
**Consequences**: `gmaslist.com` can map directly to one service. **The scrapers POST to a separate Python service not in this repo — so live scraping fails on Render** (`/api/data` still serves last-known/bundled data; failures don't crash the server). The hardcoded `localhost:8000` has since been env-parametrized (`SCRAPER_URL`, ADR-033); live freshness now only requires deploying that scraper service and leaving free tier — **deferred pending budget (ADR-033)**. Free tier cold-start (~30s) makes the first hit after idle look slow.
**Testing**: Local production-mode boot verified all three routes: `GET /`→200 HTML, `GET /api/data`→200 JSON, `GET /<spa-route>`→200 HTML fallback. Client + server builds clean.

### ADR-032: Render Deploy Reality — Manual Service, Build-Logic-in-package.json, gmaslist.com Custom Domain
**Status**: Accepted (operational record; amends ADR-031 deploy mechanics)
**Date**: 2026-06-15
**Context**: ADR-031 assumed a `render.yaml` Blueprint would configure the service. In practice Erik created the Render web service **manually** in the dashboard (service `Gma` / `srv-d8ni2ikm0tmc73e5uhcg`, region oregon, free plan), so it runs its **own** build/start commands (`npm install; npm run build` / `npm run start`) and **ignores `render.yaml` entirely**. The first deploy of `14eae8d` `build_failed` (TS2688 — `npm install` at root never installs the `client`/`server` devDeps, so vite/tsc are missing). The Render MCP exposes no tool to edit a service's build command.
**Decision**: (1) **Move the build orchestration into the repo's root `build` script** (`a7fb357`): root `npm run build` now runs `npm install --prefix client --include=dev && npm install --prefix server --include=dev && npm run build --prefix client && npm run build --prefix server`. This makes the manual service's fixed `npm install; npm run build` succeed without a dashboard edit. **Do not revert this** — it is load-bearing for the live deploy; `render.yaml` is now effectively documentation until/unless the service is recreated as a Blueprint. (2) `NODE_ENV=production` + `NODE_VERSION=22` set on the service **via the Render API** (so Express serves `client/dist`). (3) **Custom domains** `gmaslist.com` (apex) + `www.gmaslist.com` (subdomain, redirects to apex) added to the service via the Render REST API. (4) **Porkbun DNS**: URL Forwarding fully removed (it routes through Cloudflare proxy and hijacks the zone — wrong tool); apex `A`→`216.24.57.1` (Render anycast) and `www` `CNAME`→`gma-zr94.onrender.com` set as real records.
**Rationale**: Editing package.json is the only lever available given a manually-created service + an MCP that can't change build commands. Recreating as a Blueprint would honor `render.yaml` but throws away the working service mid-launch; deferred. DNS records (not URL forwarding) keep `gmaslist.com` in the address bar.
**Consequences**: Live deploy works from `a7fb357` (`/`→200 HTML, `/api/data`→200 JSON). **Reverting the root `build` script breaks the build** (regression caught 2026-06-15 — stale working-tree revert discarded). `render.yaml` and `package.json build` now disagree by design. **BLOCKER (see Known Issues): a stale `173.245.58.37` forwarding record is stuck in Porkbun's Cloudflare-backed zone** — apex resolves to *both* it and `216.24.57.1`, so Render custom-domain verification stays `unverified` and no managed TLS cert issues. `https://gmaslist.com` returns 200 intermittently (whichever IP a request lands on) but is not cleanly live until the ghost record is purged.
**Testing**: `list_deploys` shows `dep-d8o2e10g4nts73c7mc2g` (commit `a7fb357`) `live`. 30-min authoritative-NS poll (`curitiba.ns.porkbun.com`): apex consistently returns both `173.245.58.37` + `216.24.57.1`; `www`→`gma-zr94.onrender.com` clean; both Render domains `unverified` throughout.

### ADR-033: Live-Scraper Deploy Topology — Scraper as a Render Private Docker Service (Recommended, Deferred)
**Status**: Accepted (deferred — not yet deployed)
**Date**: 2026-06-15
**Context**: Live scraping fails on Render today because the three Dutchie scrapers POST to a hardcoded `http://localhost:8000/scrape` — the Python Playwright microservice (`C:\Users\erikc\Dev\Scraper`, ADR-017) that is **not deployed**. By contract (`scraperClient.ts`, ADR-029), the failure is swallowed and returns `[]`, so `runScrapers` marks those dispensaries `stale` and `/api/data` serves bundled/last-known deals. Two deeper findings shape the topology: (1) Happy runs on Render's **free web service, which spins down after ~15 min idle** — and the scrape model is an in-process `setInterval` (`server/index.ts:53-56`, ADR-010) that fires on boot then hourly, so on free tier it doesn't reliably run at all; (2) Playwright+Chromium wants ~2GB shared memory (`../Scraper/docker-compose.yml` → `shm_size: "2gb"`) vs. free tier's 512MB. **Truly-free live scraping with Playwright is not viable on Render.** Erik's decision (2026-06-15): stay free / defer live; record the recommended topology for when a hosting budget is approved.
**Decision**: (1) **Recommended target topology — keep the in-process scrape model; deploy `../Scraper` as a Render *private* Docker service; run both services always-on (paid Starter, ~$14/mo total).** The only code change is the `SCRAPER_URL` env var (done in this story — see AC-1); zero refactor of the proven `runScrapers` → `normalizeDeals` → `atomicWriteJson(data.json)` pipeline or the per-scraper GraphQL→`Deal[]` transforms (ADR-030). Happy leaves free tier so the hourly `setInterval` fires reliably and Chromium gets RAM/shm headroom. (2) **Security:** a Render **private service** keeps `/scrape` off the public internet — reachable only via Render's internal hostname (`http://<service-name>:8000/scrape`) from the Happy service; a public web service would expose the scrape endpoint. (3) **Deferral:** no Render services/cron/db/disk are created now; this is recorded for go-live. The step-by-step is in `docs/deploy-scraper-runbook.md`.
**Rationale**: Lowest-risk path to live data — reuses the entire verified live pipeline (ADR-030, 86 server tests) and changes one line of wiring. The private-service boundary is the natural fit for an internal scrape endpoint. Always-on (not cron) keeps the model identical to local dev, so behavior is predictable.
**Consequences**: ~$14/mo when activated (two always-on Starter instances). **Ephemeral disk:** Render's filesystem is not persistent — `data.json` resets to the committed seed on every redeploy, but **self-heals**: the boot-time `runScrapers()` (`server/index.ts:53`) repopulates it on the next cycle, so no persistence layer is required for the recommended topology. **Cost-optimization alternative (~$7/mo, deferred, bigger refactor):** a Render **Cron Job** runs scraping on a schedule and writes to a shared store (Postgres/persistent disk); Happy stays cheap and reads the cache. This decouples scrape latency from requests and survives spin-down, but requires moving scraping out of the Node process, adding a persistence layer, and dropping `setInterval` — noted, not built. Cross-links: amends the deploy reality of **ADR-031/032** (single manual web service) by adding a second service at go-live; the **Render internal-URL form** (`http://<service-name>:<port>`) is hypothesized from Render conventions and **must be verified against current Render docs at go-live**.
**Testing**: N/A — no deployment performed. The code half (env-parametrized `SCRAPER_URL`, default-preserving) is covered by `scraperClient.test.ts` (8 tests, incl. default-when-unset and custom-URL); behavior on current free Render is unchanged (bundled-data fallback). Go-live verification steps (scraper `/health` → `{"status":"ok"}`, then `/api/data` Dutchie dispensaries flip `stale:false`) are specified in the runbook.

### ADR-034: Live-Scraper Execution — Playwright on GitHub Actions Cron → Authenticated Ingest (Chosen)
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-18) — **chosen go-live path; supersedes ADR-033** as the topology to build. ADR-033 (paid Render private Docker, ~$14/mo) and the parked home-hardware self-host idea (`_bmad-output/brainstorming/scraper-selfhost-build-plan-PARKED.md`) are both retained as record but **not the path**.
**Context**: Same root problem as ADR-033 — 3 of 4 Dutchie sources show `stale` on live `gmaslist.com` because the Python Playwright scraper POSTs to `localhost:8000`, which isn't hosted; `scraperClient.ts` swallows the failure. A roundtable raised a tempting cheaper primitive: **drop the browser entirely and hit Dutchie's consumer GraphQL endpoint with a plain HTTP POST** (no Chromium, runnable on any free cron). A 30-min de-risking spike (2026-06-18) **falsified** that: Dutchie sits behind **Cloudflare bot management** that blocks on **TLS/JA3 fingerprint**, not just cookies. From Erik's own residential IP, a naive `curl` POST to `https://dutchie.com/graphql` → **403 "Attention Required"**; the same `curl` carrying a live `__cf_bm` cookie + `x-dutchie-session` + full Chrome headers → **still 403** (only the TLS fingerprint differs from the real browser). The Scraper repo's `HANDOFF.md` independently records that the `curl_cffi` TLS-impersonation tier "won't work for Dutchie" — which is *why* the heavyweight browser-interceptor tier exists. **Conclusion: a real browser is provably required to read Dutchie; "GraphQL-direct without a browser" is dead.** What survived the spike was the *other* unlock: the scrape need not run on Render or on home hardware — it can run on a free CI runner that pushes results in.
**Decision**: **Run the existing Playwright scraper on a scheduled GitHub Actions workflow (cron), which POSTs assembled results to a new authenticated ingest endpoint on the Render app; Happy stays on the free plan and serves last-known-good.** Concretely: (1) **Runner** — GitHub Actions free Linux runner (~7GB RAM, runs headless Chromium fine — satisfies the proven browser requirement at $0, with no Render Docker service and no home box). (2) **Scheduler** — Actions `schedule:` cron owns the timing, so Happy's free-tier spin-down no longer matters (it stops driving scraping). (3) **Store registry** — stores live as config (slug + Dutchie retailer id) in-repo; adding a store is a one-line diff, fan out across stores in a matrix so one store's failure is isolated. (4) **Ingest contract** — new `POST /api/ingest` on the Express app: shared-secret header, normalized per-store payload, server validates shape and writes **last-known-good** with a per-store timestamp; partial success allowed; **never overwrite good data with empty/invalid**. (5) **Stop in-process scraping** — retire the boot/`setInterval` `runScrapers()` on Render (ADR-010); Render becomes read-only over `data.json`/store. (6) **Observability fix (root cause)** — replace `scraperClient.ts`'s silent error-swallow with explicit per-store status (`ok|stale|failed`) surfaced in `/api/data`, plus an Actions job-failure alert.
**Rationale**: The spike proved the browser is non-negotiable, so the question collapses to *where the browser runs cheapest and most reliably*. GitHub Actions wins on every axis the roundtable cared about: **$0** (vs ADR-033's ~$14/mo), **no hardware to babysit** (vs the parked home box, flagged weakest by all four agents), store-registry + parser **fixtures/tests run in CI**, and the scraper is **fully decoupled** from the live site (a scrape crash can't take the page down). It keeps the proven `runScrapers` → `normalizeDeals` → `Deal[]` pipeline (ADR-029/030) — only its *trigger* and *destination* move.
**Consequences**: Inverts the data flow from pull (Render calls scraper) to **push** (runner calls Render) — new ingest endpoint + auth + last-known-good store become load-bearing contracts. Refresh cadence is bounded by the cron interval (minutes-stale is fine for this product; confirmed acceptable since deals don't change sub-hourly). **Scaling unit is *platforms*, not stores** (Mary): the design assumes a per-source **adapter** normalizing into one internal deal schema, so a future Leafly/Weedmaps/Jane store is a new adapter, not an app rewrite. **Known ceiling:** outgrow Actions if frequency goes sub-15-min or Cloudflare escalates to rotating-fingerprint/proxy defenses → graduate to a managed scraping API or a small always-on worker (not now). **Strategic note:** scraping rides undocumented endpoints under ToS exposure — fine to bootstrap; as store count grows, convert to sanctioned feeds/partnerships (Dutchie partner program, Weedmaps/Leafly APIs, or owner-supplied feeds — owners *want* the traffic). Implementation (ingest endpoint + auth + retiring `setInterval`) should run through the normal BMad story flow; the Actions workflow + secret are new infra.
**Testing**: De-risking spike (2026-06-18) — empirical results below; verified against live Cloudflare from Erik's residential IP.

| Test | Result | Reads as |
|---|---|---|
| Naive `curl` POST → `dutchie.com/graphql` | **403** CF "Attention Required" | non-browser blocked |
| `curl` + live `__cf_bm` + `x-dutchie-session` + Chrome headers | **403** | block is at TLS/JA3, not cookies |
| Repo `HANDOFF.md` on `curl_cffi` TLS-spoof tier | "won't work for Dutchie" | browser required |

Build-time verification (when implemented): fixture snapshot-test per store *shape* (Dutchie schema change fails CI, not prod); `/api/ingest` contract tests (rejects bad secret + malformed body, preserves prior on reject); smoke check that `/api/data` has no source older than N hours.

### ADR-035: Compliance Launch Gate — Description Sanitization, Standing Disclaimers, Age-Gate Decline
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-06-18
**Context**: The UX regulated-content review (`review-regulated-content.md`) logged three "verify with counsel" items the spec left open before public launch: (1) whether republishing scraped retailer ad copy makes the aggregator an "advertiser" under **WAC 314-55-155**; (2) which (if any) WA mandatory-warning statements apply to a non-commerce aggregator; (3) the defensibility of the age gate's no-decline posture (ADR-021). These are genuine legal questions for WA counsel, but each has an engineering posture that is most-defensible regardless of the legal answer and should ship now.
**Decision**: Two tracks. **Counsel track** (Erik + attorney) answers the legal questions and is not code. **Engineering track** (this story) ships the defensible, parameterized posture: (1) **Description sanitization at the single ingest chokepoint** — `sanitizeDescription` runs inside `normalizeDeals` (← `applyIngest` ← `POST /api/ingest`; ADR-034 retired the in-process scraper, so this is the only inbound path). Every `deal.description` is stripped of markup/emoji/control-chars, whitespace-collapsed, and length-capped (80, word-boundary). A whole-word, operator-tunable `DESCRIPTION_BLOCKLIST` of therapeutic-claim and youth-appeal terms **suppresses** the offending copy (blanks the field → DealCard renders badge+discount+window only) but **never drops the deal** (so suppression can't falsely trip the last-known-good stale contract). Whole-word matching avoids false-flagging legit cannabis vocab ("cured resin", "no kidding"); 'gummy' is intentionally not blocked. Descriptions are never rewritten/embellished. (2) **Standing disclaimer footer** (`DisclaimerFooter`, single source of truth, `Notice variant="muted"`, below the feed) always shows the accuracy/affiliation line and the gas-estimate qualifier; the exact WA mandatory-warning text is **staged behind `WA_WARNING_ENABLED` (default `false`)** and surfaces on a single flag flip pending counsel. (3) **Age-gate decline affordance** — secondary ghost "I am under 21" → terminal dead-end screen; session-only, never persists, affirm path unchanged (amends ADR-021).
**Rationale**: The actual regulatory label ("advertiser"? "must warn"?) is counsel's to assign and may stay unsettled, so the code must be correct under either answer — most-defensible by default, trivially upgraded (flip a flag, tune a list) if counsel says "must". The ingest chokepoint is the only correct sanitization site post-ADR-034. Suppress-not-drop preserves the last-known-good invariant. The decline affordance strictly strengthens good-faith posture at near-zero cost.
**Consequences**: Code must not assert a legal conclusion — counsel-decision points are marked `// COUNSEL:` with a reserved, flag-gated slot. The blocklist is a tuning surface Erik owns (false-negatives/positives are expected and adjusted by editing the constant, not logic). WA warning stays dark until counsel enables it. Folds in the review's gas-estimate-qualifier finding (#4, liability framing) at no extra cost.
**Testing**: `sanitizeDescription` (12) + `normalizeDeals` wiring (suppress-keeps-deal, strip-markup) + `DealCard` empty-description guard + `DisclaimerFooter` (4, incl. flag on/off exact-text) + `AgeGate` decline (2). Full suite green: 281 client + 124 server; `tsc` + lint clean.

---

### ADR-036: Age Gate — Two-Option Gate with Returnable Decline + "Remember me" (delta on ADR-035; supersedes ADR-021)
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-19) — **builds on ADR-035** (compliance launch gate, which added a decline affordance) and **supersedes ADR-021** (single-button, no-decline gate).
**Context**: Master's ADR-035 already added an age-gate decline path, but as a **terminal dead-end**, session-only, with no "Remember me" — confirm always persists. The `GMAS_LIST_BRIEF.md` visual-overhaul plan (§5a) specifies a two-option 21+ gate with explicit ask → in / out states, a "Remember me on this device" checkbox, and the dark Tidewater visual. These two gate designs collided when `feat/tidewater-reskin` rebased onto master. Erik ruled (2026-06-19) on the two open product questions: **decline should be returnable** (a "Go back" out-state, not a dead-end) and **keep the "Remember me" checkbox**.
**Decision**: Merge the two. The gate offers **confirm and decline**, retaining ADR-035's decline affordance but as a **returnable "out" state** ("Come back at 21" → "Go back" returns to ask) rather than a terminal dead-end. Confirm with "Remember me" checked (default) persists `gma_age_confirmed=true` to `localStorage` (unchanged strict `=== true` predicate); confirm with it **unchecked** grants a **session-only** pass held in component state. Decline is **session-only, never persisted** (ADR-035's shared-device rationale carries forward — a reload always re-gates). Dialog a11y is retained and strengthened: `role="alertdialog"`, `aria-modal`, labelled heading, focus moved into the gate on mount/return, plus a real **Tab focus-trap** (`handleKeyDown`, no Escape by design). WA WAC 314-55-155 mandated warnings render as small print below the card, sourced from a single constant (`client/src/constants/legal.ts`).
**Rationale**: Returnable decline keeps ADR-035's good-faith decline posture while forgiving a misclick at zero compliance cost (decline is still session-only and grants no content access). "Remember me" makes persistence a user choice rather than implicit — friendlier to shared/kiosk devices; the unchecked path is the more privacy-protective default for a shared browser. Session-only confirm reuses the same strict storage check, so corrupted values still fail closed.
**Consequences**: ADR-035's terminal-dead-end decline is replaced by a returnable one; the `Icon`-based decline screen is replaced by the Tidewater card. `AgeGate` gained component state (session/declined/remember) and is no longer purely storage-driven. EXPERIENCE.md's Age-gate rows are superseded by this ADR. Compliance work from ADR-035 (sanitization, DisclaimerFooter) is untouched.
**Testing**: `AgeGate.test.tsx` covers persist-on-remember, session-only-when-unchecked, decline→out→return, verbatim warnings, strict non-boolean rejection. `App.test.tsx` gate assertions updated. Full client suite re-run post-rebase (see Change Log).

### ADR-037: Front-End Visual Overhaul — "Tidewater" Token-Layer Reskin In Place (not a rebuild); True-Cost Deferred
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-19)
**Context**: `GMAS_LIST_BRIEF.md` defines a complete new visual identity — dark "Tidewater" base, single teal accent (`#4FD1C5`), Space Grotesk / Plus Jakarta Sans / Space Mono, lowercase `gmas list` wordmark + teal set-dot — replacing the shipped Epic-6 light/green "honest utility" look. Erik asked to "replace the boilerplate front end with this direction." A double-check found two things: (1) `client/src` is **not** boilerplate — it is the shipped, fully-tested app (~30 components + 6 hooks, 274 passing tests); (2) the brief's headline feature, a **true-cost total (`sticker + drive`, ranked)**, requires per-deal **sticker/original price** data that the pipeline does not have — the `Deal` type carries only `discountPct`. Building it would need new scraping across all 4 sources and reverses ADR-009 (Honest Math: never sum into one figure).
**Decision**: **(1) Reskin in place, not rebuild** — the design system is token-driven, so the dark identity ships almost entirely as a `tokens.css` value swap (semantic token names preserved) plus a `components.css` pass and four small JSX edits (wordmark, two `--green-700`→`--accent` leaks, dark scrim). All tested behavior and structure are retained. (2) **Defer true-cost** — keep the existing "discount % + drive cost" Discount Display (ADR-009 intact), reskinned dark. The true-cost-total model is logged as a separate future data+product epic (scraper price extraction + ADR-009 revisit), **not** built now and **not** faked with seed prices. (3) Fonts load via Google Fonts `<link>` (brief §3); self-hosting is a follow-up.
**Rationale**: Reskin-in-place is the lowest-risk path that satisfies "don't break anything" — it delivers the full visual overhaul while keeping the green test baseline as a safety net. Deferring true-cost avoids shipping a card that lies about real cost until real price data exists.
**Consequences**: DESIGN.md rewritten to Tidewater (`status: draft` — dark-palette **WCAG AA contrast is unverified** and must be checked before `final`; the prior light-mode contrast pairs do not carry over). EXPERIENCE.md true-cost/honest-math rows stand (discount model retained); age-gate rows superseded by ADR-036. The deal card's visual spec in DESIGN.md documents the true-cost layout for when data lands, marked pending. Branch `feat/tidewater-reskin`.
**Testing**: 277/277 client tests pass; `tsc -b` clean; `vite build` succeeds; `eslint` clean. Built CSS verified to contain the Tidewater palette + all three font families and **zero** occurrences of the old green action color `#15803d`. Visual/contrast verification in a browser still pending.

### ADR-038: Deal Feed Grouped by Store (one card per store); Default Radius 50 mi
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-20)
**Context**: The feed rendered one `DealCard` per deal, so a store with multiple active deals appeared as several stacked cards each repeating the store name, distance, and gas-cost figure (visible in production once the Dutchie sources went live — e.g. Kush21 Everett had 4 separate cards). Erik asked to consolidate to one card per store. Separately, the distance slider defaulted to 25 mi, narrower than the project's 50-road-mile coverage zone.
**Decision**: **(1) Group deals by store.** Added `groupDealsByStore(dispensaries, now): StoreGroup[]` in `client/src/utils/sortDeals.ts` that runs the existing `sortDeals` (untouched) and groups its globally-sorted rows by `dispensary.id` into an insertion-ordered `Map`. Result: stores order by their best (highest-priority) deal, and each store's deals retain `sortDeals` order — no duplicated tier logic. `DealCard` reshaped from a single `deal` to props `{ dispensary, deals: DealView[], gasCostText }` (`DealView = { deal, windowText, countdown }`): store **name + distance + one "$X to get there" gas line render once in the header** (they describe the trip, not the deal), with per-deal badge/discount/window/countdown listed below. Deals render as plain `<div>`s, **not** `<li>`s, preserving the invariant of one `listitem` per store. Card stays `urgent` when any of its deals is a happy hour. **(2)** `DEFAULT_DISTANCE_MILES` 25 → 50 in `DistanceFilter.tsx` — only affects users with no saved `gma_distance_miles`; the persisted key and 1–50 validation range are unchanged.
**Rationale**: Grouping by `dispensary.id` via a Map is the minimal change that reuses all existing sort/filter/expiry logic. Moving gas/distance to the card header is what removes the repetition (they are per-trip, not per-deal). Plain `<div>` deal blocks keep accessibility counts (one store = one list item) intact. Keeping `sortDeals` and the `Deal`/`Dispensary` types untouched means zero API/data-shape risk.
**Consequences**: New view-layer types `StoreGroup` (util) and `DealView` (card); no API/types change. Assumes `dispensary.id` is unique per store (the data contract) — two distinct stores sharing an id would merge into one card under the first; documented in code, not guarded (grouping by id is the desired behavior, and keying by id+name would re-split a legitimately-same store). Existing users keep their saved radius; only the no-preference default widened to the full zone.
**Testing**: Built via bmad-quick-dev + 3-layer adversarial review (acceptance auditor PASS, no rework; 3 patches: comment drift 25→50, documented id-uniqueness assumption, re-added em-dash regression guard). 283 client tests pass; `tsc -b` clean; `eslint` clean; `vite build` succeeds. Merged via PR #7 (`45312bd`) → master; deploy-hook auto-deployed; Erik verified live on gmaslist.com (Kush21 Everett shows 4 deals in one card, slider at 50 mi). Spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-group-deals-by-store.md`.

---

### ADR-039: Order Store Cards by Distance (closest first)
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-20)
**Context**: After ADR-038 grouped the feed into one card per store, the store cards were ordered by each store's *best deal* (the insertion order of `groupDealsByStore`'s Map). Erik asked for the card list to be ordered by how close each store is — proximity is the more useful scan order when deciding where to drive.
**Decision**: In `groupDealsByStore` (`client/src/utils/sortDeals.ts`), apply a final stable sort of the grouped `StoreGroup[]` by `dispensary.distanceMiles` ascending. Because `Array.prototype.sort` is stable and the groups already arrive in best-deal order (from the unchanged `sortDeals` + insertion-ordered Map), equidistant stores retain their best-deal relative order as a free tie-break — no composite sort key. Deal order *within* each store, and the whole filter pipeline (stale → distance → expiry → chip → group), are untouched. **Supersedes only the store-ordering rule of ADR-038**; ADR-038's deal-within-store order, one-card-per-store structure, and 50 mi default all stand.
**Rationale**: A single trailing stable sort is the minimal change and reuses every existing tier/group/filter path. Leaning on sort stability for the tie-break avoids re-deriving a secondary key and keeps the previous behavior intact for ties. `distanceMiles` is already trusted as a finite number by `DealFeed`'s distance filter, so no extra guarding was added (consistency over defensive duplication).
**Consequences**: Store card order is now proximity-driven, not deal-quality-driven — a closer store with a weaker deal sits above a farther store with a better one (the intended behavior). No API, types, network, or storage change. The ADR-038 comment/test language "stores by best deal" now describes only the equidistant tie-break.
**Testing**: Built via bmad-quick-dev. Added `sortDeals.test.ts` cases (distance-ascending order beats deal priority; equidistant tie-break preserves best-deal order; `makeDispensary` gained an optional distance arg) and a `DealFeed.test.tsx` distance-ordering test (existing best-deal test retitled as the equidistant case). [Verification results appended below once the suite is run.] Spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-order-store-cards-by-distance.md`.

---

### ADR-040: "Synthwave" Theme + Twin-Accent Deal Card
**Status**: Superseded by ADR-041 (palette only; the reskin-in-place mechanism and card layout are retained)
**Context**: Erik asked to re-skin the app to a Synthwave palette and supplied an exact spec (deep-indigo surfaces, neon cyan + pink accents, per-element font sizes, and a richer card layout). The shipped Tidewater system (ADR-036/037) is a single-teal accent with one combined "trip chip" (distance + gas) and a `30%`-only discount figure. The Synthwave spec implies card elements that didn't exist: a split distance pill vs. gas line, an "off" label, a pulse dot, and a rendered countdown (the countdown was computed in `DealFeed` but never displayed).
**Decision**: Two coordinated layers. **(1) Theme** — a whole-app value swap in `client/src/styles/tokens.css` (`:root` palette + the Tailwind `@theme` mirror): `--bg #0a0a1f`, `--surface #15122e`, `--surface-raised #1d1840`, text `#efeaff`/`#9b94c8`/`#6b6499`, divider `#2a2350`. Token *names* are preserved so every component re-skins for free. The accent role splits into two meaningful hues: `--accent` = cyan `#2de2e6` (value/go: discount, distance, primary actions, slider, chips, set-dot) and `--warning` = pink `#ff2e88` **remapped to carry urgency** (happy hour). New low-alpha fill tokens added: `--card-border` (cyan .2 card hairline), `--fill-distance`/`-border` (cyan .12/.4 pill), `--fill-happy`/`-border` (pink .13/.36 badge), `--fill-daily` (white .05). **(2) Card** — `DealCard.tsx` + the `components.css` `.gma-dealcard*` block split the combined trip chip into a cyan **distance pill** (header) + cyan **gas line** (with fuel icon, omitted when gas is null), added the muted **"off"** label beside the cyan discount figure, and replaced the generic urgency `Badge` with a pink **happy-hour badge + animated pulse dot + pink countdown** (driven by the unchanged `storeUrgencyBadge`), or a neutral daily badge otherwise. Card-specific px sizes from the spec (store name 26, discount 22, off 13, gas 12.5, pill/window 12, countdown 11.5, badge 10.5) live on the card classes, not the global type scale.
**Rationale**: Preserving token names keeps the re-skin a near-pure value swap, the same discipline that made the Tidewater swap cheap. Mapping pink onto the existing `--warning`/urgency role (rather than inventing a parallel system) means urgency stays single-sourced — card border, badge, and countdown can't disagree (ADR-009/037 invariant holds). Magnitude stays a single-hue cue, but **the ADR-037 opacity ramp is dropped** (see amendment): the Synthwave spec shows every discount at uniformly bright cyan, and on the indigo surface the faded low/mid tiers read as muddy purple, not "smaller discount." A subtle font-weight step is the only retained magnitude cue. The pulse dot honors `prefers-reduced-motion`.
**Amendment (2026-06-20)**: After first visual review, the per-tier opacity (`high 1 / mid .85 / low .7` on `.gma-deal-block__pct`) was removed — all tiers now full opacity. Erik's call: the dimmed 10–20% figures looked purple/wrong against `--bg`, not like a magnitude signal. ADR-037's "magnitude via one hue, never a second color" invariant still holds (the weight step remains).
**Consequences**: Whole app is now Synthwave; two visual accents now carry distinct meaning (cyan=value, pink=urgency) — a deliberate change from Tidewater's single accent. `--success` now reads cyan and `--danger` shifted to `#ff5470` to fit the palette (both rarely surfaced). The combined "12.4 mi · $3.63" trip string no longer exists; distance and gas are separate nodes — tests across `DealCard`/`DealFeed`/`App` were updated to assert the two figures (and the pink `.gma-countdown`) independently.
