No API, types, data, or storage change. **Supersedes the palette of ADR-036 and the single-accent rule of ADR-037**; ADR-038's one-card-per-store structure and ADR-039's distance ordering are untouched.
**Testing**: Full client suite green — 312 tests across 31 files (`DealCard`, `DealFeed`, `App` assertions rewritten for the split figures + countdown selector). `eslint .` clean; `tsc -b && vite build` succeeds. Visual verification on the running app still pending.

---

### ADR-041: "Arcade" High-Contrast Palette Swap (pink brand / amber discount / red urgency)
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-20)
**Context**: Erik asked to replace the live Synthwave identity (ADR-040) with a higher-contrast "Arcade" palette. The triggering brief was drafted against a stale pre-Synthwave ("Tidewater") snapshot — its premises (teal `#4FD1C5`/`--green-700` literals to replace, "ADR-039", "Maybe/Meh" verdict badges, a single-accent base, a "<40%" discount split) did not match the shipped code: the palette is fully tokenized (no color literals in JSX), is twin-accent (cyan value + pink urgency), has no verdict badges (ADR-009), and uses `high≥30 / mid≥15 / low` tiers. The work was re-baselined onto Synthwave reality before implementing.
**Decision**: A token-layer value swap in `client/src/styles/tokens.css` (`:root` + the Tailwind `@theme` mirror), preserving every semantic token NAME so the component layer re-skins for free — the same mechanism as ADR-037/040. Arcade is a deliberate **three-color** system, mapped onto the existing two-accent token set plus **one new token**: `--accent` = pink `#ff3d8b` (the single brand/interactive accent: buttons, active chip, focus ring, links, wordmark set-dot, distance pill, gas line, fresh status); **`--discount` = amber `#ffc83d`** — the one new token, discount-semantic ONLY (the `.gma-deal-block__pct` figure, the `--discount` badge) plus the sanctioned card-top motif line; `--warning` = red `#ff5470` (value change only — every urgency consumer keeps aliasing `--warning`, so the happy-hour badge, pulse dot, and countdown turn red without touching component code). Surfaces go near-black: `--bg #050507`, `--surface #121016`, `--surface-raised #1c1922`, `--tw-border #2a2330`; text `#f5f2f7`/`#a8a0b2`/`#6e6676`; `--card-border` and the distance-pill fills move to pink alpha, the happy fills to red alpha; `--danger` shifts to `#ff3b5c` to stay distinct from urgency. A CSS-only decorative motif (`article.gma-card::before`, the deal card being the sole `article.gma-card`) draws a 2px pink hairline over a 2px amber hairline at the card top — no DOM/className change.
**Rationale**: Preserving token names keeps the swap near-mechanical. A new `--discount` token is the honest home for amber: once `--accent` becomes the pink brand color, discount (formerly cyan via `--accent`) has nowhere semantic to live, and inlining a literal or overloading `--success`/`--danger` would break the "components reference semantic tokens, never raw palette" rule. Pink can't double as urgency now that it is the brand accent, so urgency takes a distinct red (Erik's call) to keep three signals legible: pink=interactive, amber=discount, red=urgency. Magnitude remains a single hue (amber) with a font-weight step only — ADR-037's "magnitude via one hue, never a second color" invariant, and ADR-040's dropped opacity ramp, both hold. The card-top motif is the only sanctioned non-discount use of amber, and decorative only.
**Consequences**: One new token (`--discount`) is added — a deliberate, documented exception to the "value-swap only / no new tokens" framing, required by the three-color palette. **WCAG AA is now UNVERIFIED**: ADR-040 inherited Tidewater's AA audit, but Arcade changes every load-bearing pair (amber-on-near-black, pink-on-near-black, red-on-near-black) — DESIGN.md is reverted to `status: draft` with the contrast section flagged "AA contrast UNVERIFIED — re-audit before final"; no prose may imply AA still holds until re-audited. `--success` now reads pink and `--danger` shifted to `#ff3b5c` (both rarely surfaced). No API, types, data, storage, routing, or component DOM-structure change; the deal card's classes and structure are untouched. **Supersedes the palette of ADR-040** (its reskin-in-place mechanism and card layout are retained); ADR-038's one-card-per-store and ADR-039's distance ordering are untouched. Note on numbering: the triggering brief said "ADR-039" and "supersede ADR-037" — both already taken/done; this is ADR-041 superseding ADR-040.
**Testing**: New `client/src/styles/tokens.test.ts` asserts the Arcade values are present (`--accent`/`--discount`/`--warning`/surfaces, plus the `@theme` mirror) and that no superseded hue survives (Synthwave `#2de2e6`/`#ff2e88`, Tidewater `#4FD1C5`/`--green-700`). `DealCard.test.tsx` color-naming comments updated; its class-based assertions are palette-agnostic and stay green. Full client suite + `tsc -b` + `eslint .` + `vite build` all pass. Visual verification on the running app pending.

---

### ADR-042: "Daylight" Light Theme (warm paper / emerald brand / rationed semantic color)
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-20)
**Context**: Erik asked to lighten the app — the live/in-progress dark "Arcade" identity (neon-on-near-black, ADR-041) reads as nightclub/arcade, a tonal mismatch for a calm daytime *find-a-deal-near-me* utility. He explicitly authorized the palette change, which is the gate the prior ADRs' "no palette change without a documented decision" discipline waits for. Direction chosen together: the **bigger move** — pivot the brand off pink to green — on a **warm-paper** canvas, with semantic color used **sparingly** (a touch, not a flood).
**Decision**: A token-layer value swap in `client/src/styles/tokens.css` (`:root` + the Tailwind `@theme` mirror), preserving every semantic token NAME so the component layer re-skins for free — the same mechanism as ADR-037/040/041; the ADR-040/041 card layout + CSS-only card-top motif are retained (the motif now draws emerald-over-amber, both via tokens, no DOM change). Light is now the primary mode. Surfaces invert to warm paper: `--bg #f7f6f3` (canvas), `--surface #ffffff` (cards, lifted by `--shadow-sm` + a `#e4e1d9` hairline — the neon `--card-border` retires to a quiet hairline), `--surface-raised #f1efe9`; borders `#e4e1d9`/`#d6d2c8`. Text inverts to ink: `--tw-text #1a1a1f`, `--tw-text-muted #5c5a55`, `--tw-text-faint #8a8780` (faint stays confined to metadata/captions). The **three-color** system is retained but re-hued for white: `--accent` = **deep emerald `#18794e`** (brand/interactive — buttons, active chip, focus ring, links, wordmark dot, distance pill, gas line, fresh status; passes AA both as link text on white AND as a fill carrying white labels), hover `#15663f`, soft fill/border at emerald alpha; **`--discount` = deep amber `#b45309`** (neon amber is invisible on white — deepened to an AA-passing gold, discount-semantic only); `--warning` = **crimson `#c92a44`** (urgency — happy-hour badge/pulse/countdown re-hue for free), soft fills at crimson alpha; `--danger #c0233d`. `color-scheme: light`. **`--text-on-primary` flips to white `#ffffff`** — the prior "NEVER white" rule (ADR-041) was specific to text sitting on a *light* neon accent; on deep emerald, white is the correct AA-passing label. Elevation goes shadow-led (light-mode shadows replace the dark border-led set's tuning).
**Rationale**: Green reads trustworthy/daytime and is on-theme for cannabis — the largest "lighter, friendlier" jump from neon-on-black, per Erik's "bigger move" call. Warm paper (`#f7f6f3`) over glare-white reduces eye strain. Semantic color is rationed to one job each (emerald=brand/fresh, amber=discount, crimson=urgency) so the page reads calm and color only flashes where money or a clock is at stake — "a touch, used sparingly." Re-hueing rather than literally flipping the neons is mandatory: raw `#ffc83d`/`#ff3d8b` fail AA badly on white, so each load-bearing accent was re-derived to clear 4.5:1. Token names preserved keeps the swap mechanical; one emerald value doubling as link-text and white-label-fill avoids adding tokens.
**Consequences**: **Supersedes the palette of ADR-041** (its three-color structure, `--discount` token, reskin-in-place mechanism, and card layout/motif are all retained — only values change). The `--text-on-primary` reversal is a documented supersession of ADR-041's "NEVER white" rule, not a violation. **WCAG AA is UNVERIFIED until re-audited**: every load-bearing pair changed (ink-on-paper, white-on-emerald, amber/crimson-on-white) — values were *chosen* to pass AA but the formal audit is still owed; DESIGN.md stays `status: draft`, contrast section flagged "AA contrast UNVERIFIED — re-audit before final"; no prose may imply AA holds until re-audited. The emerald-over-amber card-top motif on white may read loud — flagged for visual review. No API, types, data, storage, routing, or component DOM-structure change; deal-card classes/structure untouched. ADR-038's one-card-per-store and ADR-039's distance ordering unaffected.
**Testing**: `client/src/styles/tokens.test.ts` rewritten to guard the Daylight values (`--accent #18794e`, `--discount #b45309`, `--warning #c92a44`, paper surfaces, `@theme` mirror) and assert no superseded Arcade/Synthwave/Tidewater hue survives. Full client suite + `tsc -b` + `eslint .` + `vite build` expected green. Visual verification + formal AA re-audit on the running app pending.

### ADR-043: Store Visibility Decoupled from Distance — `distanceMiles` is Optional Enrichment
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-21)
**Context**: Showing each store's deals is the product's core purpose (gas cost is the hook). But 18 batch-resolved Dutchie stores could not appear at all, because two rules coupled *visibility* to *distance*: `applyIngest` only fills deals into a **pre-existing** `data.json` record (no record → `'unknown'` → deals dropped + red CI job), and `normalizeDispensaries` **dropped any record lacking a finite `distanceMiles`** (the data-hardening rule, `deferred-work.md` line ~77). Adding a store therefore required hand-supplying a road distance from ZIP 98270 — backwards from "deals first, distance second, gas third" (Erik, 2026-06-21). This is what reverted the earlier Dutchie wiring.
**Decision**: Make `distanceMiles` **optional enrichment, never a visibility gate.** `Dispensary.distanceMiles` is now `?: number`. `normalizeDispensaries` keeps a record when distance is **absent** (still drops it when **present but** `NaN`/`Infinity`/negative — a corrupt-record signal), and now also requires a non-empty `id` string. The three distance consumers degrade gracefully: `DealCard` omits the distance pill when distance is non-finite (gas line was already suppressed by a null `gasCostText`); the `DealFeed` radius filter never drops an undistanced store; `groupDealsByStore` sorts undistanced stores **last** (treats absent as `Infinity`). The 18 Dutchie dispensary records are seeded (id/name/url, `stale:true`, epoch `lastFetchedAt`, `deals:[]`) so the proven ADR-034 CI ingest path can match and fill them; the stashed `dutchie-stores.ts` registration (`...dutchieScrapers`) auto-adds all 18 to the `storeIds` scrape matrix.
**Rationale**: This is *more* honest, not less (ADR-007/009): a store with no known distance shows no distance and no gas, rather than a fabricated number. It unblocks the core purpose without forcing 18 hand-measured distances, and leaves a clean additive seam for real per-user distance (Deliverable 2 — geocode + ZIP + haversine — which will retire ADR-008/011; see `deferred-work.md`). The present-but-invalid drop is retained so the `.toFixed`/filter/sort paths never meet a poisoned number.
**Consequences**: **Honest interim state:** the 4 original stores still show their fixed-origin distance/gas (ADR-008), the 18 new stores show deals only — resolved when Deliverable 2 geocodes all stores. Deals do not appear at merge: seeding makes records *eligible*; they populate on the next successful GitHub Actions cron (or manual `workflow_dispatch`). A Dutchie store whose cName fails to resolve stays `stale` and simply stays hidden (and reds its per-store CI job — the ADR-034 §6 alert). `dispensary.url` is currently inert (not rendered); the 18 seeded URLs are `dutchie.com/dispensary/<slug>` placeholders pending real values. Does not touch `applyIngest`/`ingestRun`/scrape logic; ADR-038 one-card-per-store and ADR-039 distance ordering hold (undistanced stores extend the order, last).
**Testing**: Updated `normalizeDispensaries.test.ts` (absent-distance kept, present-invalid dropped, id required), `sortDeals.test.ts` (undistanced sorts last, deterministic), `DealCard.test.tsx` (pill omitted, no crash on absent distance), `DealFeed.test.tsx` (undistanced store survives narrowest slider). Full client + server suites + both `tsc --noEmit` expected green; `printStores.ts` emits all 18 new ids into the CI matrix.

### ADR-044: User-Relative Positioning — Geocode All Stores to `lat`/`lng` (substrate slice)
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-21)
**Context**: Per the 2026-06-21 brainstorm (`_bmad-output/brainstorming/brainstorming-session-2026-06-21-1721.md`), the fixed-origin-98270 distance model (ADR-008/011) is an R&D fossil — distance must be user-relative. 0 of 22 stores carried real coordinates; `distanceMiles` was the legacy 98270 number on the 4 originals and absent on the 18 Dutchie stores. ADR-044 is the umbrella decision ("User-Relative Positioning supersedes fixed-origin distance"); it is delivered as 6 build-order slices (#1 geocode → #2 location input on AgeGate → #3 live distance/sort → #4 centroid cold-start → #5 slider reframe → #6 ferry chip). **This entry covers slice #1 only** — the data substrate that unblocks #3/#4. Slices #2–#6 are in `deferred-work.md`; ADR-008/011 are formally retired when #3 ships.
**Decision**: Added optional `lat?: number` / `lng?: number` to `Dispensary` (additive enrichment like `status`; no consumer reads them yet). A **dev-time, run-once** script (`server/scripts/geocodeStores.ts`, run via `tsx`) resolves each store's street address to coordinates via free OpenStreetMap **Nominatim** (no API key, no runtime dependency, ≤1 req/sec + identifying User-Agent) and writes them into the **committed** `data.json`. Addresses live only in the script's `id → address` map (dev input, **not** a runtime schema field), each cited to its source. A `VERIFIED_COORDS` override holds cited building-precision coords for 4 addresses OSM lacks at house-number precision (Quil Ceda Village / SR-532 / 128th St SW / Guide Meridian); it short-circuits the Nominatim path. The script is **idempotent** (only fills records missing coords), validates every result against a rough WA bounding box (rejects out-of-state mis-geocodes), and exits non-zero listing any uncoorded store.
**Rationale**: Coords must be **committed** because Render's disk is ephemeral — every redeploy resets `data.json` to the seed. They survive cron ingests because `applyIngest` mutates records in place by id, touching only `deals`/`lastFetchedAt`/`stale` (verified, not changed). Nominatim keeps the project on the Render free plan (no paid geocoder, no key). Honest Math (ADR-007/009) is preserved: no coordinate is fabricated — Nominatim results are real, `VERIFIED_COORDS` are cited, and an unsourceable store is left uncoorded rather than guessed.
**Consequences**: **21 of 22 stores geocoded** (all in WA bounds). **`liv-ferndale` left uncoorded** — every lookup collided with LIV Cannabis Ferndale, **Michigan**. **~~Left uncoorded by Erik's call~~ SUPERSEDED by ADR-045:** it turned out the store *was* the Michigan one (wrongly seeded), so it was removed entirely — the data is now **21/21 geocoded**. Note for slice #6: **Salish Coast geocoded to Anacortes (12947 Casino Drive), not Port Townsend** — so the brainstorm's "Salish Coast = ferry" assumption is likely wrong (Anacortes is road-accessible). No runtime behavior changes yet (no consumer reads `lat`/`lng`); distance/sort/centroid arrive in #3/#4.
**Testing**: `server/scripts/geocodeStores.test.ts` — `inWaBounds` (WA accept, Ferndale-MI/border/BC reject), `mergeCoords` (fresh write + idempotent skip), `geocode` (injected GET: parse hit / null on empty / null on non-numeric / propagate transport error), `VERIFIED_COORDS` all in-bounds. Server suite 153 green (+9), client 321 green, full `npm run build` (client `tsc -b && vite build` + server `tsc`) clean.

### ADR-045: Remove Out-of-State Store (LIV Ferndale, MI) + WA-Bounds Guard Rail
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-22) — PR #26 merged, live on `gmaslist.com`
**Context**: While closing out the ADR-044 `liv-ferndale` "uncoorded" gap, the store was found to be **LIV Cannabis in Ferndale, MICHIGAN** — an out-of-state store wrongly swept into the Dutchie expansion (ADR-043 batch). Because each Dutchie store's id *is* its embed cName, the hourly cron was scraping a Michigan dispensary every run, and the geocoder could never place it in WA (it wasn't in WA). It was a data-quality bug, not a missing-address gap.
**Decision**: Removed `liv-ferndale` from all three sites — `server/data/data.json` (22 → 21 stores, all WA-coorded), `DUTCHIE_STORE_IDS` in `server/scrapers/dutchie-stores.ts` (stops the cron hitting the MI embed), and `server/scripts/geocodeStores.ts` (`EXPECTED_UNCOORDED` is now empty). Added a **guard rail** — `server/scrapers/storeRegistry.test.ts` — a data-integrity test over the committed seed asserting every store has finite `lat`/`lng` inside `inWaBounds`, ids are unique, and no Dutchie registry id is an orphan.
**Rationale**: The guard is deliberately a **CI test, not a runtime filter** — a runtime filter could silently hide a store from users and would entangle with the ADR-043 deals-first rendering path (a store renders without distance). The test enforces "every shipped store is geo-verified in WA" at PR time — exactly where `liv-ferndale` slipped through — while changing zero runtime behavior. Adding a legitimate WA store stays the normal flow (add row + registry id → run `geocodeStores.ts` → commit WA coords). A store that can't geocode to WA simply doesn't belong on the list.
**Consequences**: Live data is now **21/21 stores geocoded**, all in WA; no Michigan deals can surface. Any future out-of-state store fails CI rather than reaching production. The ADR-044 "left uncoorded" note is superseded. `EXPECTED_UNCOORDED` remains as a mechanism but is empty — a non-empty set is now a smell (geocode the store, or it likely doesn't belong).
**Testing**: `server/scrapers/storeRegistry.test.ts` (4: WA-bounds for all stores, unique ids, no orphan registry id, liv-ferndale absent); `geocodeStores.test.ts` updated to assert `EXPECTED_UNCOORDED` is empty. Also landed `server/integration/ingestFreshness.test.ts` — the ingest → freshness → alert integration suite (real `POST /api/ingest` → `applyIngest` → `GET /api/data` → `evaluateAlert` through one temp `data.json`). Server suite **177 green (20 files)**, `npm run build` (`tsc`) clean. Verified live post-deploy: `gmaslist.com/api/data` serves 21 stores, no `liv-ferndale`, all coorded.

### ADR-046: Badge-Anchored Title De-Prefix — Suppress the Redundant "N% off" Stutter
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-22)
**Context**: Each discount deal card rendered its magnitude twice — once in the styled percent badge (`gma-deal-block__pct` + "off", e.g. "50% off") and again at the head of the title, because the scraped `deal.description` embeds the same phrase ("50% off Select Brands"). The card read as a stutter ("50% off · 50% off Select Brands"), undercutting the deliberate badge-plus-clean-title layout (ADR-040/041, DealCard.tsx). Spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-deal-title-discount-dedup/SPEC.md`.
**Decision**: Display-only fix. New pure helper `stripDiscountPrefix(title)` in `client/src/utils/dealView.ts` drops a leading `N% off` phrase via `^\s*\d+(?:\.\d+)?\s*%\s*off\b[\s·:•–—-]*` + defensive `.trim()`. `dealTitle(deal, badgeRendering)` in `DealCard.tsx` calls it **only when the percent badge is rendering** (`discountTier(deal.discountPct)` non-null — badge-anchored); when stripping empties the title it falls through to the existing kind fallback (`'Happy hour'` / omit), **never** the raw description.
**Rationale**: The badge is the canonical place for the magnitude; the title carries only the remainder. Badge-anchored so a discount shown nowhere else is never erased. Case-insensitive on "off" because live data uses "Off"/"OFF" while the badge renders lowercase — a case-sensitive match would silently no-op on real descriptions. Whole-phrase consumption (number + "%" + "off" + trailing separator) avoids a dangling "Off" or leading space. `\b` keeps it off "Offers"/"off-brand". Nothing server-side or at ingest changes — `deal.description` is untouched in memory and storage (ADR-007/009 Honest Math: no data rewritten). Non-percent prefixes ("$5 off", "BOGO") are deferred — the badge is percent-only, so they have nothing to duplicate today.
**Consequences**: On a discount card the magnitude appears exactly once (badge + clean title). No badge → title intact. The badge element and its computation (`discountTier`, `deal.discountPct`) are unchanged. Net new prop on the private `dealTitle` helper; no public/API surface change.
**Testing**: `dealView.test.ts` — `stripDiscountPrefix` across N=10/25/50, casings off/Off/OFF, no-prefix passthrough, "$5 off"/"Offers" left intact, empty-on-phrase-only, input-not-mutated. `DealCard.test.tsx` — badge-anchored strip renders clean title (no dangling "Off"), **no-badge negative case** leaves "50% off Select Brands" intact + description not mutated, empty-strip falls back to kind label. Client suite 335 green (+14), server 177 green, full `npm run build` (client `tsc -b && vite build` + server `tsc`) clean.

### ADR-047: Commit-Back Seed — Durable Live Data Across Render Deploys
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-22)
**Context**: The UI renders `data.meta.lastScraperRun` and the count of `stale===true` stores straight from `data.json` (`DealFeed.tsx:113`/`:93`). Render's disk is **ephemeral**, so every redeploy resets `server/data/data.json` to the **committed seed** — frozen at `lastScraperRun=2026-06-14T16:28:05.534Z` with 17 epoch-seeded `stale:true` stores. PR#24's push-triggered re-scrape POSTs to live `/api/ingest` but loses the deploy-cutover race (PR#28: `[ingestRun] ok` at 16:16:10, then the redeploy discarded the write 35s later); scheduled crons fire only ~every 6h, so prod sat frozen for hours after each deploy → the "Last updated Jun 14" / "17 sources unavailable" flip-flop. Investigation: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/investigations/stale-last-updated-and-unavailable-count-investigation.md`; Sprint Change Proposal 2026-06-22.
**Decision**: Keep the **committed** `data.json` continuously fresh by committing the merged scrape result back to `master` (Correct-Course option 1c). A new `commit-back` job in `.github/workflows/scrape-ingest.yml` (`needs: scrape`, `if: always()`, `continue-on-error: true`) downloads the per-store scrape artifacts and merges them into the checked-out `data.json` via `commitBackSeed.ts` → the **existing `applyIngest`**, then commits only `data.json` to master (`chore(data): refresh seed [skip ci]`, `git pull --rebase` + one push retry). The ephemeral reset then restores a recent seed → deploys become non-destructive. `ingestRun.ts` gains an additive `--emit`/`INGEST_EMIT_DIR` that writes each normalized `IngestEntry` to `<dir>/<store>.json` **before** the POST (one scrape feeds both).
**Rationale**: The seed is built from the raw scraped `Deal[]` via `applyIngest` — **NOT** from `GET /api/data`. `dataRoute.ts:21` pipes deals through `filterActiveDeals` (a time-of-request projection that drops out-of-window happy hours / wrong-weekday deals); round-tripping that lossy view into the source of truth would progressively delete real deals. `applyIngest`'s last-known-good semantics (empty scrape → `stale` flag, deals/`lastFetchedAt` untouched; `lastScraperRun` bumped only when `accepted>0`) make the committed file byte-equivalent to live disk and guarantee no active deal is lost across a cycle. Reading git not prod makes the job deploy-independent (no cutover race). Emit-before-POST means a POST that loses the race (exit 1) still leaves a good seed — and the upload step is `if: always()` so that non-zero step doesn't skip it. **Supersedes PR#24's push-re-scrape as the durability mechanism**; the `push` trigger is retained only for the immediate live refresh between deploys.
**Loop prevention**: `paths-ignore: ['server/data/data.json']` on the `push` trigger breaks the commit→scrape→commit loop **in-repo** (fully in our control); `[skip ci]` is belt-and-suspenders. Does NOT depend on Render's "Ignored Paths" (Erik-owned, optional) — even an unsuppressed Render deploy is at worst one harmless extra deploy onto the just-committed fresh seed, not a loop. `git add` is scoped to `data.json` only (never `-A`) so downloaded artifacts can't break the path-based breaker.
**Consequences**: `permissions: contents: write` for the workflow (commit-back pushes; master is unprotected → GITHUB_TOKEN suffices). "N unavailable" now sticks at its true floor (~11–12 genuinely-unresolved Dutchie cNames) instead of resetting to 17 each deploy — this does NOT lower the floor (separate ADR-043/coverage track). The `accepted>0` guard at `applyIngest.ts:59` is unchanged (correct — "Last updated" should reflect when data last truly changed); commit-back re-commits the live `lastScraperRun`, fixing the visible frozen-date symptom without touching the guard. `alert-gate` (ADR-034 §6) remains the SOLE alert; a permanent push failure surfaces as a `::warning::` without reding the run. `server/out/` is gitignored.
**Testing**: `commitBackSeed.test.ts` — (a) fresh non-empty scrape replaces deals + clears stale; (b) **AC2 invariant** empty entry keeps last-known-good deals + `lastFetchedAt`, flips stale, doesn't bump `lastScraperRun`; (c) absent store untouched; (d) a **time-inactive** (02:00–03:00) deal survives the merge while `filterActiveDeals(noon)` drops it (proves the `applyIngest`-not-`/api/data` correctness point); empty/missing dir → byte-identical no-op; `readEntries` skips malformed artifacts. `ingestRun.test.ts` — emit writes the normalized `IngestEntry`, emits before POST (survives a POST failure), no emit without the flag; `parseEmitDir` flag-over-env. Full server suite + `npm run build` green.

### ADR-048: Strip Trailing Cross-Location Tags from Deal Titles (Happy Time "PULLMAN")
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-22)
**Context**: Every deal card for **Happy Time - Mount Vernon** read "… Pre-Rolls PULLMAN" / "… Off Flower PULLMAN". Investigation proved this is **source data, not our code**: the store id `happy-time-mt-vernon` (`dutchie-stores.ts:15`) resolves to the genuine Mount Vernon embed (matches its geocoded `200 Suzanne Ln` address), so it is NOT the Pullman menu mislabeled. `transformSpecials` (`server/scrapers/_dutchie.ts:108`) sets `description ← card.menuDisplayName` **verbatim** (whitespace-collapsed only) — no location field is concatenated. Happy Time is a chain that authors ONE shared promo list across its Pullman and Mount Vernon shops and types a "PULLMAN" tag into each special's display name; we render it unfiltered. Neither cleanup layer removes it: server `sanitizeDescription` is compliance-only, client `stripDiscountPrefix` (ADR-046) only drops the percent figure.
**Decision**: Display-only fix (operator chose client-side over an ingest strip: keeps raw data intact, works immediately even on the committed seed, matches the existing `dealView.ts` display-cleanup pattern). New pure helper `stripCrossLocationTag(title)` in `client/src/utils/dealView.ts` removes one tag from an **operator-maintained** `CROSS_LOCATION_TAGS` list (currently `['PULLMAN']`) pinned to the **END** of the title (`[\s·:•–—*-]*\b(?:…)\b\s*$`, case-insensitive), then cleans the seam. `dealTitle` in `DealCard.tsx` calls it in **both** branches (badge and no-badge) after `stripDiscountPrefix`; an empty result falls through to the kind fallback, never the raw description.
**Rationale**: Operator-tuned allowlist mirrors `DESCRIPTION_BLOCKLIST`'s philosophy — a real city name is only stripped when a listing warrants it, so adding a future chain's tag is a one-line edit. Trailing-only + whole-word (`\b`) keeps it surgical: it can't bite a tag appearing mid-title nor a longer word merely ending in one ("SPULLMAN"). Empty list ⇒ a never-matching `(?!)` pattern (safe degenerate). Data untouched (ADR-007/009 Honest Math) — the dispensary's own text stays in storage; only the rendered card is cleaned.
**Consequences**: Mount Vernon Happy Time cards now read "JUNE 2026 SUMMER SALE Flower" etc. with no location noise. If Happy Time stops tagging, the strip silently no-ops. If another chain cross-posts with a different city, the operator adds it to `CROSS_LOCATION_TAGS`. No server/ingest/API change; the committed seed still contains "PULLMAN" in `data.json` by design (display-time strip).
**Testing**: `dealView.test.ts` — `stripCrossLocationTag` across the three real Happy Time strings, casings (PULLMAN/Pullman/pullman), mid-string left intact, whole-word ("SPULLMAN" untouched), no-tag passthrough, empty-on-tag-only, input-not-mutated. `DealCard.test.tsx` — end-to-end render of a Happy Time deal asserts the card shows "JUNE 2026 SUMMER SALE Flower" and `textContent` does NOT contain "PULLMAN". Targeted suites 68 green; full `npm run build` (client `tsc -b && vite build` + server `tsc`) clean.

### ADR-049: Per-Tier Badge for Layered "Up to X% Off Sale - Y% Off ‹subject›" Cards
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-22)
**Context**: KushMart North, Local Roots (128th), and Kushman's (Evergreen Way) each post ONE card per brand-tier under a shared headline: `Up to 50% Off Sale - 50% Off Brands`, `… - 40% Off Brands`, `… - 30% Off Brands` (8 cards total — the only multi-percent-figure titles in the whole live catalog). Two faults stacked: **(1) over-promise** — the scraper's `parsePercent` (`_dutchie.ts:91`) grabs the FIRST figure ("Up to 50%"), so `discountPct=50` for every tier, badging the 40%/30% tiers as **50% off**; **(2) stutter** — `stripDiscountPrefix`'s multi-tier guard (`figures.length >= 2 → return title`, ADR-046) keeps the title whole, so the card shows badge "50% off" AND a title repeating "50% Off … N% Off". Operator confirmed against the real menus these are genuine tiers (shoppers get Y% on those brands), and chose the per-tier render: badge each tier's own Y%, title the subject only.
**Decision**: Display-only fix (same client-layer pattern as ADR-046/048; data untouched). New pure helper `resolveLayeredSale(description)` in `dealView.ts` matches `^up to N% off sale [-–—] (Y)% off (subject)$` (case-insensitive; hyphen/en/em-dash) and returns `{ pct: Y, title: subject }`, else `null`. `DealCard.tsx` calls it **first** in the deal map: a match overrides both the badge magnitude (`pct = layered.pct`, feeding `discountTier` and the `{pct}%` label) and the title (`layered.title`, e.g. "Brands"), bypassing `dealTitle`/`stripDiscountPrefix` entirely. Non-matches keep the existing path unchanged.
**Rationale**: The helper MUST run before the strip — it takes precedence over the very multi-tier + dangling-qualifier guards (`^up to`) that exist to preserve these strings, so placing it after would mean it never fires. The badge figure is taken from the percent **bound to the product** (Y, the tier), not the marketing headline (X) — the truth-claim half of the fix, gated on operator confirmation, kept separate from the cosmetic strip. Scoped to this exact shape so no other title is touched (verified: 8/8 multi-figure cards are this pattern). Display-layer (not a scraper/`parsePercent` change) keeps it immediately live on the committed seed, preserves the raw `discountPct` in storage, and matches the established title-cleanup architecture.
**Consequences**: The three tiers now render `[50% off] Brands`, `[40% off] Brands`, `[30% off] Brands` — clean and per-tier-truthful. **Known minor divergence**: `sortDeals` still keys off the stored `discountPct` (50 for all three), so within a store the tiers sort together in the 50% band; because they sit in data order (50,40,30) they still render visually descending, and other 50% deals may interleave — acceptable, not worth a data-layer change for 8 cards. This is now the **4th** title-cleanup rule (strip-prefix, dangling-qualifier guard, cross-location, layered-sale); if these keep accruing, consider a general "badge = magnitude, title = subject" consolidation (noted for the operator, not actioned). The `$50 Crossroads Ounce` card (KushMart) is a separate latent risk — currently `discountPct=null` (no badge, correct) but a future scrape that parses "$50" as 50% would falsely badge it; not addressed here.
**Testing**: `dealView.test.ts` — `resolveLayeredSale` across the three real tier strings (50/40/30 → "Brands"), en/em-dash forms, case-insensitivity, null for every non-layered title (`Dabstract - 50% off`, single-figure, bare headline, empty), and null/undefined input. `DealCard.test.tsx` — end-to-end render of all three KushMart tiers asserts badges 50%/40%/30%, exactly three "Brands" titles, and no "Up to"/"Off Brands" anywhere; the old multi-tier-guard test repurposed to a NON-layered 2-figure string so the component-level guard stays covered. Full client suite 373 green; `npm run build` (client + server) clean.

---

### ADR-050: Suppress Description-Only Percent on Price-Titled Dutchie Cards
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-23)
**Context**: `parsePercent` (`_dutchie.ts`) reads the **combined** `name + menuDisplayDescription` text, but the card's displayed title (`Deal.description`) is the `name` ALONE. The `menuDisplayDescription` is not rendered. So a "% off" living only in that (invisible) description badges a card whose visible title shows something else — and when that title is a **price**, the badge directly contradicts it. This is the `$50 Crossroads Ounce` (KushMart) failure: title shows `$50`, a `50% OFF …` in the description made it badge **50% off** in the screenshot, then flipped to no-badge on the next scrape whose description had no `%`. The bare `$50` was never the trigger (`parsePercent` requires a literal `%`); the invisible description was. Flagged as a latent risk in ADR-049's Consequences. **Blast-radius audit across the live catalog (`server/data/data.json`, 2026-06-23): 65 deals, 62 badged; exactly 1 carries a badge percent absent from its visible title — `The Joint – Everett`, title `"Join the Joint: Savings for Everyone!"`, badged 20% from description `"20% OFF ALL ONLINE ORDERS"` — and that one is a GENUINE prose-titled discount. Price-titled badged deals (the `$50` pattern): 0 currently (transient).** So the discriminator is NOT "is the percent in the title" (that bucket holds both the legit Joint deal and the buggy `$50` card) — it is **does the title quote a price**.
**Decision**: Scraper-layer guard, scoped to price-titled cards. New `resolveDiscountPct(name, text)` in `_dutchie.ts`: when the `name` matches `/\$\s*\d/` (states a price) AND has no percent of its own, return `null` — drop the description-sourced percent. Otherwise parse the combined `name + description` as before. `transformSpecials` calls it in place of `parsePercent(text)`. Prose titles with no price (the Joint deal) keep the description percent; a title stating BOTH a price and its own percent (e.g. `"$50 Oz - 50% off"`) keeps that percent.
**Rationale**: Fixes the contradiction at the source (stored `discountPct` becomes correct), unlike the ADR-046/049 display-layer title cleanups — here the data itself was wrong, not just the rendering. Targeting price-titled-without-own-percent is the minimal cut that kills the `$50` contradiction while preserving the one real desc-sourced badge in the catalog: **live blast radius 0** (0 price-titled badged deals today; the Joint deal is prose-titled, untouched). Keeping `parsePercent` reading `name + description` is deliberate — the Joint deal depends on it.
**Consequences**: A price-titled Dutchie special whose only percent lives in the hidden description now renders with no badge (correct — the visible title is a price). A genuine "$50 ounce that is also 50% off" must state the `%` in its displayed title to badge; an operator who wants the badge can adjust the menu title (acceptable — the alternative is re-contradicting the price). This is the first scraper-side (data-layer) percent fix; the title-cleanup rules (ADR-046/048/049) remain display-only. Does not address non-price prose titles whose description percent is wrong/over-stated — out of scope, none observed.
**Testing**: `_dutchie.test.ts` + fixture — added a `$50 Crossroads Ounce` card with `menuDisplayDescription: "50% OFF select ounces"`; asserts `discountPct === null`. Existing card-count tests bumped (5→6 cards, 4→5 deals); the Joint deal's `discountPct === 20` assertion unchanged (proves prose titles still keep the description percent). Server suite green; `npm run build` clean.

### ADR-051: Retry-on-Empty for Dutchie Specials Capture (spurious-stale root fix)
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-23)
**Context**: The hourly `scrape-ingest` alert-gate (ADR-034 §6) kept reding on 7 stores stale for several runs (`the-joint-everett`, `cannazone-old-hwy-99`, `hangar-420-everett/-west`, `cannazone-bellingham`, `2020-solutions-north-bellingham/-pacific-highway`). Forensic trace of the clean scheduled run 2026-06-23 11:58 UTC: **11** Dutchie stores scraped empty (returned 0 deals), of which alert-gate names only the 7 that had previously ingested real deals (last good ~05:01 UTC, now >6h stale); the other 4 are epoch-seed stores excluded from the persistent-stale check. The empties are NOT blocks/404s/cName errors — `postScrape` logs `[scraperClient]` and sets `success:false` on those, and **zero such lines appear** in the run. The Python scraper returns `success:true` with an **empty `getSpecialMenuCards.menuCards`**: the embed loads, but `menuCards` is empty. Downstream, `applyIngest` (correctly) treats an empty scrape as `stale`, keeps last-known-good, and does NOT bump `lastFetchedAt`, so a store that captures empty silently decays past the 6h alert window. **Manual residential-IP probe of the actual embeds (2026-06-23, production stealth `BrowserManager`, control `kushmart-north` = 6 specials confirms the probe) shows the "empty" is NOT one cause but several:** (1) genuinely specials-less stores — `starbuds-bellingham`, `bud-hut-camano-island`, `cannazone-mt-vernon` returned `[0,0,0]` across 3 loads (nothing to scrape — correctly empty, not a bug); (2) Dutchie-side intermittent specials — `the-joint-everett` ingested a real deal at 05:01 but returned `[0,0,0]` at probe time (its special genuinely comes and goes, e.g. day/time-windowed); (3) a CI-environment gap — `sweet-relief-mt-vernon` returns a stable, valid special (`"To-Go Tuesday" / "25% Off All Online Orders Only"` → a clean `discountPct:25, daysValid:[tuesday]` deal) on `[1,1,1]` from a residential IP, yet has NEVER ingested in CI, implicating GitHub's Azure egress (bot-mitigated empty specials) or a CI-only capture-timing miss — NOT a parse/transform drop (the card transforms fine). The original late-emit capture-race (`dutchieRequest`'s documented hazard) is real but is only the (3)-timing slice, not the whole story.
**Decision**: New shared `scrapeDutchieSpecials(storeId, opts)` in `_dutchie.ts` wraps the `postScrape → transformSpecials` pair every Dutchie store already used, retrying while the result is empty up to `DEFAULT_ATTEMPTS` (3 = 1 try + 2 retries). A non-empty capture returns immediately (no wasted retries on healthy stores); a genuinely specials-less store stays empty across all attempts (cheap — empty scrapes return fast). It never throws — a thrown attempt logs `[scraper:<label>]` and counts as empty, preserving the degrade-to-`[]`→stale contract. All four Dutchie entry points (`the-joint-everett`, `jet-cannabis-everett`, `kush21-everett-evergreen` dedicated files + the config-driven `makeDutchieScraper` in `dutchie-stores.ts`) now delegate to it; the per-file try/catch and direct `postScrape`/`transformSpecials` calls are deleted. `ingestRun.ts` `SCRAPE_TIMEOUT_MS` raised 90s→180s so the backstop can't abort a legitimate 3-attempt sequence (each attempt bounded by scraperClient's own 50s axios timeout).
**Rationale**: Node-layer retry (not a Python `scraper-svc` change) is testable with an injected `postFn`, ships immediately, and keeps the vendored service untouched. Retrying on empty is the minimal mitigation for a capture-timing race without re-architecting the wait strategy (a deeper fix — wait for non-empty `menuCards` inside the service — is possible later but higher-risk). `attempts`/`postFn`/`label` are injectable for unit tests and to keep dedicated-store logs readable (their `storeId` is an opaque embed id/cName).
**Consequences**: Retry is a low-cost mitigation that helps ONLY the capture-timing slice (cause 3-timing): a store whose specials exist but are occasionally captured mid-populate now gets up to 3× the chance per run. The probe (see Context) shows that does NOT cover the other empties: genuinely specials-less stores (starbuds/bud-hut/cannazone-mt-vernon) have nothing to scrape — retry runs 3 fast empties and correctly yields `[]` (negligible cost, no false data); Dutchie-side intermittent specials (the-joint) only ingest when a special is actually live, which is correct; and the `sweet-relief` CI gap — a stable special that CI misses — will be DIAGNOSTIC after deploy: if it starts ingesting `"To-Go Tuesday"` on the next scheduled runs, the cause was capture-timing (fixed); if it stays empty while the residential probe still sees the special, the cause is GitHub's datacenter egress (Azure IP served empty specials), which needs a different fix (residential/proxy egress or a server-side wait-for-non-empty), NOT more retries. So this ADR closes the timing slice and converts the rest into a clean, observable next signal rather than claiming to fix all 7. Latent: a store that legitimately drops all specials still (correctly) alerts after 6h — a real "gone dark" signal.
**Testing**: `_dutchie.test.ts` — new `scrapeDutchieSpecials` suite with a queued/counting fake `postFn`: returns first-attempt deals with no retry; retries past two empty captures then returns populated deals; returns `[]` after exhausting attempts on a specials-less store; treats a thrown attempt as empty and keeps retrying; respects a custom `attempts` count. Server suite 197 green; `npm run build` (client + server) clean. **LIVE-CONFIRMED 2026-06-23 (push run 28037294947, master `32eb2a2`): alert-gate flipped 🔴→🟢, `fresh=10/21 → 18/21`, `persistentlyStale=[7] → []`.** All 7 previously-flagged stores now ingest `ok`, INCLUDING `sweet-relief-mt-vernon` (its stable "To-Go Tuesday" special is now captured) — so cause 3 was **capture-timing, not the Azure-IP egress hypothesis**, which is hereby disproven. The only 3 stores still `stale` (`bud-hut-camano-island`, `cannazone-mt-vernon`, `starbuds-bellingham`) are exactly the probe-confirmed genuinely-specials-less stores — correct (epoch-excluded, no alert).

### ADR-052: SEO Spec Decomposition — Split Phase 0a from 0b at the Risk-Class Seam (0a ships independent of legal review)
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-24)
**Context**: The "SEO & AI Crawler Visibility" spec (`_bmad-output/specs/spec-seo-crawler-visibility/`, commit c1a76f7) raised a handoff question: can it go to implementation whole, or must it be broken into epics/stories first? A party-mode roundtable (PM/John, Dev/Amelia, Architect/Winston) plus a direct code trace converged on one decision point — Phase 0a (server-side content delivery: Express injects a deal/store snapshot into `index.html`, touching `server/index.ts:44-46`) and Phase 0b (AgeGate mount-but-visually-gate) share a *goal* ("the crawler sees real content") but are different **risk classes**. 0a is pure engineering — testable, fully controllable, ships on our timeline. 0b carries a HARD external dependency: legal review of WAC 314-55-155 (may content sit in page markup while visually/interactively blocked?), which is NOT an engineering call. Code trace confirms the seam is real: `client/src/main.tsx:6` mounts with `createRoot().render()` (NOT `hydrateRoot`), which **discards** `#root` on boot — so server-injected content inside `#root` survives only for clients that never run React (the non-JS crawlers 0a targets), while humans/Googlebot run React → `createRoot` wipes `#root` → `AgeGate` (`App.tsx:17`, renders children only when `isIn`, `AgeGate.tsx:37`) gates exactly as today. Thus **0a satisfies CAP-1 (non-JS crawlers) with zero legal dependency; CAP-2 (Googlebot past the gate) genuinely requires 0b + legal sign-off.**
**Decision**: Do NOT hand the spec over whole. Decompose at the 0a/0b seam. 0a is its own story and ships now, independent of the legal gate; 0b is a separate story with status `blocked: legal` (WAC 314-55-155) that does not enter a workflow until legal answers the open question. Proposed structure (to be finalized when `bmad-create-epics-and-stories` runs — held pending discussion with Erik): Epic 1 Crawler Content Delivery (Story 1.1 = 0a, Story 1.2 = 0b/blocked); Epic 2 Discoverability (Phase 1 robots/sitemap/meta/JSON-LD + 1a `/store/<slug>`, depends on 0a); Epic 3 Enrichment & Measurement (Phases 2–6, low priority). Phase 5 (off-site) is outside the repo, not a dev story.
**Rationale**: Coupling of *purpose* is not coupling of *risk* (Winston). Welding a legal blocker onto the change that delivers ~90% of the value (Phase 0) makes the legal review our critical path — 0a sits hostage behind 0b for no engineering reason. Splitting at the seam ships most of the value while the legal question is answered in parallel. The code trace removes the ambiguity that made "hand it whole" tempting: 0a provably does not expose content past the gate for any JS-executing client.
**Consequences**: 0a's story carries a load-bearing AC — inject content *inside* `#root` (a crawler-only sink `createRoot` erases), NOT as human-visible markup outside it; injecting outside would leak deal content past the gate to humans and drag 0a into 0b's compliance territory. One sub-issue handed to the 0b legal review (does NOT block 0a): a sub-second pre-hydration window where raw deal HTML exists in a human's DOM before `createRoot` wipes it — a far weaker form of the 0b question (transient flash vs. content persistently parked behind an overlay). Phase 2 (info-gain stats) has thin AC — its `data.json` field schema must be pinned before that story is dev-ready. Decision detail and the full roundtable transcript live in `_bmad-output/specs/spec-seo-crawler-visibility/HANDOFF_DECISION.md`.
**Testing**: N/A (decomposition/process decision; no code shipped). Code trace evidence: `server/index.ts:44-46` (static SPA fallback, no injection today), `client/src/main.tsx:6` (`createRoot`), `client/src/App.tsx:17` + `client/src/components/AgeGate.tsx:37` (whole-app conditional gate).

### ADR-053: Decoupled Dutchie Product / Menu-Pricing Capture (additive price-comparison engine + longitudinal dataset)
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-24)
**Context**: gmaslist.com shows deals but never an actual product price — the specials feed (`GetSpecialMenuCards`) carries only free-text percent-off. Live recon (`_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/investigations/menu-pricing-source-inventory.md`, real fixture `server/scrapers/__fixtures__/dutchie-products.json`) confirmed Dutchie's **`FilteredProducts`** op exposes real per-weight prices, special prices, and pack/inventory data. SPEC-dutchie-product-pricing asked for a backend **price-comparison engine + dataset Erik owns** (normalize to $/gram and $/item so unlike packs compare correctly; track price over time) as a NEW capability that must NOT disturb the hardened in-production deals pipeline (`_dutchie.ts`, the `Deal` type, `filterActiveDeals`, the `/api/data` contract, the 197-test regression net).
**Decision**: Build it fully additive. New module `server/scrapers/_dutchieProducts.ts` issues a SEPARATE scrape request waiting on `FilteredProducts` (imports only `dutchieEmbedUrl` from `_dutchie.ts`), assembles products across paginated responses (concat + dedupe by id), and retries on empty (mirrors `scrapeDutchieSpecials`). Base price ← `POSMetaData.children[]` matched by `option` (NOT index — children can be shorter than `Options[]` under thresholds); special price ← positional `recSpecialPrices[i]`. `normalizeProduct.ts` (CAP-5) parses pack count from the free-text `Name` and grams from the `Options` label, computing $/gram (all) and $/item (pre-rolls); the raw `weight` field is a VALIDATION signal only (cross-checked against `measurements.netWeight` and `weight×packCount` → `weight-mismatch` flag), never the per-gram basis. `productsStore.ts` (CAP-6) is append-only — each scrape appends a timestamped observation per product, computed $/g and $/item stored in the observation. New read-only `GET /api/products` serves the dataset; `server/scripts/scrapeProductsRun.ts` is the commit-back CLI. **Persistence = commit-back to repo** (Erik's call 2026-06-24): CI appends + commits `server/data/products.json` so history survives Render's ephemeral disk — a server-written file would lose history every deploy. Launch scope = Pre-Rolls, Flower, Vaporizers; rec pricing only.
**Rationale**: Welding product capture onto the deals path risked the live revenue surface for zero benefit; full decoupling (separate module/request/type/store/endpoint) keeps the deals contract byte-for-byte unchanged (the 197 tests prove it) while adding the new capability. Commit-back is the only durable store given the ephemeral disk + "no new infra" constraint, and it doubles as the export format. Children-by-option (not index) is correct against `removeProductsBelowOptionThresholds`; `weight` as validation-not-basis avoids betting per-gram math on a one-sample unit inference.
**Consequences**: `/api/products` ships read-only. Infra WIRED with Erik's sign-off 2026-06-24: (1) `.github/workflows/scrape-products.yml` — a DAILY commit-back job (separate concurrency group / file / schedule from the hourly deals `scrape-ingest.yml`) boots the scraper, runs `scrapeProductsRun.ts` via `tsx` against SOURCE (so `DEFAULT_PRODUCTS_PATH` resolves to `server/data/products.json`, the committed-back file — NOT `dist/server/data/...`, the compiled-run gotcha this avoids), and pushes the refreshed dataset with `[skip ci]`. (2) Pagination closed by an opt-in `scroll_after_wait` flag added through `ScrapeRequest` (TS + Pydantic) → `interceptor.navigate_and_collect`: after `FilteredProducts` fires the service now scrolls + holds to pull the rest of the menu's pages, which `pickProducts` assembles + dedupes. The flag defaults false, so the deals scrape's wait-and-return timing is byte-identical (only `dutchieProductsRequest` sets it). **[SUPERSEDED 2026-07-18 by ADR-089: the embed menu is NUMBERED pagination, not scroll — `scroll_after_wait` never pulled pages 2..N and was removed; the opt-in `paginate` in-page walk replaces it.]** The three original Dutchie stores (the-joint/jet/kush21) remain excluded from product scraping pending embed-id wiring (id ≠ cName). **[UPDATE 2026-06-24 — Item 3 DONE, PR#34 `f18fdb7`]** Now WIRED: `ORIGINAL_DUTCHIE_PRODUCT_CNAMES` in `dutchie-stores.ts` registers each under its readable id while passing the cName (taken from the per-store deals files) to `scrapeDutchieProducts`, so observations key to the readable `dispensaryId`. Verified live (run 28125705593, commit `72f06f3`): the-joint **65**, jet **75**, kush21 **332** products → store coverage 14→**17**, total 1667→**2142**. (kush21's Dutchie-Plus custom-domain concern did NOT materialize — its standard embed serves `FilteredProducts`.) Guard tests added to `storeRegistry.test.ts` (originals registered + product-scraper-id-in-seed orphan check); suite 258→260. **Separate PRE-EXISTING gap (NOT Item 3, open):** 3 BATCH stores still silently yield 0 products — `cannazone-mt-vernon`, `the-vault-silvana`, `bud-hut-camano-island` — fail-soft, no error; needs embed/intercept investigation. **Non-blocking ToS/legal reminder** (per Erik 2026-06-24): whole-menu pricing + live `quantityAvailable` across 21 stores is a heavier extraction than the promo feed — revisit before any public surfacing / redistribution / commercial use; does NOT gate R&D / private-dataset use. Open question (retention horizon / export shape) resolved provisionally: keep all history in-repo, export = the JSON file itself.
**Testing**: 56 new server tests, all green; full suite **253 passed** (197 baseline preserved). `_dutchieProducts.test.ts` (19) — request preset, multi-page assembly + dedupe, multi-option Space Kush base↔special alignment, children-shorter-than-Options by-option match, category filtering, retry-on-empty. `normalizeProduct.test.ts` (20) — pack/grams parsing tables, 2g/2-pack $4/joint, 3pk-vs-5pk different $/item same $/g, unparseable-pack flag (no silent qty=1), weight-mismatch flag, flower $/gram-only. `productsStore.test.ts` (10) — append-not-overwrite, price-change detectable across ≥2 scrapes, fail-soft read, round-trip. `productsRoute.test.ts` (2) + `scrapeProductsRun.test.ts` (5). `npm run build` (server tsc + client vite) clean; `products.json` ships to `dist/server/data`. Deals contract files (`_dutchie.ts`, `Deal` type, `filterActiveDeals`, `dataRoute`, `data.json`) untouched per git.

### ADR-054: Fix silent fractional-ounce mis-parse in `parseGrams` (`1/8oz`→1g bug)
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-24)
**Context**: The FIRST live `scrape-products` run (PR #32 merged as `e6a4907`; run 28122803634; observation `2026-06-24T19:05:43Z` committed as `5f2f6a9`, served live at `gmaslist.com/api/products`) captured 1665 products across 14 stores. Verification of the committed data exposed a normalization bug: `parseGrams("1/8oz")` returned **1 gram** instead of 3.54. The regex `([\d.]+)` matches only the leading numerator (`1`), stops at the `/`, and the `oz` branch never runs — so an eighth/quarter/half ounce was treated as 1g. This inflated `pricePerGram` for **568 of 710 oz options** (a $35 eighth showed "$35/g" instead of ~$9.89/g). Worst part: a number *did* match, so `weightGrams` was non-null → the `unparseable-weight` flag never fired (SILENT), and the `weight-mismatch` reconciliation couldn't catch it because it trusts the same parsed grams. `1oz`→28.35 was always correct; `$/joint` (pricePerItem, pre-rolls) was unaffected.
**Decision**: Add a fraction branch to `parseGrams` that matches `(\d+)\s*/\s*(\d+)` with an optional unit and evaluates numerator/denominator before the general number match. `1/8oz` → 0.125 × 28.3495 → **3.54g** (true eighth-ounce, consistent with the existing `1oz`→28.35 true-ounce convention — NOT the industry-rounded 3.5g). Genuinely unparseable labels still return null and flag as before; the fix only removes the wrong-non-null path. Four parsing-table cases added (`1/8oz`, `1/4oz`, `1/2oz`, `1/2 oz` whitespace-tolerant).
**Rationale**: $/gram is the headline comparison metric for Flower/Vaporizers — a silent 3.5× error on the most common flower denominations defeats the engine's purpose. A targeted fraction branch is minimal and leaves the validated `g`/`mg`/`oz`/bare-number paths byte-identical. Fix-forward chosen over scrubbing the already-committed bad observation (Erik 2026-06-24): it's an R&D/private dataset with no public UI consumer, so the corrected observation simply appends after the bad one in `history`.
**Consequences**: Corrects all future fractional-oz observations. The single bad observation `2026-06-24T19:05:43Z` REMAINS in `history` by decision — CAP-6 longitudinal consumers must tolerate it (or filter by `observedAt`). No data-shape change; deals pipeline untouched. The three original Dutchie stores remain excluded (ADR-053). No bare fractions (`1/8` without a unit) appeared in the live dataset, but the new branch handles them too.
**Testing**: `normalizeProduct.test.ts` 25 green (4 new fraction cases); full server suite **258 passed** (was 254); `npm run build` (server tsc + client vite) clean. Re-dispatch of `scrape-products` confirms `1/8oz`→3.54g / ~$9.89/g in the next committed observation.

---

### ADR-055: Store Address on the Deal Card + Retire the Hardcoded National-MPG Default
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-26) — supersedes the *default* half of ADR-003 and all of ADR-013; fueleconomy.gov precision mode (ADR-003/028) retained
**Context**: Chunk 1 of `SPEC-location-distance-and-card-revisions` (the consumer-facing half of ADR-044, plus two corrections Erik called out). Deal cards never showed a store's street address — the one fact identifying *which* store a shopper is looking at. Separately, gas-cost math silently fell back to a hardcoded 28-MPG national average (ADR-003/013), a fabricated number violating Honest Math (ADR-007/009): it looked exact but meant nothing for a user not driving the average car. Addresses already existed, cited, in `geocodeStores.ts` `STORE_ADDRESSES` (dev input only, no runtime field).
**Decision**: (1) **Address** — promoted to a real optional schema field `Dispensary.address`, committed verbatim into `data.json` for every store (additive enrichment mirroring `lat`/`lng`/`status`; NOT a visibility gate per ADR-043). Rendered top-right in the card header at deal-title size (`var(--text-sm)`, matching `.gma-deal-block__title`); the distance pill tucks beneath it; the gas line is pushed below. With no location, the address is the sole top-right element. `DealCard` guards with `typeof` + `.trim()`; a bad/empty/non-string address omits the line but never hides the store (`normalizeDispensaries` does NOT gate on address — only `distanceMiles` keeps its present-but-invalid drop, since it feeds `.toFixed`/filter/sort math). (2) **MPG** — removed `meta.nationalMpg` end-to-end (type, `data.json`, the `DealFeed` fallback, `VehicleSelector` copy, all 9 test fixtures). MPG now comes only from the user's chosen vehicle; gas cost requires BOTH a finite distance AND a vehicle MPG — either missing → no gas line (distance still shows).
**Rationale**: More honest, not less — never a gas figure built on a guessed MPG. Address-as-enrichment keeps ADR-043 deals-first intact (a store renders on id+deals). Lifting cited addresses (no fabrication) preserves Honest Math. The frozen-spec "drop on bad address" rule was reversed after the blind-hunter review flagged it would hide a store's deals over a cosmetic field (Erik-directed).
**Consequences**: Users now see each store's address; gas $ appears only after picking a vehicle (more users see no gas $ than before — intended). fueleconomy.gov becomes load-bearing for ALL gas figures (no fallback) — its reliability gaps (timeout/spinner/retry) are scheduled for chunk 3. The `distanceMiles` shown is still the interim fixed-origin number (ADR-008/011) until chunk 2 makes it per-user. Docs synced (`data-models.md`, `api-contracts.md`).
**Testing**: client **383 green**, server **260 green**; full `npm run build` (client `tsc -b && vite build` + server `tsc`) clean; `grep nationalMpg client/src server` = zero. 3-reviewer adversarial pass (blind/edge/acceptance) — acceptance auditor fully compliant; one normalize patch + the address-gate reversal applied.

### ADR-056: Remove Duplicate Store `kushmans-everett-evergreen-way` (same site as `kush21-everett-evergreen`)
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-26)
**Context**: The chunk-1 review (ADR-055) surfaced that `kush21-everett-evergreen` and `kushmans-everett-evergreen-way` carried the identical address (`8911 Evergreen Way`, verbatim from `STORE_ADDRESSES`, "same site as Kush21 Evergreen") and already shared `lat`/`lng` since the ADR-044 geocode. Erik confirmed they are the same physical shop.
**Decision**: Removed `kushmans-everett-evergreen-way` entirely (same pattern as ADR-045): from `server/data/data.json` (21→**20 stores**), `server/data/products.json` (−272 product observations), `DUTCHIE_STORE_IDS` (`dutchie-stores.ts`), and `STORE_ADDRESSES` (`geocodeStores.ts`). `kush21-everett-evergreen` is retained as the canonical record for that site.
**Rationale**: Two records for one shop showed users a duplicate and double-scraped the same menu. Removing the duplicate (not kush21) keeps the canonical wired original. The `storeRegistry.test.ts` guard rail (ADR-045) enforces no orphan registry/product-scrape ids, so removal had to span all four sites.
**Consequences**: Live store list is now **20 stores**, all WA-geocoded. No new kushmans products accrue (registry entry gone); the 272 historical kushmans product observations were purged as duplicates of kush21's menu. Guard rail re-verified green.
**Testing**: `storeRegistry.test.ts` 6 green; server **260 green**; build clean.

### ADR-057: User-Relative Distance via Device GPS + WA ZIP Centroids — Retire Fixed-Origin Distance (ADR-008/011)
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-26) — supersedes ADR-008 and ADR-011 (fixed-origin, hardcoded `distanceMiles`)
**Context**: Chunk 2 of `SPEC-location-distance-and-card-revisions` (CAP-2 + CAP-3, the rest of ADR-044 #2/#3). The 4 original stores carried a single fixed-origin distance (from ZIP 98270, ADR-008/011) identical for every visitor — a Honest-Math violation (ADR-007/009): exact-looking, meaningless to anyone not in Marysville. All 20 stores already carry committed `lat`/`lng` (ADR-044). No paid services / API keys allowed (Render free).
**Decision**: (1) **Location input — two doors** (Erik-confirmed placement): a first-run onboarding step after the age gate AND a persistent top-of-feed `LocationBar`, both driving one `useLocation` hook. Door A = browser Geolocation (one tap, exact, any region); Door B = a typed WA ZIP resolved against a committed Census-2024 ZCTA centroid table (`client/src/data/zipCentroids.wa.json`, 605 WA ZIPs, public domain, filtered to GEOID 98001–99403 ∩ WA bounds). A non-WA/garbage ZIP → honest no-location state (no number, no crash). Location persists in `localStorage` (`gma_location`), validated at the use site like `gma_vehicle_mpg`. (2) **One transform** `applyUserDistance` REPLACES every store's `distanceMiles` = `haversine(user, store) × 1.3` road factor, run once in `DealFeed` before the radius filter / nearest-first sort / render — so all existing consumers and the existing `roundTripGasCost` are untouched. With no location (or no store coords) it STRIPS `distanceMiles` → no pill, no gas line anywhere; deals still list (CAP-2, ADR-043). (3) **Carry-in (ADR-044 #1):** `normalizeDispensaries` now STRIPS a present-but-non-finite `lat`/`lng` but KEEPS the store + its deals — coords are deals-first enrichment like `address`, NOT a drop gate (deviation from mechanism.md's "mirror the distanceMiles drop", Erik/advisor-aligned).
**Rationale**: The unconditional REPLACE is what actually retires ADR-008/011: the seed stores' stale fixed-origin value can never reach the UI, and stripping `data.json` was rejected (the `chore(data)` cron may re-inject it). One formula home (no second distance/gas formula). WA-only ZIP is by design (non-goal: national coverage). GPS unrestricted because haversine is global and the radius slider already bounds the feed.
**Consequences**: Distance/gas are now genuinely per-user; a visitor who skips location sees deals but no distances/$ (intended). New client surface: `distance.ts`, `zipCentroids.ts`+table, `withUserDistance.ts`, `useLocation.ts`, `LocationInput`/`LocationOnboarding`/`LocationBar`. `tsconfig.app.json` gains `resolveJsonModule`. The radius-slider reframe (#5) and centroid cold-start sort (#4) remain out of scope; fueleconomy.gov hardening + vehicle-selector prominence (CAP-5) are chunk 3.
**Testing**: client **439 green** (incl. new distance/zip/transform/hook/component suites + `DealFeed.distance.test.tsx` proving CAP-2/CAP-3 with the real transform, and the identity-mock seam in `DealFeed.test.tsx`); server **260 green**; full `npm run build` clean.

### ADR-058: Obvious Vehicle Control via a Persistent VehicleBar — Retire the Header Gear (CAP-5)
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-27)
**Context**: Chunk 3 of `SPEC-location-distance-and-card-revisions` (CAP-5). The vehicle selector was reachable only through an unlabeled gear `IconButton` in the `Header`. That became a discoverability gap once ADR-055 removed the national-MPG default: with no vehicle chosen there is no gas figure anywhere (Honest Math), so a first-time user had no signposted path to the feature that makes gas costs appear. Chunk 2 (ADR-057) had just established a clean precedent — `LocationBar`, a persistent labeled top-of-feed control with a set/change summary.
**Decision**: Add a persistent, labeled `VehicleBar` directly below `LocationBar` (Erik-chosen form), mirroring its structure/`.gma-*-bar` styling so the two read as a pair. No vehicle → a plainly labeled "Set your vehicle for gas costs" CTA + a **Set** button; vehicle set → a "`<label> · NN MPG`" summary + a **Change** button. Both open the EXISTING `VehicleSelector` bottom sheet via App's existing `settingsOpen` state. The unlabeled `Header` gear (and its `onOpenSettings` prop) is **removed** — `Header` is now a prop-less wordmark, leaving a single obvious entry point (Erik-confirmed). `VehicleBar` is purely presentational (`{mpg, label, onOpen}`); `VehicleSelector` and `useVehicleMpg` are reused unchanged.
**Rationale**: Reusing the LocationBar pattern keeps one mental model for the two "set your context" controls and avoids a competing affordance in the header. Removing the gear (rather than keeping both) prevents two entry points to one sheet. Honest Math holds: the bar shows only the validated stored vehicle label/MPG, never a gas figure or guess; a corrupt/half vehicle pair collapses via `useVehicleMpg` to the CTA (no half-state).
**Consequences**: New `client/src/components/VehicleBar.tsx` (+test); `Header` is prop-less (tests updated to assert no button + intact wordmark); `App` mounts the bar and drops `Header`'s prop. Scope was narrowed by Erik to **CAP-5 only** — fueleconomy.gov hardening (timeout/spinner/retry/empty-result) and the radius-slider reframe / empty-feed #1 (ADR-044 #5) were explicitly deselected and remain deferred (`deferred-work.md`), including a logged item to integer-validate MPG at the `resolveMpg` source so both the bar and the sheet render clean values.
**Testing**: client **452 green** (incl. new `VehicleBar.test.tsx`); 3-reviewer bmad pass clean (no loopback); eslint + full `npm run build` (client + server) clean.

### ADR-059: ZIP Door Reads the Live DOM Value at Submit — Don't Gate on Controlled State Alone
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-28)
**Context**: Erik reported (twice, across sessions) that manually entering a WA ZIP (e.g. 98274) "does nothing / doesn't save," while "Use my location" (GPS) works. Investigation ruled out data and build: 98274 is in the committed + live ZCTA table, the live bundle == current source, and a jsdom repro of the typed flow *passed*. The tell was behavioural — **no red error AND no change** ⇒ `handleSubmit` never ran ⇒ the Go button was disabled ⇒ controlled `zip` state was empty ⇒ the field value never reached React state. `LocationInput` gated the button on `disabled={zip.trim()===''}` and read `zip` (state) at submit. Any input path that sets the field value WITHOUT firing React's `onChange` (mobile autofill, keyboard suggestion strip, IME composition) leaves state empty → button stays disabled → tap is a silent no-op. GPS is a plain button, immune. The prior "proof" was hollow because `fireEvent.change` *fires* onChange, exercising only the path that already worked.
**Decision**: Make the ZIP door mechanism-agnostic. `handleSubmit` now reads the live DOM value (`event.currentTarget.querySelector('input')?.value ?? zip`), early-returns on empty (so an empty tap is a no-op, NOT a spurious "bad ZIP" error), then drives `setZip`/`setSubmittedZip`/`onZipSubmit` with that value. The `disabled` gate on the Go button is removed so the submit always fires. Input stays controlled for the normal typed path.
**Rationale**: Don't name the device quirk — fix the class. Reading the DOM at submit + an always-tappable button works regardless of how the value arrived. Both changes are required: removing `disabled` alone (still reading empty state) would convert "nothing happens" into a red `zip-not-found` error without setting location. Honest Math is untouched — an invalid-but-nonempty ZIP still errors correctly.
**Consequences**: `LocationInput.tsx` submit handler + Go button changed; `TextField` left as-is (no `forwardRef` needed — the form is `event.currentTarget`). New regression test sets the input's DOM value with NO `fireEvent.change`, then `fireEvent.submit` — proven red on old code, green after. Could not device-verify (Chrome extension offline); targets the most likely cause, pending Erik's confirmation on his tablet.
**Testing**: red→green regression test added; full `LocationInput` suite 11 green; `npm run build` (client) clean (bundle `index-CbtUYPZa.js`).

### ADR-060: Echo the Active ZIP in LocationBar — Make a Wrong/Auto-Filled ZIP Visible
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-28)
**Context**: Erik reported the ZIP feed was "doing something wrong — Everett shown closer than Tulalip for 98274." Investigation proved the opposite: a `changeZip` integration test against the **real live `/api/data`** showed changing 98224→98274 re-centers correctly in one submit (first card = CannaZone Old Hwy 99 ~12mi; Tulalip ranks ahead of Everett). The decisive evidence was a screenshot whose three distances (41.9/42.1/43.1 mi) matched the **98224** (Baring) centroid to the decimal — the box held 98224, not 98274 (a one-digit typo, possibly aided by Chrome auto-filling a saved ZIP into the `postal-code` field). The order logic was correct; the real gap was that the bar only ever said "Distances from your ZIP area" — it never showed **which** ZIP was active, so a wrong value was invisible and undiagnosable.
**Decision**: Store the resolved 5-digit ZIP on the location (`UserLocation.zip?`, optional/backward-compatible; `validate` keeps it only when it matches `^\d{5}$`, never lets a bad value invalidate the coordinates) and echo it in `LocationBar`: "Distances from ZIP 98274". GPS unchanged. Self-revealing AND diagnostic — if the bar's ZIP and the order disagree, that's a real bug; if they agree, it's an input problem.
**Rationale**: Mechanism-agnostic and minimal. ZIP **number only** — there is no committed ZIP→city table, so a city name would require inventing data (rejected). The `autoComplete` angle is silent-hardening territory, not sold as the cause (Chrome routinely ignores `autoComplete="off"` on postal fields; the parsimonious cause is a typo).
**Consequences**: `UserLocation` gains optional `zip`; `useLocation.setFromZip` records the 5-digit prefix; `LocationBar` summary branches on `location.zip`. New permanent `changeZip.test.tsx` (inline 3-store fixture, no committed live dump) asserts change-of-ZIP re-centering + ZIP echo. Could not device-verify (extension offline) — the echo IS the on-device diagnostic handed to Erik (clear box, type 98274, read back the bar).
**Testing**: full client suite **454 green**; `npm run build` clean (bundle `index-1OZk6YlX.js`).

### ADR-061: Decorative Randomized Icon Mosaic Behind the Age Gate + Horizontal Bottom Warning Bar
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-28)
**Context**: Erik supplied 39 product-category illustrations (`GmasINCOlist/`, 1024² JPEGs, ~12MB total) to use as a full-page background behind the 21+ age gate, with the WA-mandated legal warnings moved to run horizontally along the bottom — and an explicit requirement that the icons never make the warnings (or card) unreadable.
**Decision**: (1) **Asset pipeline** — resized all 39 JPEGs → 128px WebP tiles (`client/src/assets/age-icons/`, **147KB total** vs 12MB) via ImageMagick; source folder untouched. Collected with `import.meta.glob('../assets/age-icons/*.webp', {eager, query:'?url'})` so the set self-updates. (2) **Background** — a new `IconField` (aria-hidden, `pointer-events:none`) renders a tight, gapless CSS grid (`repeat(auto-fill, minmax(96px,1fr))`) sized to the viewport; tiles are a per-mount shuffled cycle of the 39 icons at `opacity 0.16` over the `--surface-inverse` fill — faint texture, not a wall. It sits **outside** the `alertdialog`/focus-trap container. (3) **Readability is structural, not a dimming gamble** — the overlay background is now `transparent`; the centered card keeps its opaque `--surface-card`, and the four warnings moved from a vertical stack into a `WarningBar` — a full-bleed bottom strip with its own opaque `--surface-card` background (4-up `auto-fit minmax(200px,1fr)` grid → horizontal on desktop, stacks on mobile). No random tile arrangement can touch text legibility. (4) The icon field renders on **both** gate states (ask + declined "Come back at 21"); warnings stay on the ask state.
**Rationale**: Inlining was the trap — at 128px the tiles fall under Vite's 4KB inline limit, so the first build base64-baked ~190KB into the critical JS bundle that **every** visitor loads even after the gate is dismissed. A `build.assetsInlineLimit` function excluding `age-icons` keeps them as separate cacheable files fetched only when the gate actually renders (JS gzip **124KB→79.5KB**). Per-region opaque backgrounds make legibility a property of the layout, not a tuning of opacity. `AGE_GATE_WARNINGS` (single source of truth per `legal.ts`) is reused verbatim — relocation only.
**Consequences**: `AgeGate.tsx` gains `IconField`/`WarningBar` + restructured top/center/bottom column layout; `smallPrintStyle` replaced by `warningBarStyle`/`warningItemStyle`. New committed asset dir (39 WebP, 147KB). `vite.config.ts` gains a build option. Tiles depend on `window.inner*` at mount (no SSR here — fine). The gate now reads as a **light** mosaic page (light tiles cover the dark fill), a shift from the prior dark takeover — intentional, looks on-brand.
**Testing**: all 13 `AgeGate.test.tsx` pass unchanged (warnings asserted by verbatim text, layout-agnostic); `tsc --noEmit` clean; full `npm run build` clean (39 WebP emitted as separate assets, JS gzip 79.5KB). **Visual verification (the gating check, unit tests can't see layout):** headless Playwright screenshots at 1440×900 and 390×844 confirm tight randomized mosaic, fully-readable card, and horizontal/stacked warning bar on an opaque strip.
