### ADR-062: Cross-Store Value Matcher — proprietary same-product price-disparity dataset (A1 keystone)
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-28)
**Context**: The scraped product dataset (`products.json`, 2461 records / 16 stores) is commodity data any one store already holds. The AI-search/proprietary-data plan (`investigations/ai-search-data-strategy-investigation.md`) identifies item **A1** — matching the SAME product across DIFFERENT stores to emit a cross-store price-disparity fact — as the keystone the cheapest-delivered index, brand matrix, and AI-search surfacing all compose from. Prior investigations proved discount % is a flat storewide promo rate (no per-item signal, `fix6`) and that $/gram is honest ONLY same-product cross-store with quality held constant (`value-analysis` §4). The risk: a sloppy matcher that emits FALSE disparities (comparing different strains or mis-parsed weights) reintroduces exactly the "data that lies" the plan exists to avoid — the output's integrity IS the moat.
**Decision**: A purely additive, read-only matcher over `ProductsFile`. (1) `server/utils/productMatchKey.ts` — `deriveMatchKey(rec)` builds a weight-free identity key `brand|strainToken|strainType|category` (unmatchable → explicit `{unmatched}` bucket, never dropped). `canonicalWeightGrams(option)` reuses `parseGrams` (ADR-054) then **snaps to the nearest standard cannabis weight within 2%** so `1/8oz` (3.54g) and `3.5g` both canonicalize to 3.5g (an eighth is an eighth), while `3.6g` stays distinct. (2) `server/utils/crossStoreValue.ts` — `buildMatchReport(file)` groups by `(identityKey, canonicalWeight)`, takes the latest observation's `specialPrice ?? basePrice`, reduces to the cheapest offer per store, requires ≥2 distinct stores, and emits `Disparity{matchKey,displayName,category,weightGrams,lowPrice,highPrice,spread,spreadPct,storesCarrying[]}` sorted by widest spread%. (3) `GET /api/value/disparities` (`valueRoute.ts`) serves the report private/internal, mirroring `productsRoute`. **Honesty gates:** records flagged `weight-mismatch`/`unparseable-weight`/`unparseable-pack` excluded entirely; same canonical weight only (no cross-weight, no whole-catalog $/gram leaderboard); price is the real price paid, never a discount %.
**Rationale**: **Deviation from the story's literal AC1** ("name token only where brand is null"): the strain/name token is included **always**. Brand+strainType+category+weight alone collides distinct strains (GG4 vs Blue Dream of the same brand) → FALSE disparities, violating AC1's own "different products → different key" clause and the integrity mandate. Differing store name formats only cause UNDER-matching (a safe false-negative) — the bias Open-Q2 chose. The matcher recomputes weight from the option LABEL rather than trusting stored `weightGrams` (some committed observations carry the pre-ADR-054 bad parse), so it is robust to stale data. AC5 audit proves excluded records have ZERO output effect via a full-vs-filtered equivalence assertion on real data.
**Consequences**: New `Disparity`/`DisparityStore` types (additive). New private route `/api/value/disparities`. No change to `Deal`, `filterActiveDeals`, `/api/data`, `_dutchie.ts`, `normalizeProduct.ts`, or the products write path (ADR-043/053 decoupling intact). Live run: 2461 records → 427 weight-mismatch excluded, 0 unmatched → **82 honest cross-store disparities** (e.g. OG Chem 3.5g $15–$30 across 4 stores). Downstream Phases 2–5 (Weedmaps wiring, accrual, legal gates → public SSR surfacing, monetization) remain out of scope and gated on non-engineering decisions — this dataset is private only, on the safe side of the ToS/legal line.
**Testing**: 50 new tests — `productMatchKey.test.ts` (19: key match/no-match/unmatchable, weight snapping), `crossStoreValue.test.ts` (10: spread math, special-vs-base, ≥2-store, per-store-cheapest, flag exclusion, same-weight-only, label unification), `crossStoreValue.audit.test.ts` (18: Phase 0 audit on committed data + parse-contract regression lock), `valueRoute.test.ts` (3). Full server suite **310 green**; client+server `npm run build` clean.

---

### ADR-063: Cross-store matcher honesty hardening — sold-out exclusion + strain-number preservation (post-merge code review)
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-29)
**Context**: A code review of the ADR-062 matcher (PR #43, different reviewer pass) surfaced two real defects that let the engine emit a fact that lies — the exact failure ADR-062's integrity mandate exists to prevent. **(1) Sold-out low price:** `buildMatchReport` computed `lowPrice`/`spread` from every priced option, never checking `quantityAvailable`. A reported `quantityAvailable: 0` (sold-out) offer could set the headline low and inflate the spread, advertising savings no shopper can act on. Live `products.json` carries **108 / 2838** latest-obs options at qty ≤ 0 (3.8%) — not hypothetical. **(2) Numeric-leading strain tokens dropped:** `strainToken` filtered every token matching `/^\d/`, intended for weight noise (`1g`, `3.5`, `2pk`) but also deleting genuine strain identifiers — `707 Headband` → `headband`, `9 Pound Hammer` → `pound hammer` — so a numbered strain over-matched its unnumbered sibling of the same brand into a FALSE disparity.
**Decision**: **(1)** Add honesty **Gate 4** in `crossStoreValue.ts`: skip an option when `quantityAvailable !== null && quantityAvailable <= 0`. `null` (unknown stock — scraper didn't report it) is kept; only a *reported* zero/negative is excluded, so the matcher compares buyable prices only. **(2)** Replace the blanket `/^\d/` filter with a `stripMeasurements(name)` pass that runs **before** `norm()` (critical: `norm()` strips the `.` in `3.5g`, splitting it into bare `3`+`5g` that would leak). It removes fractional weights (`1/8oz`), number+unit (`3.5g`,`2pk`,`10ct`,`28g`), and bare **decimal** weights (`3.5`) — but keeps bare **integers**, far more likely a strain id (`707`,`9`) than a unitless weight. A unitless integer weight at worst causes UNDER-matching (the safe bias, Open-Q2).
**Rationale**: Both fixes move strictly toward honesty/under-matching, never over-claiming. Gate 4 mirrors the existing price/weight gates (an unbuyable price is as untrustworthy as a mis-parsed weight). The measurement-strip-before-normalize ordering is the load-bearing detail — filtering digit-leading tokens *after* `norm()` cannot distinguish a split weight fragment from a strain number, which is why the original blunt filter existed.
**Consequences**: No type/route/signature change — purely internal to the two utils. Tightens live output to buyable, correctly-identified disparities. Findings #3 (whole-record exclusion when one option is `unparseable-weight`) and #4 (empty-token same-brand merge) were reviewed and **deliberately left** — both are the documented safe under-match direction and are audit-counted, not silent.
**Testing**: +6 tests — `crossStoreValue.test.ts` (3: sold-out excluded, cheapest-in-stock wins over sold-out, unknown-stock kept), `productMatchKey.test.ts` (3: leading-number strain kept, numbered≠unnumbered no-merge, weight/decimal/fraction noise still stripped). Full server suite **310 → 316 green**; client+server `npm run build` clean.

### ADR-064: Residential-IP commit-back for Weedmaps accrual (AI-search Phase 3)
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-29)
**Context**: Phase 3 = let CAP-6 observations accrue days→weeks so B1 "vs own rolling median" trendlines become real. Weedmaps is the richest source, but the scheduled path is walled: a `workflow_dispatch` re-probe (2026-06-29, run 28406848309) returned **HTTP 406 on 44/44** requests from the GitHub-Actions runner, while the identical desktop-UA fetch returns 200 from Erik's residential IP — the block is IP-driven, not header-driven (gate caveat #4 CONFIRMED). The durable product store is the committed `server/data/products.json` (Render disk is ephemeral), so accrual must be driven from a residential machine and committed back. The 406 confirmation + DO-NOT-ENABLE hardening had been written on the un-merged `feat/weedmaps-source-wiring` branch and was **lost in the PR#44 squash-merge** — master still carried the soft "re-probe to confirm" language until this change.
**Decision**: Add a committed PowerShell runner `scripts/scrape-weedmaps-local.ps1` that runs the **unchanged** `scrapeWeedmapsRun.ts` and mirrors the `scrape-weedmaps.yml` commit-back step locally: **hard-reset a dedicated worktree to `origin/master`** (guaranteed-clean base) → scrape → only if `products.json` changed, `git add` that ONE file, commit `…[skip ci]`, push `master`; a raced push is retried once on a rebased base, and a products.json rebase conflict is aborted + skipped (re-accrues next run). A one-time `scripts/setup-weedmaps-task.ps1` creates a **dedicated git worktree DETACHED at `origin/master`** (`~\Dev\Happy-weedmaps-ingest`), installs server deps there, and registers a daily `-StartWhenAvailable` Windows Scheduled Task. The GH-Actions `schedule:` stays disabled (re-enabling would 406 nightly); `workflow_dispatch` is kept only to re-probe the runner IP later.
**Rationale**: A **worktree** (not Erik's working checkout, not a fresh clone per run) is the load-bearing choice — server deps install once, and a 3:30am unattended run can never collide with whatever branch/edits Erik has in flight in the main checkout. **Detached** (not a `master` branch checkout) so the main checkout can still `git checkout master` (git forbids one branch in two worktrees). The **hard-reset-to-`origin/master` base** (vs `pull --rebase` in place) is the second load-bearing choice surfaced by the parallel code review: a rebase conflict on the shared `products.json` (the Dutchie cron also commits it) would otherwise leave the worktree mid-rebase, and `productsStore.readProducts` is fail-soft → it would parse the conflict-markered file as **empty and truncate the whole longitudinal dataset**. Resetting to origin every run makes that state unreachable; a skipped run just re-accrues. `[skip ci]` + products.json-only exactly matches the existing Dutchie/Actions commit-back, so CI never retriggers (Render still redeploys the fresh file). Fail-soft per store + commit-only-on-change means a 406/offline night is a clean no-op. Reusing the scrape core verbatim keeps the matcher's zero-change contract (ADR-053/062) intact.
**Consequences**: Accrual depends on Erik's PC being on near the trigger (a missed night runs at next wake; no data lost — next run appends). The task runs as Erik **only while logged on** (`-StartWhenAvailable` covers off/asleep; logged-*off* waits for next logon) — acceptable for a personal desktop, documented in the runbook. Pushes land directly on `master` from a personal machine (accepted; mirrors the bot's existing master commit-back). Lock + daily log + a `last-success.txt` heartbeat (touched when the scrape succeeds, so a stale timestamp flags a silent stall) live under the worktree's `.weedmaps-ingest\` and are never committed (runner only ever stages `products.json`). A runner preflight aborts loudly into its log if git/node/npx are off the Scheduled-Task PATH. Operator runbook: `docs/weedmaps-residential-ingest.md`. **Deferred:** real alerting/monitoring of weedmaps accrual freshness (the CI alert-gate deliberately excludes weedmaps) — heartbeat-file inspection is the v1 detection.
**Testing**: PowerShell scripts (ops glue, no unit suite) — both pass `[Parser]::ParseFile` syntax validation and are ASCII-only (avoids the PS-5.1 BOM-less ANSI mis-parse of non-ASCII punctuation, which also affects `powershell.exe -File` at Task-Scheduler runtime). Hardened against a 3-reviewer parallel code-review pass (blind/edge/acceptance): rebase-wedge→dataset-truncation (fixed via clean-base reset), stranded-commit, dead-PID lock, PATH-loss, worktree-owns-master, stray-dir, trailing-backslash. Runtime end-to-end (real residential-IP 200 + push) is Erik's post-merge verification. Server test suite unchanged (scrape core not modified — `git diff … -- server/` empty).

### ADR-065: Weedmaps fetch must use native `fetch`/undici, NOT axios (residential 406 root cause)
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-30)
**Context**: ADR-064's premise was "datacenter IPs 406, residential IPs 200" — true, but incomplete. The post-merge runtime verification (the step ADR-064 deferred to Erik) ran the real `scrape-weedmaps-local.ps1` from Erik's confirmed residential IP `50.125.219.81` and still got **HTTP 406 on all 44 requests, 0 observations, no commit**. Isolating on the same machine / same IP / same headers by HTTP client: `curl` (the scraper's exact UA124 + `Accept: text/html`) = **200**, Node native `fetch`/undici = **200**, but **`axios` = 406**. So the wall is the **axios request fingerprint** (its default header set / `Accept-Encoding` + Node `https`-module TLS + HTTP/1.1), not the IP and not the User-Agent. The Phase-2 gate that "proved residential = 200" used a curl/browser probe that never exercised axios, masking this.
**Decision**: Swap `defaultGet` in `server/scrapers/_weedmaps.ts` from `axios.get(...)` to native `fetch(url, { headers, redirect: 'follow', signal: AbortSignal.timeout(config.timeout) })`, returning the body as `{ data: await res.text() }` to satisfy the injectable `getFn` contract; a non-2xx throws so the caller's per-page `catch` logs it and that page degrades to `[]`. Drop the now-unused `import axios`. Nothing else changes.
**Rationale**: undici is proven 200 on the exact target connection where axios is 406-walled — the smallest change that unblocks accrual. Throw-on-`!res.ok` preserves the prior axios fail-soft (axios also threw on non-2xx), so the matcher/persist contract (ADR-053/062/064) is untouched; only the transport swaps. `AbortSignal.timeout` mirrors axios's `timeout`; `redirect: 'follow'` mirrors `maxRedirects`.
**Consequences**: This *does* break ADR-064's "scrape core byte-unchanged" claim — but only the transport line; the run/transform/persist path is identical. The injectable `getFn` seam means the 26 `_weedmaps` unit tests (which inject their own `getFn`) still pass unchanged; the real `defaultGet` fetch path has no unit test (network) and is covered by the live verification below. `caravan-cannabis-burlington` returns 0 products (pre-existing slug/redirect quirk, not the client) — logged, not fixed here.
**Testing**: Live run from the residential IP through the actual scraper code path (`npx tsx scripts/scrapeWeedmapsRun.ts`, fetch fix in place): **558 observations appended across 10/11 stores** (68/72/48/59/0/72/48/71/48/47/25), vs 0/11 with axios. `npx vitest run scrapers/_weedmaps` = 26/26 green. Data-file write reverted in the working checkout (the worktree runner does the real `[skip ci]` commit-back on master once this lands).

### ADR-066: Public-surfacing legal posture — Phase 4 legal gates CLOSED (founder determination)
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-30) — *working legal posture for this project; founder (Erik) determination, NOT independent counsel*
**Context**: AI-search Phase 4 (public SSR fact-pages) was held behind two open legal gates flagged across the corpus (`ai-search-data-strategy-investigation` Missing Evidence; SEO spec Phase 0b): (1) whether WA's WAC 314-55-155 cannabis-advertising warnings bind an unlicensed aggregator's public price pages, and (2) the ToS-redistribution / scraping-liability posture. Both were "outside engineering, await counsel."
**Decision**: Erik closes both as established project fact: **(1) WAC 314-55-155 binds *licensed retailers*, not unlicensed third-party aggregators** — LCB rules regulate licensees only, so the 4 mandatory warning statements do not attach to GmaS. Retained caveats: (a) displaying **licensee ads/content** could attach liability to *the licensee*; (b) WA **general advertising / UDAP** law still applies to GmaS → no health claims, nothing deceptive/unfair. **(2) ToS breach is a civil contract claim** (cease-and-desist, IP/account ban, injunctive relief, damages — rare for public data), **not criminal**: CFAA needs *unauthorized access* (bypassing logins/bot-detection), and *hiQ v. LinkedIn* held scraping **public** data is not CFAA "unauthorized access." GmaS scrapes public pages → risk is **civil contract, not criminal**.
**Rationale**: The plan's transform-don't-copy resolution already minimizes both surfaces — publishing **computed relational facts** (disparity %, delivered index, regional floor), not verbatim menus/inventory, is both lower redistribution risk and higher RAG information-gain. Under this determination that path is cleared to go public.
**Consequences**: **Phase 4 is no longer legally gated** — it remains *data-gated* on B1 accrual (multi-day/week price history; Phase 3 only began accruing 2026-06-30). Build/design constraints that flow from the retained caveats: do NOT transfer/host licensee ad creative in a way that imports licensee warning-obligations; keep all public copy free of health claims and UDAP-deceptive framing; surface computed facts, never verbatim menus. The SEO spec's Phase 0b legal flag is resolved by this same determination. This is a founder business-risk acceptance, revisitable if facts change (e.g. actual C&D received, or a move into licensee-ad monetization in Phase 5).
**Testing**: N/A (posture decision, no code). Gates marked RESOLVED in `ai-search-data-strategy-investigation.md` (Cross-Cutting Risk + Missing Evidence).

### ADR-067: Per-item sale-tag icons on deal cards (Erik's art, description-driven)
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-30) — **SHIPPED + LIVE** (PR #48 → master `691b654`, Render deploy confirmed)
**Context**: Deal cards rendered each store's "X% off ___" promo as text only. Erik wanted a small icon of *whatever item the deal names* beside the text so the feed is scannable, supplying his own illustrated "sale tag" art (`GmasINCOlist/Store sale tags/`, 29 AI-generated 1024² JPEGs). Two false starts were corrected by Erik: (1) the *deals* feed (`GET /api/data`, 52 promo banners) is the right source, NOT `/api/products` — he is tagging the blank in "X% off ___", not browsing individual SKUs; (2) a minimalist line-SVG redraw of the art was **rejected outright** ("I hate those single line icons… I do NOT want that slop") — his actual art must be used, resized not redrawn. The data carries no product-category field on a deal — only `type` (`happy_hour|daily`) + free-text `description` + `discountPct` — so item identity must be parsed from the description (Honest Math: emit an icon only for an item the text actually names).
**Decision**: New pure matcher `client/src/utils/dealIcons.ts` (`dealIcons(description) → DealIconName[]`) returns an ordered, de-duped list, one glyph per item in reading order: **store-wide is exclusive** (storewide/entire order/all cannabis); pre-roll **pack count** → `joint-single|double|triple`; concentrate **sub-forms**, most-specific-first → `diamond|dabs|concentrate`; a fixed-dollar price ("$50 ounce") appends `price-drop` (spend thresholds like "Greater Than $100" do NOT); a deal naming no recognizable product → the single `special-pricing` tag. False-friend brand/program names (`Join the Joint` membership, `Cookies` brand) and exclusion clauses (`(Excluding Capsules)`) are stripped before matching. Art: Erik's JPEGs resized via ImageMagick (`-fuzz 10% -trim -resize 128 -extent 144 white → webp`) into 14 assets at `client/src/assets/deal-icons/` (≤5 KB each), mapped by `dealIconAssets.ts` and rendered as `<img>` on a uniform white rounded tile (`.gma-deal-icon`, 28px) **to the right of the deal text** (`DealCard.tsx`).
**Rationale**: Description-parse is the only honest signal available, and a confident-match-or-nothing rule keeps it from fabricating categories. Using Erik's own raster art (resize, not redraw) was a hard product requirement; the uniform tile gives a multi-item row visual coherence without altering the art. The matcher is decoupled from the art map (`DealIconName` is a stable contract) so files can be swapped without touching logic. 14 line-SVG glyphs were also added to `Icon.tsx` during the rejected first pass and **kept (now unused)** at Erik's instruction.
**Consequences**: Icons reflect deal-banner text, so coverage tracks how stores phrase promos; multi-item lists yield multiple tags (e.g. "Concentrates, Flower, Prerolls and Vapes" → 4). Categories with art but rarely/never named in banners (chocolate edible — skipped for its gray-textured bg in favor of the orange gummy) simply don't render. Operator-tuned lists (`FALSE_FRIENDS`, exclusion regex, pack-count tokens) will need occasional additions as new promo phrasings appear, same philosophy as `CROSS_LOCATION_TAGS`/`DESCRIPTION_BLOCKLIST`.
**Testing**: 47 new tests — 36 `dealIcons.test.ts` (real deal strings: single/multi/dedupe/exclusion/false-friend/pack-count/sub-form/store-wide/dollar-vs-threshold/catch-all) + 2 `DealCard.test.tsx` (4-icon row + accessible label; single special-pricing) + existing. Full client suite (494) + client/server `npm run build` green. Live-verified by Erik on gmaslist.com.

### ADR-068: Store-card links → own-site URLs + residential link-liveness guard
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-30)
**Context**: Erik reported `dutchie.com/dispensary/the-vault-silvana` 404ing when he clicked a store card. Root cause: the public link is `Dispensary.url`, and for the 16 batch Dutchie stores it was hand-seeded (ADR-043, commit `3e9912f`) as `https://dutchie.com/dispensary/<our internal id>` — a *guess* that our readable id equals Dutchie's public slug. Nothing ever fetched it; the client's `isLinkableUrl` (`DealCard.tsx:67`) validates URL **syntax**, not liveness; and the deal scrape uses a different path (`/embedded-menu/<id>`), so a store can scrape fine yet ship a dead public link — they never cross-check. That is why it failed **silently**. INVESTIGATION TWIST: a residential-IP liveness sweep (below) showed **all 20 slugs actually return 200**, including the bare Dutchie ones — so the screenshot 404 was a **transient / location-state glitch** (the page had defaulted to "Pickup near Ferndale, MI"; Dutchie's SPA 404s when the location cookie conflicts with the store), NOT a wrong slug.
**Decision**: Two parts. (1) **Link pivot** — for the 9 batch stores with a confirmed, location-specific official own-site, repoint `url` from the guessed Dutchie slug to the store's own page (`the-vault-silvana`→thevaultcannabis.com/silvana/, `happy-time-mt-vernon`, `sweet-relief-mt-vernon`→mountvernonbud.com, `bud-hut-camano-island`, `local-roots-everett-128th`, `evolve-cannabis-bellingham`→evolvewashington.com, `starbuds-bellingham`, `salish-coast-cannabis`, `2020-solutions-pacific-highway`). All 9 verified live 200 (happy-time returns 429 bot-throttle even from residential — page is real, users unaffected). The 7 stores with only a generic multi-location homepage (cannazone×3, hangar-420×2, kushmart-north, 2020-solutions-north-bellingham) were **left on their working Dutchie slug** — own-site there is no more robust and loses per-location precision. This extends the pattern the 4 original stores already used (own-site links). `applyIngest` only mutates `deals`/`lastFetchedAt`/`stale`, never `url`, so the pivots survive prod refreshes. (2) **Liveness guard** — new `server/scripts/checkStoreLinks.ts`: pure, unit-tested `classifyProbe` (200–399=ok, 404/410=broken, 403/429/5xx/transport-error=unknown) + a `main` that GETs every store `url` and exits non-zero only on a DEFINITE break. Built to run from a **residential IP** (the ADR-064 Weedmaps local-runner / Scheduled-Task pattern) because Dutchie 403-walls datacenters exactly like Weedmaps — CI cannot tell a real 404 from the bot-wall, so this guard deliberately lives off-CI.
**Rationale**: Own-site links are immune to the Dutchie location-state 404 that actually bit the screenshot, satisfying Erik's rule ("pivot only where it won't break AND is more robust"). Keeping the 7 multi-location stores on Dutchie avoids trading a working precise link for a vaguer homepage. The guard mirrors `alertGate`'s "only alert on a real signal" philosophy so bot-walls/throttles never produce false alarms.
**Consequences**: 9 links now point at stores' own sites (homepage or location page, like the originals) rather than a Dutchie menu — a deliberate, consistent trade. The liveness guard was initially **run on demand** only; it is now scheduled nightly per **ADR-069**.
**Testing**: 5 new `checkStoreLinks.test.ts` (ok/broken/unknown/skip classification incl. bot-wall + transport-error false-positive guards). Server suite 362 green; client+server `npm run build` clean. Live sweep from Erik's residential IP: 20 stores → 0 broken, 1 unknown (happy-time 429), 19 ok.

---

### ADR-069: Schedule the link-liveness guard as a nightly residential task
**Status**: Accepted (2026-06-30)
**Context**: ADR-068 built `checkStoreLinks.ts` but left it run-on-demand — a dead public link could sit unnoticed for weeks between manual sweeps. Erik gave the go-ahead to wire it into a nightly residential Scheduled Task (the only place it can run honestly, since Dutchie 403-walls CI/datacenter IPs).
**Decision**: Mirror the ADR-064 Weedmaps local-runner pattern, **read-only** (no commit-back). `scripts/setup-check-store-links-task.ps1` (one-time) creates a dedicated git worktree `~\Dev\Happy-linkcheck` detached at `origin/master`, installs server deps, and registers a daily Scheduled Task at **04:30** (after the 03:30 Weedmaps task; own worktree, so no collision). `scripts/check-store-links-local.ps1` (the task target) hard-resets the worktree to `origin/master` (always runs the committed guard), then runs `checkStoreLinks.ts` against the **live** feed (`DATA_URL=https://gmaslist.com/api/data`) — the links users actually click. Alerting (the point of a nightly check): on a dead link it writes a persistent `LINK-ALERT.txt` (cleared on the next clean run) + best-effort Windows toast/balloon + logs the verdict; on a clean run it touches `last-success.txt` (stale = silent stall). It distinguishes a real 404/410 from a run that couldn't complete (live feed unreachable) so a transient blip isn't mistaken for a dead link. Runbook: `docs/store-link-liveness-check.md`.
**Rationale**: Reuses the proven, isolated worktree pattern so the nightly job never runs WIP or collides with dev. Read-only means none of the Weedmaps commit-back/rebase-race machinery is needed. The runner exits 0 even on a dead link (a failed Scheduled-Task result code is invisible day-to-day) — the durable signal is the alert file + toast, matching `alertGate`'s "alert only on a real signal" discipline.
**Consequences**: A dead store-card link now surfaces within ~24h instead of only at the next manual sweep. Costs a second worktree + `node_modules` (~disk) and a nightly run while logged on. NOT yet executed live — Erik must run `pwsh scripts/setup-check-store-links-task.ps1` from a residential network once + a manual runner pass to confirm.
**Testing**: No new unit tests (guard logic unchanged; `classifyProbe` already covered by ADR-068's 5 tests). PowerShell scripts are infra, verified by the manual runner pass during setup.

### ADR-070: Deal→SKU scope bridge (read-only third consumer joining the two decoupled pipelines)
**Status**: Accepted (2026-07-01) — code complete incl. AC7 (Erik chose option B: private route built); story at `review`
**Context**: A 2026-06-30 data-layer roundtable audit named the one relationship the model was missing: which in-store SKUs a free-text deal banner actually covers, and when. Deal banners live in `data.json` and `ProductRecord`s in `products.json` on separate cadences, deliberately decoupled (ADR-043/053). The honesty gate from `fix6-basePrice-verdict.md` is load-bearing: a banner `discountPct` is a flat storewide/brand promo rate with NO per-item signal, so a link must be EXPLANATORY + TEMPORAL ("this banner covers these SKUs, on these days/times"), never a savings computation.
**Decision**: New pure, read-only module `server/utils/dealScope.ts` — a THIRD consumer modelled exactly on `crossStoreValue.ts`: reads both files, mutates neither, counts everything it cannot place. `parseDealScope(deal)` infers exactly one `DealScope` from the description (`storewide` | `category`+optional `weightGrams` | `unsupported-category` | `brand` (deferred stub) | `unresolved`); ambiguity resolves to `unresolved`, NEVER silently to storewide, and an out-of-catalog cue (edibles/drinks/…) wins over a storewide word so it is never fanned across the flower menu. `buildDealScopeLinks(data, products)` joins each banner to the SAME store's priced SKUs (weight-qualified scopes drop untrustworthy-weight records via the shared `EXCLUDED_FLAGS`) and returns a `DealScopeReport` with per-bucket bookkeeping (AC5 — every deal lands in exactly one counter, zero-match distinct from unresolved). `isDealScopeLinkActive` answers "on a banner deal right now" (AC3). New types in `server/types/index.ts`; NO field added to `Deal`/`ProductRecord`/`ProductsFile`, NO write path touched.
**Rationale**: A read-only join preserves the ADR-043/053 decoupling while surfacing the missing relation. Weight snapping reuses `canonicalWeightGrams` (ADR-054 fractional-oz fix inherited); the honesty gates mirror `crossStoreValue.ts` so integrity stays single-sourced. **Reuse (code-review):** rather than duplicate the day/time-window logic, `isDealActive` in `filterActiveDeals.ts` was **exported** (param widened to `Pick<Deal,'daysValid'|'startTime'|'endTime'>`, so a full `Deal` still satisfies it and `filterActiveDeals` is byte-identical — the LOGIC is untouched, honoring AC6) and `isDealScopeLinkActive` now delegates to it, and `EXCLUDED_FLAGS` was exported from `crossStoreValue.ts` and imported — both eliminate copies that would otherwise drift.
**Consequences**: AC7 built (Erik, option B): a PRIVATE read-only `GET /api/value/deal-scope` (`dealScopeRoute` in `valueRoute.ts`, wired in `index.ts`) mirroring `/api/value/disparities` — additive, no public page/schema.org markup (Phase 4, legal-gated), no UI consumer. It reads the RAW deals (NOT `filterActiveDeals`) so each link keeps its temporal window for `isDealScopeLinkActive`; a pre-filtered view would erase out-of-window deals and defeat AC3. Fail-soft on both file reads (missing/malformed → zero links, never throws). Coverage tracks how stores phrase banners; the scope-cue lists (storewide/unsupported/brand) are operator-tuned like `CROSS_LOCATION_TAGS`. Two code-review fixes were folded in before commit: (1) the weight ternary now checks `isEighth` before `isOunce` so a spaced fractional-ounce eighth ("1/8 oz") resolves to 3.5g not 28g; (2) a dealScope-only guard treats "your cart"/"in your cart" as the shopping-cart sense (not Vaporizers), leaving `_weedmaps.ts`' identical cue untouched (it runs on structured category taxonomy where the false friend cannot appear).
**Testing**: `dealScope.test.ts` (17 — scope parser over real banner strings incl. the eighth/ounce + cart-guard regressions, temporal mirror) + `dealScopeLinks.test.ts` (7 — join proof) + `valueRoute.test.ts` (+3 — `GET /api/value/deal-scope` 200 + DealScopeReport shape + AC5 buckets + independence from /api/data). Full server suite **389 green**; `npm run build` (client `tsc -b && vite build` + server `tsc`) clean. Live sanity against committed data: 52 deals → 16 links, scope-class buckets sum to 52 (AC5 invariant holds on real data).
