### ADR-089: Dutchie product scrape walks the NUMBERED menu pages (kills the ~100-item cap) via in-page fetch, not scroll (dutchie-product-pagination)
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-07-18
**Context**: Diagnosis 2026-07-17 (lever A of "why so few honest price drops"): the product scrape captured only ~100 products/store, and that cap was the dominant reason `price-vs-own-median` suppressed most rows — SKUs churned in/out of the captured "first ~100" day-to-day, so few series ever cleared the `MIN_OBSERVED_DAYS = 7` distinct-day floor or the must-have-observation-today gate. Root cause, proven live on `happy-time-mt-vernon`: the scraper navigated to the embed **homepage**, whose category carousels each fire one `FilteredProducts` GraphQL op (perPage=25) that union to ~100. The real menu lives behind **NUMBERED pagination** (a zero-indexed `page` variable, `perPage` up to 100), **not infinite scroll** — so the former `scroll_after_wait` machinery was a complete no-op (scrolling a category listing grows the page by nothing and fires zero new ops). `happy-time`'s true menu is **1,372 products / 16 pages** across the five categories vs ~100 captured (<8%). A Task-0 live spike locked the mechanism: `FilteredProducts` is a persisted-query GET; the paginating variables are `page`/`perPage` (top-level) and `productsFilter.types` (category, values = `DEFAULT_PRODUCT_CATEGORIES`); page count is deterministic from `data.filteredProducts.queryInfo.totalPages`.
**Decision**: After the existing homepage capture, the scraper **walks every numbered page of every category** by lifting ONE captured `FilteredProducts` GET URL as a template and rewriting its `variables` (`page`, `perPage=100`, `productsFilter.types=[category]`) — preserving the persisted-query hash + `dispensaryId` verbatim, so a Dutchie hash rotation degrades softly. **The walk runs as same-origin in-page `fetch` inside the cleared browser session** (a new opt-in `paginate` capability on the vendored `scraper-svc`), because a bare Node/HTTP GET from the home residential IP is **403'd by a JS challenge** even with full browser headers (dev-start check, confirmed) — and the GET needs an `apollo-require-preflight` header to pass Apollo's CSRF guard. Each page's response is appended to the intercept set, so the existing TS `pickProducts` union+dedupe-by-id assembles the full menu with zero change to `transformProducts`. The dead `scroll_after_wait` flag is removed from `models.py`/`server.py`/`interceptor.py`/`scraperClient.ts`/`dutchieProductsRequest`. **Chosen mechanism = service-side in-page fetch (story Option 2)** because the preferred Node-side direct fetch (Option 1) failed its dev-start gate.
**Rationale**: Coverage-only — no fact-math, honesty-gate, or deals-path change (the specials `dutchieRequest` never carried `paginate`, so its navigate-wait-return timing is provably unchanged). Deterministic `totalPages`-driven page counts beat scrolling (which never worked) and beat blind walking (kept only as an empty-page terminator fallback when `totalPages` is absent). URL-lifting (vs hardcoding the hash/variables) is drift-resistant. In-page fetch reuses the browser's cleared session/cookies, immune to the IP/JS gating that blocks Node. The walk is best-effort per page: a failed page is skipped, never aborting the category or store, preserving the ADR-053/077 fail-soft + the first-page retry-on-empty budget.
**Consequences**: **Full-menu capture confirmed live** — `happy-time-mt-vernon` went from ~100 → **1,369 products** (Flower 227, Pre-Rolls 388, Vaporizers 520, Edible 154, Concentrate 80) in **17.8s**. The extra pages are sub-second GET replays, not 10–45s navigations, and the change *removes* the former 8s dead scroll-hold. **Measured full-run (AC-8, post-review-patch 2026-07-18): 17 stores in 5m 36s vs ~4m 15s before — +~80s/night for ~9× the data (23,470 observations vs ~2,000–2,900), leaving >54 min headroom before the 4:00 AM `GmaS Derive Facts` task.** **Review hardening (bmad-code-review, 3 layers, same day — 1 decision + 6 patches applied, 1 defer, 4 dismissed):** (1) HIGH/CONFIRMED — the template picker was first-match and homepage carousels can be **pinned to explicit `productIds`** (live-confirmed in the committed kushmart-north fixture): a pinned template silently collapses the whole walk to that ~25-product subset, and capture order is a network race. Fixed: selection prefers an unconstrained template + the rewrite clears `productIds`/`subcategories`/`strainTypes` to `[]`. Proof the finding was live, not theoretical: `local-roots-everett-128th` — "already full" at 532 pre-patch — yields **2,023** post-patch (its 532 was itself a scoped-template under-capture). (2) Detached the passive response listener before the walk (each walked page was captured twice, nondeterministically). (3) `clientTimeoutMs`: paginate requests get 180s (a 50s axios timeout could abort a slow-nav+walk that the service completes, discarding the capture and burning full retries to zero yield); deals stay 50s. (4) Blind-mode walk now tolerates one transient page failure (stops after 2 consecutive) + pauses on failure. (5) Every degrade path logs (`[paginate] …`) so a silent regression to page-0 coverage is visible in nightly logs. (6) Pydantic `ge=1` bounds; template-finder + cap-branch + blind-tolerance tests; tightened exception assertions; walk wrapped in try/except (degrades to page-0 capture, never 502). Deferred: `server.py` reads `interceptor._captured` (pre-existing `/discover` pattern). Post-patch: server **705** green, scraper-svc **17** green, `npm run build` clean. Expect downstream `price-vs-own-median` suppression (belowFloor + noObservationToday churn) to fall for every store as the substrate refills over the next ~7 days of accrual — the intended effect, not a code change. Files: `scraper-svc/scraper/{interceptor.py (pure `rewrite_filtered_products_url`/`read_total_pages`/`count_products` + `paginate_filtered_products` walk), models.py (`PaginateFilteredProducts`), api/server.py (`_find_filtered_products_template` + wiring)}`, `scraper-svc/tests/test_pagination.py` (NEW, 9 tests) + `pytest.ini`; `server/utils/scraperClient.ts` (`paginate` field), `server/scrapers/_dutchieProducts.ts` (`paginate` preset + category threading + comment correction) and `_dutchieProducts.test.ts`. Server **702** green, scraper-svc **9** green, real `npm run build` clean. Vendored `scraper-svc` is booted per-run by `scripts/scrape-dutchie-local.ps1` (local product accrual) and the Actions ingest — both boot from master, so the fix reaches production on push. Cross-ref ADR-053 (decoupled product scrape), ADR-077 (SQLite substrate), ADR-083 (confirmed-empty deals, untouched), and the fix6 gates in `priceVsOwnMedian.ts` this unblocks.

---

### ADR-090: Mobile Lighthouse quick wins — `display=optional` fonts, idle-deferred analytics, scoped pin sizing (lighthouse-perf)
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-07-18
**Context**: Two mobile Lighthouse reports of `gmaslist.com/` (LH 13.3.0, simulate) scored Performance 76 (age gate showing) / 64 (past the gate); A11y / Best-Practices / SEO all 100, so Performance is the only lever. Investigation (`_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/investigations/lighthouse-perf-investigation.md`) found the past-gate 64 was dragged by **CLS 0.254** and ~68 KiB render-time-unused analytics JS. LH's `layout-shifts` audit attributed the shift to **"Web font loaded" (0.232, ~91%)** and the location-bar pin **"media element lacking an explicit size" (0.021)** — NOT the deal grid resizing (its skeleton already reserves 100vh, ADR-081). The age gate hides the deal-grid reflow, which is why the gate-up run reads CLS 0.001. LCP ~4.9s / FCP ~3.1s (client-side render) is the *remaining* ceiling — the separate ADR-082 lever, out of scope here.
**Decision**: Three surgical edits (via bmad-quick-dev, 3-layer review). (1) **Fonts:** `display=swap`→`display=optional` on all three Google Fonts URLs — a late web font never swaps in, so no FOUT reflow; keeps the ADR-081 non-blocking `media="print" onload` pattern. Erik's explicit trade (AskUserQuestion): on a slow *first* visit the brand font may fall back until a cached load, chosen over metric-adjusted `@font-face` fallbacks (more work) or skipping fonts. (2) **Analytics:** keep the inline `dataLayer`/`gtag()` stub + `gtag('js')`/`gtag('config')` so early events queue, but append the ~166 KiB `gtag.js` inside `requestIdleCallback(loadGtag, { timeout: 3000 })` after `load` (`setTimeout(1500)` fallback for engines without rIC) — off the initial-load critical path. (3) **Location pin:** explicit `width/height:36px` **scoped to `.gma-location-bar__label .gma-location-pin`**, not the shared base class.
**Rationale**: `display=optional` is a one-line, robust CLS kill for the dominant cause vs the fiddly per-family `size-adjust` route. Deferring analytics to idle keeps measurement (queued events flush when the script arrives) while shedding the biggest unused-JS chunk; the `{ timeout: 3000 }` (review-caught) stops a never-idle main thread from stranding hits. The pin size is scoped because `.gma-location-pin` is reused at 16/22/36/56px (LocationInput/VehicleSelector/bars/onboarding) — a blanket rule (the first cut) would have forced the 16px and 22px pins to 36px (edge-case-hunter-caught regression).
**Consequences**: Shipped + **verified live** on `gmaslist.com` ~30s after PR #89 squash-merge (`b34886e`): served HTML carries `display=optional`, deferred `gtag.js` with `timeout: 3000`, bundle `index-C9w6Vdr7.js`. Build (`tsc -b && vite build`) green; 594 client + `shellRoute` tests pass; `</head>` snapshot anchor (ADR-082) and JSON-LD (indexHtml.test.ts) untouched. **CLS < 0.1 target still UNVERIFIED** — needs a mobile Lighthouse re-run on the *past-age-gate* flow (the gate-up run can't show the 0.254). Known residual accepted: deferring GA drops pageviews for very-fast bounces (inherent to the deferral); `display=optional` first-visit fallback font; gtag adblock/onerror loss (pre-existing under the old eager `async` tag). Reusable: LH attributes deal-grid CLS to its *causes* (fonts/unsized media above it), not the grid — a `min-height` on the shifted node is the wrong fix; the real levers sit above it. Cross-ref ADR-081 (non-blocking fonts + skeleton), ADR-082 (snapshot injection / the LCP ceiling this does NOT address).

---

### ADR-091: Reserve the deal-card gas-line slot to kill the residual past-gate CLS (lighthouse-perf follow-up)
**Status**: Superseded by ADR-092 (gas-line diagnosis was wrong; the change is live but did not move CLS)
**Date**: 2026-07-19
**Context**: The first past-age-gate mobile Lighthouse re-run after ADR-090 (`gmaslist.com-203.json`, LH 13.3.0, simulate, moto g power) **confirmed the font fix worked** — the "Web font loaded" culprit is gone from the shift causes and CLS dropped 0.254 → **0.162**. But 0.162 is still over the 0.1 target, and the report now names a single, unambiguous culprit: `li > article.gma-card > div.gma-deal-grid > div.gma-deal-block` at 100% of the shift (score 0.162, `top:673`). The deal grid reflows *downward* after first paint because the card's **gas line** (`.gma-gas-line`, a 40px icon + cost) is rendered only when `gasCostText !== null`; when a round-trip cost resolves a beat after the card mounts (vehicle/fuel-price async), the line appears and shoves the whole deal grid down. This is exactly the deal-grid space-reservation that ADR-090 had ruled out — masked then by the larger font shift, it's the whole residual now. Perf past-gate 79; LCP 3.3s (the ADR-082 ceiling, unchanged).
**Decision**: Reserve the gas-line slot so a late-resolving cost fills text into an already-sized box instead of inserting a new one. In `DealCard.tsx` the render gate changes from `gasCostText !== null` to **`distanceText !== null`**: whenever the store has a (computable) distance the `.gma-gas-line` span renders with its 40px pump icon, and the `$` figure fills in only once `gasCostText` resolves. `.gma-gas-line` gets `min-height: 40px` so the slot holds even before the icon paints. A store with **no** distance (no trip to price — DealFeed nulls distance without a location or store coords) still renders no gas line at all: nothing there ever shifts, so nothing to reserve.
**Rationale**: Gating on `distanceText` (not literally "always") ties the reserved slot to the same signal that governs whether a gas cost can ever exist, so it reserves height exactly for the cards that will show a cost — fixing the measured shift — without reserving on no-distance cards. Icon dominates the line height (40px) in both the with-cost and without-cost states, so filling the text in causes no height change. Erik's explicit AskUserQuestion trade: in the **location-set-but-no-vehicle** state (gas is null for every card until a vehicle is picked) the reserved slot shows a **bare pump icon with no price** on every card. Chosen over hiding the icon until a cost exists (which reserves the same height with no bare icon) — Erik wanted the icon visibly present. That state causes no CLS on its own (gas stays null, nothing shifts); the bare icon is a pure cosmetic cost accepted for the always-present-icon look. Honest Math preserved: no `$` figure renders until the cost is real.
**Consequences**: `DealCard.tsx` gate + `.gma-gas-line { min-height: 40px }`; three tests updated to the new behavior (`DealCard` "reserves the gas-line slot…", `App.test` recalc-gas, `DealFeed` no-mpg) — they now assert the slot renders with an icon but **no `$`** when a distance exists and gas is null; the two genuine no-distance tests (`DealCard` ADR-043, `DealFeed` undistanced-store) stay `.gma-gas-line` null and were untouched. 594 client tests green; `tsc -b && vite build` green (bundle `index-ygXDC-xI.js`). **Verification loop still open until a *third* past-gate LH re-run** confirms CLS < 0.1 with the deal-block culprit gone. Cross-ref ADR-090 (the font/analytics/pin wins this follows), ADR-040 (gas line as its own figure), CAP-4 (national-MPG default removed → the no-vehicle null-gas state).

---

### ADR-092: Snapshot the "Real price drops" into `window.__GMA_DROPS__` — the actual residual-CLS fix (supersedes ADR-091's diagnosis)
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-07-19
**Context**: A second past-gate mobile Lighthouse re-run *after* ADR-091 shipped (`gmaslist.com-205.json`, LH 13.3.0, simulate, moto g power) showed CLS essentially **unchanged: 0.162 → 0.148**, same `div.gma-deal-block` culprit, and this time **no cause attributed** (no font, no unsized media). So ADR-091's gas-line hypothesis was **wrong** — reserving the gas slot did not move CLS. Re-traced from the code with the "no cause = a DOM/content insertion" clue: `DealFeed` renders the feed as soon as `useDeals` resolves, but `useValueDrops` (the "Real price drops" data) is an **ungated `fetch`-on-mount** that starts `[]` and populates in a `useEffect` *after* first paint. When it resolves, the feed re-renders: `ValueDropStrip`s expand the cards that have drops, and — decisively — a nearer **drop-only** store can slot in at the top (the feed re-sorts by distance), shoving the first card's deal grid down. The live endpoint was serving **128 honest-drop rows across 8 stores** at diagnosis time, so this fires on every real load. That post-paint reflow is the sole remaining past-gate CLS once fonts were fixed (ADR-090).
**Decision**: Extend the existing ADR-082 snapshot so the drops are present on the FIRST render — no second pass, no reflow. Server (`shellRoute.ts`) injects a second inline global `window.__GMA_DROPS__ = <price-vs-own-median envelope>` alongside `window.__GMA_DATA__`, via a new shared `readPriceVsOwnMedian()` reader exported from `valueRoute.ts` (same file the route already serves, so snapshot ≡ fetch payload). Both globals share one `<script>`, both run through the same `<`/U+2028/U+2029 escaper (drops carry scraped product names — hostile-by-premise), and both are gated on `buildApiData()` succeeding (a broken `data.json` injects neither; each hook's fetch is the fallback). Client (`useValueDrops.ts`) gains a synchronous `readSnapshot()` mirroring `useDeals`: init state from `window.__GMA_DROPS__` when present (even `[]` → a valid empty snapshot, skip the fetch), else `[]` + fetch. A `hasSnapshot` ref decides once at mount whether to fetch.
**Rationale**: The snapshot route is the established, correct tool (ADR-082) — it kills the round trip AND the reflow in one move, and preserves the fail-soft/additive posture (`selectDrops` degrades any bad shape to `[]`, so a corrupt snapshot looks exactly like no drops). Rejected alternatives (AskUserQuestion): **gate first paint on the drops fetch** (simplest, but delays the feed and couples the "drops never block the feed" design to the critical path) and **reserve space for incoming strips/cards** (fragile — drop heights are dynamic and drop-only cards insert by distance, so exact reservation is unreliable). Coupling the drops snapshot to `buildApiData()` success is deliberate: it keeps the "no `__GMA_DATA__` when data is broken" contract holding for drops too, at the cost that a (rare) data.json-broken-but-drops-fine load loses the drops snapshot and fetches instead — negligible.
**Consequences**: `valueRoute.ts` (+`readPriceVsOwnMedian` export, route delegates to it), `shellRoute.ts` (+drops injection, `escapeForScript` helper factored from the three inline replaces), `useValueDrops.ts` (+snapshot init/skip-fetch). Tests: `shellRoute.test.ts` asserts the drops global injects once as a valid envelope and that a broken `data.json` drops BOTH globals; `useValueDrops.test.ts` +3 (hydrate-from-snapshot/no-fetch, empty-snapshot-authoritative, absent→fetch). 597 client + 705 server source tests green; full `npm run build` green (client `index-JuS1hBtN.js`, server `tsc`). Adds ~8 KiB gzipped inline JSON to each shell load (128 rows) — small next to the existing `__GMA_DATA__` payload, and revalidated `no-cache` like it. **VERIFIED — CLS goal MET:** the third past-gate mobile LH run post-deploy (`gmaslist.com-2028l.json`, deal-feed nodes present in the report) reads **CLS 0 / score 1.0, zero layout shifts** — the `gma-deal-block` culprit is gone. Full arc: 0.254 (ADR-090 baseline) → 0.162 (fonts fixed) → 0.148 (gas-line no-op) → **0** (drops snapshot). Perf 86, A11y/BP/SEO/agentic-browsing all 100; LCP 3.3s stays the ceiling (ADR-082 lever, untouched). **ADR-091's gas-line change stays live but achieved nothing for CLS** — its only observable effect is the bare-pump-icon no-vehicle state; a revert is offered separately. Cross-ref ADR-082 (the snapshot infra extended here), ADR-088 (ValueDropStrip / drops-in-card), ADR-090/091 (the CLS thread).

---

### ADR-093: Externalize the deal/location icon art out of the critical JS bundle (the LCP lever, minus SSR)
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-07-19
**Context**: With CLS at 0 (ADR-092), the sole remaining past-gate mobile score gap is **LCP 3.3s (Perf 86)**. Traced from `gmaslist.com-2028l.json`: TTFB is **50 ms**, main-thread work **0.8 s**, TBT **50 ms** — all fine — but **FCP is 3.1 s** and LCP rides 0.2 s behind it. So the whole gap is the browser waiting for the entry JS to download + execute before the empty `#root` paints anything (a client-rendered SPA has no server HTML to paint). gtag was *already* deferred to idle (`index.html`, prior spec-perf work), so it is off the critical path. The obvious lever — **SSR the feed** — is blocked twice over: (1) the age gate reads `gma_age_confirmed` from `localStorage`, which the server can't see, so `renderToString(<App/>)` emits the *age gate*, not the feed — zero past-gate LCP benefit and a guaranteed hydration mismatch; (2) emitting human-readable deal HTML server-side is the **deferred Phase-0b legal decision** (see `shellRoute.ts` header + spec-seo). So SSR is off the table without Erik's legal sign-off. Measuring the bundle instead: **40 of Erik's webp icons were inlined as base64 = 127 KB uncompressed = 32.6% of the 391 KB entry chunk**, sitting squarely on the FCP/LCP path — and because base64 image bytes are high-entropy, they gzip poorly and bloat the *transfer* size disproportionately.
**Decision**: Extend the existing `vite.config.ts` `assetsInlineLimit` predicate — which already externalizes `age-icons` for this exact reason — to also return `false` for `deal-icons` and `location-icons` (regex `/[\\/](age-icons|deal-icons|location-icons)[\\/]/`). All of Erik's art now ships as separate hashed, cacheable files; only genuinely tiny non-art assets keep Vite's default inlining.
**Rationale**: Cheapest safe win with the biggest measured payoff. The deal icons already render with fixed `width={28} height={28}` (BOGO 28, gas-pump 40) **and** `loading="lazy" decoding="async"` (`DealCard.tsx`) — so (a) externalizing them cannot reintroduce CLS (dimensions are reserved; ADR-090/091/092 kept CLS 0), and (b) `loading="lazy"` was previously **dead** on an inlined data-URI — now it's live, so only above-the-fold icons load on first paint and the rest defer entirely. Rejected: true SSR (legally gated + gate-defeated, above); manual `React.lazy` code-splitting of the entry (deps are only React+ReactDOM — no heavy libs to split; first-paint components are nearly the whole app, so splits would trim <15 KB); an icon sprite sheet (large refactor for marginal gain over externalized+lazy files).
**Consequences**: One-line predicate change in `client/vite.config.ts` (+comment); no source/logic change, so all **597 client tests pass** unchanged and both production builds are green (`npm run build --prefix client` and `--prefix server`). Entry chunk **391 KB → 265 KB uncompressed**; gzip **transfer ~180 KB → 84.8 KB (≈ −53%)** — the base64 removed gzips far worse than its raw share. 40 icons become 2–5 KB standalone lazy/cacheable requests over HTTP/2. **VERIFIED post-deploy:** past-gate mobile LH re-run against the new bundle (`gmaslist.com-202607.json`, `index-CQ_8ioq8.js`, fetched 2026-07-19T17:44Z) reads **Performance 90 (was 86), FCP 3.1→2.8s, LCP 3.3→3.0s, Speed Index 3.1→2.8s, CLS 0 held, TBT 40ms**; A11y/BP/SEO/agentic-browsing all still 100. **Perf goal cleared 90.** **The LCP ceiling is not fully removed** — a client-rendered SPA still paints nothing until React mounts (~0.5s of LCP headroom left); closing the rest requires the Phase-0b SSR legal decision. Cross-ref ADR-082 (snapshot infra), ADR-090/091/092 (the CLS thread this continues), spec-seo Phase 0b (the deferred SSR half).

---

### ADR-094: Fix the mobile horizontal-overflow ("opens zoomed into the left third") — grid-blowout + nowrap min-content, plus the first width breakpoint
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-07-19
**Context**: On a real phone the site opened with only the left ~60% visible, requiring a manual pinch-zoom-out. The `<meta name="viewport">` tag was already correct (`width=device-width, initial-scale=1.0`), so this was **horizontal overflow**, not a viewport bug: some element rendered wider than the screen, so the browser parked the user at the left edge at 1:1 and let the rest spill right. Diagnosed empirically in-browser at a simulated 390px layout (desktop Chrome won't resize a window below ~808px, so the feed was cloned into a fixed-390px box and measured by `scrollWidth`/min-content). The feed `<section>` (`DealFeed.tsx` `feedStyle`) was `display: grid` with **no explicit columns** → items land in a single *implicit `auto` track*, and an `auto` track can never shrink below its widest child's **min-content**. Peeling the layers (each fix revealed the next-widest driver): (1) `.gma-chips` category bar, min-content 1039px; (2) the store cards, floored at ~635px. The card floor traced to the **"Real price drops" strip** (`.gma-value-drops`, 601px), whose row product name is `white-space: nowrap` + ellipsis — and **nowrap text contributes its FULL width to min-content sizing**; the ellipsis only clips once a width is imposed. `.gma-value-drops__identity` set `min-width: 0`, but its grid/flex **ancestors** (`__row`, `__list`, the strip) defaulted to `min-width: auto`, so the truncation never propagated and the name floored the card. The header (26px name + address/distance) was a red herring — its true isolated min-content is ~136px; it only *looked* like a driver because flex `align-items: stretch` ties siblings to the parent width (a measurement artifact that repeatedly mis-pointed the min-content chain-tracer).
**Decision**: Six CSS/style changes, verified by injecting the exact final CSS on the live page and forcing a 390px render (result: `leakCount: 0` — nothing overflows except the chip bar, which correctly scrolls inside its own `overflow-x: auto` container — and the product name truncates with an ellipsis): (1) `feedStyle` → `grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr)` (lets the column shrink below content-min); (2) `.gma-chips { min-width: 0 }` (so its `overflow-x: auto` engages instead of stretching the feed); (3) `.gma-value-drops__figure` → `flex: 0 1 auto; min-width: 0` (was `flex: none`); (4) `.gma-value-drops__support` / `__headline` → drop `white-space: nowrap` so the "$X vs $Y usual" line wraps when squeezed; (5) **`min-width: 0` chained down `.gma-value-drops` → `__list` → `__row`** — the keystone that makes the identity's existing `min-width: 0`/ellipsis actually engage; (6) the project's **first width breakpoint**, `@media (max-width: 480px)`, stacking the deal-card header vertically (`.gma-dealcard__head` → `flex-direction: column`; `__head-right` → `max-width: 100%`, left-aligned). Erik approved the two look-affecting calls (wrapped figures + stacked header) with "we may tweak it later."
**Rationale**: `minmax(0, 1fr)` + a `min-width: 0` chain is the canonical cure for grid/flex blowout and keeps the layout fluid (no clipping, content genuinely reflows) — chosen over the blunt `html, body { overflow-x: hidden }` safety net, which only *masks* the spill and would hide the real overflow. The header stack (#6) turned out not to be needed for the overflow fix (the header fits), but is a reasonable mobile improvement and was kept per Erik's go-ahead. Dropping `nowrap` is safe on desktop — text only wraps when space is actually tight.
**Consequences**: `client/src/components/DealFeed.tsx` (feedStyle) + `client/src/styles/components.css` (5 rules incl. the new media query). Pure CSS/style-object change, no logic — `npm run build` green (client CSS 29.18 KB, entry JS 265 KB unchanged). Introduces the first non-preference `@media` breakpoint into a previously fully-fluid stylesheet — future card work should assume a 480px mobile boundary exists. **Not yet pushed/deployed** (awaiting Erik's go-ahead); verified only via live-DOM injection + local build, not yet on a real device against prod. Follow-up if desired: confirm on an actual phone post-deploy, and the "tweak later" pass on the wrapped figures / stacked header. Cross-ref ADR-088 (ValueDropStrip/drops-in-card, the strip fixed here), ADR-057 (user-relative distance = the header distance pill), Technical Constraints "must be mobile-responsive."

### ADR-095: Data-acquisition strategy — build a better information platform, not buy a licensed feed
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-07-21
**Context**: All deal + pricing data rides on undocumented private APIs, defeated anti-bot protection, and pinned selectors (Dutchie GraphQL 403 JS-wall via hash-lifting/apollo-preflight spoof; Vercel "Security Checkpoint" 429s beaten with browser-tier raw_html; Weedmaps 406-walls routed through a residential runner). This raised the question: is there a legitimate paywall we can pay to cross? The investigation (`_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/investigations/licensed-data-source-options-2026-07-21.md`) surveyed three paid paths: (1) Dutchie/Weedmaps official APIs — DO NOT FIT (each requires the individual *store owner* to authorize us as an approved partner; non-starter for a third party surfacing 21 stores we don't own); (2) CannMenus licensed aggregator — best nominal fit; **[corrected 2026-07-24: pricing is public and self-serve, not enterprise-gated — API Standalone $500/mo, 3 states, 10k req/state/mo, self-serve signup; WA fits the base tier]**; (3) Apify managed scrapers — cheaper, self-serve, but same ToS posture (buys less fragility, not legitimacy). Reframing the survey: the paid options are not better *data*, they are **risk transfer** — you pay someone else to hold the ToS/legal bag and the anti-bot maintenance pager. Two hidden weaknesses in that trade: CannMenus's "removes ToS risk" claim is **unverified** (aggregators very likely scrape Dutchie/Weedmaps/Leafly/Jane themselves, so the risk is laundered, not removed), and a licensed feed gives raw menus only — it cannot replace the proprietary derivation engine (price-vs-own-median, regional floors, brand personas, disparity rollups) that is the actual moat.
**Decision**: Do **not** buy a licensed/managed feed. Build a better information platform on the acquisition we already control, and treat the data source as a **pluggable commodity beneath the derivation engine** — the engine, not the scrape, is the defensible product. Keep diversifying acquisition (own-site scrapers already prove no single source is load-bearing) so no single cease-and-desist is fatal. Close the four self-inflicted gaps that make the current path weaker than it needs to be, as shippable work (Layer 2): (1) ship the staleness gate at `buildMatchReport` (already promoted to a named story in the Epic-2 retro) so a stale prior-day price can't set a floor/disparity; (2) get WebFetch out of the honesty-critical path (it hallucinated a 15%-off deal not in the DOM) — verify every claimed offer against the real rendered DOM; (3) add a real dead-man's-switch on ingest so a silent outage (stopped Scheduled Task looking like fresh data) alerts instead of masquerading; (4) formally decouple the acquisition layer from the derivation engine so sources become interchangeable inputs. CannMenus pricing inquiry deferred (not pursuing now). **Update 2026-07-24: no inquiry needed — pricing is public and self-serve ($500/mo base for WA), so we scouted it without surfacing the project. Knowing the number strengthens this ADR rather than reopening it: $500/mo (~$6k/yr, ~$5.1k annual-prepay) to launder an unverified ToS risk, against a $0-Render project at ~18 users/28d, is clearly not worth it now. CannMenus is shelved as a known-price, pluggable option to revisit only when traffic/revenue justifies it. Drafted inquiry email deleted — premise dead.**
**Rationale**: For a solo/$0-Render project, enterprise pricing to *launder* a ToS risk that may not actually disappear is a poor trade — especially when the moat (derived relational facts) is untouched by which source feeds it. Closing the four gaps costs nothing and removes the genuine weaknesses (honesty holes, silent-failure fragility, single-C&D kill-switch) without surrendering coverage control (we target exactly our stores; an aggregator covers what it covers) or the value layer. Chosen over CannMenus (cost + unverified legitimacy), Apify (no legitimacy gain), and official APIs (structurally ineligible as non-owner). Making the source pluggable also preserves optionality: a future CannMenus/Apify trial could feed the *same* engine and be A/B'd against our scraper without rework.
**Consequences**: No spend; acquisition stays self-hosted and multi-source. The legal kill-switch risk is acknowledged and mitigated by diversification rather than eliminated — a C&D remains possible but is no longer single-path-fatal. Four gaps become Layer-2 stories (staleness gate is half-specced from the Epic-2 retro). The pluggable-source decoupling (#4) is the "stronger than the options" architectural move: it turns paid feeds into optional commodity inputs to something they can't replicate. Cross-ref the investigation doc (above), ADR-034 (push-ingest, the sole deals path), ADR-084/085/086 (accrual/disparity facts the staleness gate protects), ADR-089 (full-menu capture). Superseded prior consideration: ADR-033 paid-Docker scraper (already superseded; this ADR reaffirms no paid data path).
**Testing**: Strategic decision — no code artifact. Verification is the Layer-2 stories' own acceptance criteria (staleness gate behavior, DOM-verified honesty, dead-man's-switch alerting, source-decoupling seam).

### ADR-096: Oracle freshness gate (Gate 6) at `buildMatchReport` — a stale prior-day price can no longer set a cross-store low or floor
**Status**: Accepted (3-layer code review passed 2026-07-21; not yet pushed)
**Date**: 2026-07-21
**Context**: Layer-2 gap #1 of ADR-095, and the single named story promoted from the epic-derivation-2 retro (action item 3), absorbing the epic-1 retro's mt-vernon-persistent-stale + recurring-Dutchie-outage items. `buildMatchReport` (`server/utils/crossStoreValue.ts`) read each product's latest observation (`rec.history.at(-1)`) with **no freshness check**, so a store that silently stopped being scraped — an outage, or a SKU that dropped off the menu — contributed a *prior-day* price to the disparity oracle. That stale price could set the cross-store `lowPrice`, inflate `spread`, or become a regional floor, and because every disparity-derived fact (`cheapest-delivered` ADR-085, `regional-price-floor` ADR-086, `disparity-rollups` ADR-084) reads `report.disparities`, the defect was engine-wide. Grounding (committed `products.db`, anchor day 2026-07-06): even with every store scraped on the max day, **1,228 of 5,219 records (23.5%) had a latest observation staler than 1 day** — structural, not just outage stores. It catches a *different* population than the existing sold-out Gate 4: a silently-vanished SKU carries no `quantityAvailable<=0` signal.
**Decision**: Add **Gate 6 (freshness)** inside `buildMatchReport`. A record whose latest-observation day is `< anchor − maxLagDays` is excluded and counted (`staleRecords`), surfaced in the disparities envelope's `excluded[]` as a new `stale` reason + `coverage.staleRecords` — same excluded-and-counted discipline as gates 1–5, never silent. Ratified constants (Erik delegated 2026-07-21): **anchor = global max observed day in the dataset** (`globalMaxObservedDay`), **`FRESHNESS_MAX_LAG_DAYS = 1`**, **per-record** granularity. `buildMatchReport(file, opts?)` gains optional `freshnessAnchor`/`maxLagDays`; when the anchor is omitted it **self-derives** the global max day, so the gate is un-disableable even for a one-arg caller. The runner passes the anchor explicitly for clarity. Siblings inherit the gate for free (no edits); `price-vs-own-median` is untouched (own windowed reader that already gates on distinct calendar days).
**Rationale**: The anchor is **data-derived, not wall-clock `today`** — this is seam-proof (a derive just after 00:00 UTC cannot flag the whole fleet stale, the footgun the 2.1 retro flagged) and fleet-relative, so a store lagging the fleet is caught while `extraction-health`'s wall-clock `today` was rejected. Global (not per-store) is required: a per-store anchor can never flag a persistently-stale store (its own latest is trivially its own max). `maxLagDays=1` tolerates ordinary one-day scrape jitter (07-05 kept) while excluding multi-day staleness (07-04 dropped). Self-deriving default beats the originally-planned "runner must remember to pass the anchor" — impossible to leave off — and, because every existing fixture is single-day, it is a **no-op on all prior tests** (smallest blast radius; no assertion churn). An unparseable `observedAt` is treated as stale (a price that can't be proven fresh must not set a headline).
**Consequences**: `server/utils/crossStoreValue.ts` (gate + `FRESHNESS_MAX_LAG_DAYS`/`globalMaxObservedDay`/`observedDay`/`subtractDaysUTC`/`MatchReportOptions`/`staleRecords`), `server/scripts/deriveFactsRun.ts` (explicit anchor + `stale` envelope reason/coverage), `server/routes/valueRoute.ts` (`staleRecords: 0` in the empty-fallback envelope). Additive/honesty-only — no route/client/`data.json`/`Deal` change (ADR-043/053 preserved); FR16 type-gate holds (no price-pair/discount/potency reachable). +11 tests (10 unit incl. UTC-seam non-misfire + explicit anchor + unparseable-date, 1 runner exclusion+downstream-inheritance); two literal `MatchReport` builders gained `staleRecords: 0`. **715 server tests green**; `npm run build` clean. Live proof (committed DB): **228→134 disparities, 1,228 stale (23.5%), 94 disparities removed** as resting on a stale price. ⚠️ Those figures are an **upper bound** — the committed snapshot predates ADR-089 full-menu capture (top-100 carousel era → poor day-to-day coverage overstates staleness); on the live home DB the steady-state stale fraction and disparity reduction will be materially smaller, and the store-level fall-out case (mt-vernon-style) will appear there. **Not yet pushed/deployed** — awaiting Erik's go-ahead; re-serves only after a home `derive-facts-local` republishes the derived artifacts. Cross-ref ADR-095 (Layer-2 gap #1), ADR-084/085/086 (the inheriting siblings), ADR-083 (confirmed-empty ingest, adjacent freshness mechanism).
**Review hardening (2026-07-21)**: 3-layer adversarial `bmad-code-review` (Blind Hunter / Edge Case Hunter / Acceptance Auditor, all Opus 4.8) confirmed AC1/3/4/5/6 and every preserved invariant, then surfaced an input-robustness cluster on the new date helpers (none live-reachable today — the scraper always stamps valid UTC ISO — but this is a load-bearing honesty function). Applied 1 decision + 3 patches: (1) **future-day anchor guard** — `globalMaxObservedDay(file, today=UTC-now)` now ignores any observation day `> today`, so one clock-skewed/future record can no longer poison the anchor high and silently blackout the whole fleet (the zero-collapse guard would NOT catch that, since `totalRecords` stays pre-gate); upper-bound only, AC6 lower-seam behavior untouched (Erik's call over clamp-to-today). (2) **`observedDay` rejects calendar-invalid dates** (`2026-13-45` etc.) by round-tripping through a real UTC `Date`, closing both a `toISOString()` RangeError crash path and a phantom-low read. (3) **option validation** — `maxLagDays` coerced to `Math.max(0, Math.trunc(x))` (NaN→default; negative no longer inverts the gate), and an unparseable explicit `freshnessAnchor` falls back to the self-derived anchor instead of throwing. +6 hardening tests (721 server tests green; build clean). Four findings deferred to `deferred-work.md`: AC2 reconciliation not literally exact (pre-existing no-history/unpriced remainder), **AC7 live-home-DB proof still owed** (store-level mt-vernon fall-out unproven until the next home derive), whole-DB-unparseable disables the gate (pathological), non-UTC-offset day mis-bucketing (scraper stamps UTC).

### ADR-097: Crawler-visible homepage HTML (Phase 0a-HTML) + AgeGate/onboarding content mounting (Phase 0b) — the SEO ~90% unlock; Phase 0b legal go-ahead released
**Status**: Accepted (implemented + full suites/build green 2026-07-23; awaiting push/deploy)
**Date**: 2026-07-23
**Context**: The homepage `/` served an empty SPA shell — `curl -A GPTBot/1.0 https://gmaslist.com/` returned `<body><div id="root"></div></body>`, so non-JS AI crawlers (GPTBot, PerplexityBot, OAI-/Claude-SearchBot) saw title + meta + thin JSON-LD only, zero deals. This was deliberate (`shellRoute.ts:29-32`): the crawler-visible half of SEO-plan Phase 0a was deferred behind the Phase 0b legal question (WAC 314-55-155 — may deal content exist in markup while an age gate blocks it?). ADR-052 split 0a/0b by risk class; ADR-066 established the statute binds *licensed retailers*, not an unlicensed aggregator (and `/compare` + `/api/data` already publish deal-derived content un-gated). Two React content gates each withheld deals from the rendered DOM: `AgeGate` (`if (isIn) return children` else nothing) and — discovered during story-creation code trace — `LocationOnboarding` (rendered *instead of* `DealFeed` until `onboarded`), so a JS crawler (Googlebot) past the age gate still landed on the onboarding prompt with the feed absent.
**Decision**: Erik **released the Phase 0b legal go-ahead 2026-07-23** on the ADR-066 basis, scope = 0a-HTML + 0b together (the plan's "actual unlock"). Two coupled changes: **(0a-HTML)** `shellRoute.ts` now also server-renders escaped, human-readable deal/store HTML (`server/utils/renderShellBody.ts`) **inside `<div id="root">`** — the crawler-only sink React's `createRoot` (not `hydrateRoot`) wipes on hydration, so humans never see un-gated deal content in steady state; built from the SAME `buildApiData()` call that feeds `window.__GMA_DATA__` (one derivation, no new data path/exposure), injected via a **function** replacement (deal text routinely contains `$`, and a string replacement expands `$`-patterns — the ADR-082 lesson, now applied to the body path too). A static WA 21+/health line renders into the body (Erik-ratified, AC8). **(0b)** `AgeGate.tsx` and `App.tsx` stop withholding content: both now **always mount** their children and mark them **`inert` + `aria-hidden`** (React 19.2 native `inert`) while gated, with the existing opaque full-bleed overlays layered on top. A human sees and does exactly what they did before (zero visual/behavioral change — the overlays already fully occlude); a JS crawler reads the mounted feed.
**Rationale**: Non-JS crawlers (the primary AI-search audience) are satisfied entirely by the server HTML, independent of any JS gate — the robust ~90%. Injecting *inside* `#root` (vs a sibling/`<noscript>`) is load-bearing: `createRoot` erasing it keeps steady-state humans gated, so 0a-HTML doesn't drag deal content into 0b's compliance territory (only a sub-second pre-hydration flash remains, accepted under the released go-ahead). `inert` is the single-attribute, spec-correct way to make gated content non-interactive + unfocusable + AT-hidden without a CSS/layout change or a focus-trap rewrite. Both gates had to be handled or 0b half-delivered for Googlebot.
**Consequences**: New `server/utils/renderShellBody.ts` (+test); `server/routes/shellRoute.ts` (+body injection, shared `buildApiData()`, root-marker guard mirroring the `</head>` guard, all fail-soft paths preserved: malformed/unreadable `data.json` → plain 200 shell, missing shell → 500); `client/src/components/AgeGate.tsx` + `client/src/App.tsx` (mount-behind-inert; focus-trap/warnings/attestation/onboarding auto-advance all preserved); tests updated (`shellRoute.test.ts` +body/`$$$`-safety, `AgeGate.test.tsx` present-but-inert contract, `App.test.tsx` +AC7 feed-inert-behind-onboarding). Additive/honesty-preserving — no `/api/data`/`Deal`/`data.json` change; deal `description` is already sanitized at the `normalizeDeals` chokepoint (compliance-launch-gate), escaped here not re-sanitized. **599 client + 726 server tests green; `npm run build` (real `tsc -b && vite build`) clean.** Live-verified against the built server (`NODE_ENV=production`, `curl -A GPTBot/1.0`): `/` returns real store names + deal text + the 21+ line inside `#root`, `__GMA_DATA__` intact, `$100` rendered uncorrupted. Closes the two deferred 0a-crawler-HTML items (`deferred-work.md`). Still open in the SEO plan (unchanged): rest of Phase 1 (OG/Twitter meta, per-store `LocalBusiness` JSON-LD), Phase 1a per-store routes, Phase 6 Search Console submission. Cross-ref ADR-052 (0a/0b risk split), ADR-066 (legal), ADR-078/079 (SSR pattern reused), ADR-082/092 (`__GMA_DATA__`/`__GMA_DROPS__` injection + the `$`-pattern lesson).

### ADR-098: `/healthz` keep-warm probe to defeat Render free-tier cold-start (SEO reach prerequisite)
**Status**: Accepted, then **SUPERSEDED in practice 2026-07-25 by ADR-101** (paid Render Starter = always-on, cold-start now impossible; the keep-warm is a redundant safety net). Implemented + 730 server tests/build green + live-boot-verified 2026-07-24; keep-warm monitor went LIVE 2026-07-25 (UptimeRobot #803591198, 5-min interval on `/healthz`, 100% uptime) — a few hours before the paid upgrade obsoleted it.
**Date**: 2026-07-24
**Context**: With the ADR-097 crawler-visible homepage live, the site's SSR'd deals are only reachable if the server is *awake*. gmaslist.com runs on Render's **free web tier, which spins the instance down after ~15 min idle** and serves Render's own "service waking up / application loading" holding page (~40s) on the next request while it cold-starts. AI crawlers (GPTBot, Google-Extended, PerplexityBot) do not wait — a crawl landing on a cold instance indexes Render's holding page, not our HTML, defeating the entire Phase-0 SSR unlock. The only cron today is the hourly `/api/ingest` POST, which wakes the instance once an hour but lets it re-idle ~45 min later, so most of each hour a crawl hits a cold instance. Reach is the current binding constraint (STRATEGY.md), so this gates everything downstream.
**Decision**: Erik chose the **free keep-warm route**. Add a dependency-free `GET /healthz` Express route (`server/routes/healthzRoute.ts`) returning `{ status, time }` — registered before the SPA fallback so it serves JSON, not the React shell; added to the Vite dev proxy for parity. An **external uptime monitor** (UptimeRobot / cron-job.org — Erik's account, outward-facing) pings it every ~5 min to hold the single instance awake. Deliberately rejected: (a) a **GitHub Actions cron** keep-warm — scheduled workflows are routinely delayed 10–40 min / skipped under load, unreliable against a 15-min spin-down window; (b) **paid always-on** (~$7/mo Render Starter) — deferred, not needed yet. Also declined an adjacent desktop-Claude GEO suggestion to add schema.org **Offer/Product** structured data — it conflicts with the not-a-seller posture (ADR-066); `LocalBusiness`/`Dataset` only.
**Rationale**: `/healthz` is the cheapest possible request that still wakes/holds the instance — no file reads or derivation (unlike `/` or `/api/data`), so each ping is near-free and can never 500 the keep-warm signal. Render free gives ~750 instance-hours/month per workspace; one web service kept awake 24/7 is ~730 hrs, under the cap, and the scraper runs locally (not on Render), so nothing else consumes those hours — the free route genuinely fits. An external pinger holds the 15-min window reliably where GitHub Actions cannot.
**Consequences**: New `server/routes/healthzRoute.ts` (+`healthzRoute.test.ts`, 2 tests); `server/index.ts` registers it before the SPA fallback; `client/vite.config.ts` proxies `/healthz`. Additive — no data/contract change, no compliance surface (returns no deal data). 730 server source tests green; `npm run build` clean; live-boot-verified (`node dist/server/index.js` → `GET /healthz` = 200 JSON). **Was made effective, then superseded the same day.** The monitor went live (UptimeRobot #803591198, target `https://gmaslist.com/healthz`, 5-min interval, "Up" 100%, ~140ms, verified 2026-07-25) — then Erik upgraded to paid Render Starter (**ADR-101**), which holds the instance awake at the platform level and makes both the monitor and this keep-warm route redundant safety nets (`/healthz` is retained as a plain health probe). The "revisit → paid always-on" note below **happened 2026-07-25 (see ADR-101)**; it remains the fallback rationale should the free tier ever be reconsidered (e.g. a second Render free service) or if crawl-time cold hits persist despite the monitor. Cross-ref ADR-097 (the SSR this protects), ADR-033 (parked paid hosting), ADR-066 (not-a-seller schema posture).

### ADR-099: Per-store pages `/store/<id>` with LocalBusiness JSON-LD (SEO Phase 1a / CAP-3 + CAP-4)
**Status**: Accepted (implemented + 743 server tests/build green + live-boot-verified 2026-07-24)
**Date**: 2026-07-24
**Context**: After the ADR-097 crawler-visible homepage, the site still had no dedicated URL per dispensary — the only store-specific content was one card inside the homepage feed, not separately indexable and not a citable answer for "Remedy Tulalip deals" / "dispensary deals Tulalip WA." SEO-plan CAP-3 (stable per-store routes) and CAP-4 (structured data) were the largest remaining reach lever. Reach is the binding constraint (STRATEGY.md).
**Decision**: New `GET /store/:slug` Express route (`server/routes/storeRoute.ts`), registered before the SPA fallback and unconditional (dev too), mirroring the `/about` (ADR-078) / `/compare` (ADR-079) SSR pattern. One server-rendered page per store — `<h1>` name, address, a `rel="nofollow"` link to the store's own site, the store's CURRENT active deals (from the SAME `buildApiData()` the feed uses), the WA 21+/health notice, and internal links to `/about` + `/compare`. Each page carries **`LocalBusiness` JSON-LD** (name, structured `PostalAddress` parsed from the committed address, `GeoCoordinates` from lat/lng, `sameAs` = the store's own site, `areaServed` = Washington). Discovery: every store `<h2>` in the homepage crawler-HTML (`renderShellBody`) now links to `/store/<id>`, and `sitemap.xml` enumerates one `/store/<id>` per live store (fail-soft — unreadable data.json → no store URLs, never a 500). Two decisions ratified by Erik (2026-07-24): **distance is OMITTED** from the crawlable HTML (it is user-relative — a crawler has no location, so any number would be fabricated), and **deals stay "active today"** with no fabricated `priceValidUntil` (they churn hourly; page cached 5 min).
**Rationale**: `LocalBusiness` only — deliberately NOT `Product`/`Offer`/`AggregateOffer`, which imply a seller/advertiser and collide with the not-a-seller posture (ADR-066, WAC 314-55-155); we describe the store as a *place that exists*, not a priced menu. geo is emitted only for real finite lat/lng (Honest Math). `url` in the JSON-LD is THIS page (the canonical describing-page); the store's real site is `sameAs`, linking the two as one entity. No new data path or exposure — all fields already public via `/api/data` and the homepage. An unreadable data.json → 500 (transient server fault), NOT a misleading 404; an unknown slug → 404 (short 60s cache, a store may be added later).
**Consequences**: New `server/routes/storeRoute.ts` (+`storeRoute.test.ts`, 9 tests: render, JSON-LD address/geo/no-Offer, distance-omitted, empty-deals honest state, 404, hostile-text escape, 500, + 2 unit); `server/utils/renderShellBody.ts` (store headings now link to `/store/<id>`; exported `AGE_NOTICE`; +link test); `server/routes/sitemapRoute.ts` (`buildSitemapXml` takes `storeSlugs`, fail-soft `readStoreSlugs` via `buildApiData`; +2 pure +1 route test); `server/index.ts` registers the route; `client/vite.config.ts` proxies `/store`. Additive, honesty-preserving — no `/api/data`/`Deal`/`data.json` change. **743 server source tests green; `npm run build` clean; live-boot-verified** (`GET /store/remedy-tulalip` → 200 with LocalBusiness JSON-LD incl. PostalAddress + geo + sameAs, no distance word, canonical correct; sitemap lists 18 `/store/` URLs; unknown slug → 404). Deferred fast-follows: per-store "real price drops" ($X vs usual) on the page (highest-value crawlable specificity, keyed off `price-vs-own-median.json`); OG/Twitter meta; human-facing SPA links to `/store` pages. Cross-ref ADR-078/079 (SSR pattern), ADR-080 (sitemap), ADR-066 (not-a-seller schema line), ADR-097 (homepage crawler HTML this links from).

---

### ADR-100: "Real price drops" on per-store pages (the honest per-item discount, SSR)
**Status**: Accepted (implemented + 749 server tests/build green 2026-07-25)
**Date**: 2026-07-25
**Context**: ADR-099's `/store/<id>` pages listed active deals + `LocalBusiness` JSON-LD but omitted the single most citable specificity we already compute — the **honest per-item discount** (a SKU priced below its OWN rolling median; price-vs-own-median, FR13/Gate 2). A crawler asking "what's actually cheap at Remedy Tulalip" got the banner deals but not the "$9 vs usual $18" fact that is the derivation engine's flagship output. This was the highest-value ADR-099 deferred fast-follow.
**Decision**: Render a **"Real price drops"** section on each `/store/<id>` page from the served `price-vs-own-median.json` fact (via `readPriceVsOwnMedian`, the SAME reader the in-app card + shell snapshot use). `storeRoute.storeDrops(store)` filters the served rows to this store's `dispensaryId`, sorts deepest-discount-first, and caps at 12; `renderStoreHtml` renders each as **plain prose** — `"{name} ({option}) — {pct}% below its usual: ${current} vs ${median} usual"`. Honesty gates mirror the in-app surface **verbatim** (ValueDropStrip / DealFeed): a **failed/stale store carries NO drops** (its current price — the drop's numerator — is itself untrustworthy), and a **sub-1%-display mover is suppressed** (`renderableStoreDrops`: `pctVsMedian < 0 && round(|pct|·100) ≥ 1`) so no "0% below its usual" ever renders.
**Rationale**: Prose only — deliberately NOT `Offer`/`Product`/`AggregateOffer` JSON-LD, holding the not-a-seller posture (ADR-066): we state an observed fact about price movement, not a priced offer to sell. The banner/promo % is a flat store/brand rate with no per-item signal and is structurally absent from this fact (fix6 / Gate 2) — so a below-own-median drop is the ONLY discount magnitude honest enough to surface. Fail-soft: a missing/malformed `price-vs-own-median.json` degrades to no drops section (never a 500), so the page is unchanged when the home derive hasn't republished. Additive to ADR-099 — no data path, contract, or JSON-LD change.
**Consequences**: `server/routes/storeRoute.ts` gains `storeDrops` + `renderableStoreDrops` + `dropPercent`/`money` helpers (mirrors `ValueDropStrip.tsx`) and `renderStoreHtml(store, drops)`; `storeRoute.test.ts` +6 tests (route drops-render + no-Offer/Product + other-store-isolation + empty-omit; `storeDrops` unit: store-filter/sort, 12-cap, failed/stale gate; `renderStoreHtml` unit: sub-1% display gate). The store-page tests now mock `readPriceVsOwnMedian` so they never read the real committed derived file. **749 server tests green; `npm run build` clean.** Cross-ref ADR-087 (`/api/value/price-vs-own-median` + the fact), ADR-088 (in-card `ValueDropStrip`, the gates this mirrors), ADR-099 (the page this extends), ADR-066 (not-a-seller schema line).

---

### ADR-101: Paid Render Starter (always-on) supersedes the free-tier keep-warm
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-07-25
**Context**: ADR-098 defeated Render free-tier cold-start (a crawl landing on a spun-down instance indexes Render's "service waking up" holding page, not our SSR HTML) with a free external keep-warm pinger. That works but is a workaround: it depends on UptimeRobot's 5-min schedule, any missed window risks a cold hit, and the free tier is capped (~750 instance-hours/mo, 512MB RAM). Reach is the binding constraint (STRATEGY.md), and the SSR stack (ADR-097–100) only pays off if crawlers reliably reach live HTML.
**Decision**: Erik upgraded the **single existing** Render web service ("Gma", `srv-d8ni2ikm0tmc73e5uhcg`, created 2026-06-14, url `gma-zr94.onrender.com`, `gmaslist.com` custom domain attached) from Free to **Starter (~$7/mo)**. Verified via the Render API 2026-07-25: exactly ONE web service in the workspace, `plan: starter`, `numInstances: 1`, autoDeploy on `master` commit — i.e. an in-place upgrade, NOT a second service, so there is no duplicate and no double-billing. Starter instances do not spin down, so cold-start is structurally impossible.
**Rationale**: Platform-level always-on is strictly stronger than an external pinger — no dependency on a monitor's schedule, no missed-window cold hit, and more RAM headroom. $7/mo is trivial against the reach investment and removes the last availability risk on the crawler path. The build/deploy that confirmed the upgrade came from the same repo/branch (`erikchvac-byte/Gma`@`master`), built clean (`npm run build`), and went live serving the current SSR bundle.
**Consequences**: The ADR-098 `/healthz` route + UptimeRobot monitor (#803591198) are now **redundant safety nets**, not load-bearing. `/healthz` is retained as a plain health probe (candidate for Render's `healthCheckPath` for auto-restart, currently unset); the UptimeRobot monitor is **kept as a free downtime alerter** (Erik's decision, 2026-07-25) — no longer load-bearing for keep-warm, but it now emails if the site ever goes down for a real reason. Supersedes ADR-098's free-route rationale. Distinct from ADR-033 (parked paid Docker **scraper** hosting) — the scraper still runs locally and is unaffected; this upgrade is the Happy **web** service only. No code change; seed-cron ingest, local scraper pipeline, and every SSR route are untouched. Cross-ref ADR-098 (keep-warm this supersedes), ADR-097–100 (the SSR stack this protects), ADR-033 (parked scraper hosting, separate concern).

### ADR-102: Sitemap submitted to Google Search Console; Porkbun DNS record set corrected after a URL-forwarding incident
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-07-25
**Context**: Phase 6 was the last outward SEO step — submit `sitemap.xml` to Google Search Console so the engine is *actively told* "come index me" instead of waiting to be discovered. Reach is the binding constraint (STRATEGY.md), and the whole SSR + sitemap + per-store-page stack (ADR-078–100) is inert until a search engine is pointed at it. `site:gmaslist.com` had been ~0 pages.
**Decision**: Verified `gmaslist.com` ownership in Google Search Console via a **DNS TXT record** (`google-site-verification=iGaSRKM0GOZM7nGfvO3wzBoH34g9ha35L_iP9ZLH-HY`) added at **Porkbun** (the domain's DNS host; nameservers `*.ns.porkbun.com`), then **submitted `sitemap.xml` successfully** (26 URLs: `/`, `/about`, 5× `/compare/*`, 18× `/store/*`). Google now periodically re-processes it and alerts on errors.
**Incident (≈1hr outage during setup)**: Adding the TXT via Porkbun's **"URL Forwarding"** flow silently **deleted the apex service record and parked the domain** at `207.207.210.x` → 302 `gmaslist-com.l.ink` (a generic 21+ parking page). Symptoms cascaded confusingly: the site "disappeared," then www appeared "down" while `gma-zr94.onrender.com` worked (www's CNAME survived and 301'd to a then-unresolvable apex). Diagnosed from authoritative NS + 8.8.8.8 lookups (not the DNS panel). **Recovery**: delete the URL forward (URL-Forwarding tab, trash icon — do NOT hit Submit), then restore the correct record set in the DNS-Records tab.
**Correct record set (recovery runbook)**: `ALIAS @ → gma-zr94.onrender.com` (Porkbun flattens ALIAS to Render's A; fallback `A @ → 216.24.57.1`); `CNAME www → gma-zr94.onrender.com` (Render 301s www→apex); `TXT @` google-site-verification + `TXT @` SPF; plus Render's two `TXT _acme-challenge` records (managed Let's Encrypt TLS — leave them). Verified live: apex resolves to Render (`216.24.57.7`/`.15`) on both Porkbun NS and 8.8.8.8, `https://gmaslist.com/` → **HTTP 200 valid cert** (Express), `sitemap.xml` → 200, `www` → 301→apex.
**Rationale**: ALIAS at apex (Porkbun supports it) auto-tracks Render's edge IPs — cleaner than a hardcoded A. **Never enable Porkbun URL Forwarding on this domain** — it wipes the service records. This also finally closes the long-stale 2026-06-15 "stuck `173.245.58.37` ghost record" deploy blocker: the domain is cleanly live with managed TLS.
**Consequences**: No code change — DNS console + Google console only. **STILL OPEN (outward, Erik's action): Bing Webmaster Tools sitemap submission** (can "Import from Google Search Console" to skip re-verification). Full record set + the URL-forwarding hazard captured in memory `reference_gmaslist-dns-config`. Cross-ref ADR-032/ADR-101 (prior DNS + Render-Starter history), ADR-080 (the sitemap route), ADR-097–100 (the SSR stack this submission activates).

---

### ADR-103: Explicit per-agent AI-crawler Allow groups in robots.txt + homepage canonical
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-07-26
**Context**: Two AI/SEO-reach gaps surfaced while validating the ADR-102 submission. (1) Google's Gemini browser reported a `URL_FETCH_STATUS_GOOGLE_EXTENDED_OPT_OUT` fetch failure for `gmaslist.com` and then **fabricated an "audit" from the failure state** (it admitted no page data was retrieved). Live verification showed the site is **not** opted out — the `User-agent: * / Allow: /` group already covers `Google-Extended` by robots.txt fallback — but some AI tools read the *absence of a dedicated agent group* conservatively. (2) GSC URL Inspection on `/` showed **User-declared canonical: None** — the homepage was the only key page without a `<link rel="canonical">` (the `/about`, `/compare`, `/store/*` SSR routes each already emit one; Google was self-selecting the correct canonical, so this was a consistency gap, not a live defect).
**Decision**: (1) In `robotsRoute` (`server/routes/sitemapRoute.ts`), keep the `*` catch-all and add **explicit `User-agent: <token> / Allow: / / Disallow: /api/` groups** for 13 named AI/citation crawlers, generated from an `AI_CRAWLER_AGENTS` list: **training/model-usage** — `Google-Extended`, `GPTBot`, `ClaudeBot`, `Applebot-Extended`, `CCBot`, `Meta-ExternalAgent`; **citation/live-answer** — `OAI-SearchBot`, `ChatGPT-User`, `PerplexityBot`, `Perplexity-User`, `Claude-SearchBot`, `Claude-User`, `Bingbot`. (2) Add a self-referencing `<link rel="canonical" href="https://gmaslist.com/" />` to `client/index.html` (shipped PR #106), trailing slash matching the sitemap `/` entry + Organization `url`.
**Rationale**: The `*` group already allowed every agent (Phase-3 "allow all"), so this **changes no actual crawl access** — it makes the allow intent **un-misreadable as an opt-out** for each named AI bot. A named robots group is self-contained (a crawler that matches it ignores `*`), so each group repeats `Disallow: /api/`. **Honest caveat:** this may not change Gemini's behavior — its fetch failed for its own reasons and no robots.txt edit fixes a client-side tool failure — but explicit allows are the correct, on-plan hardening regardless. The canonical locks tracking-param / trailing-slash variants of the homepage to one URL and matches its siblings.
**Consequences**: `ROBOTS_TXT` now emits **14 `User-agent` groups** (was 1); the robots test is rewritten to assert every named group Allows, the `*` group persists, no group ever emits a bare full-site `Disallow: /`, and there is exactly one `Disallow: /api/` per group. Homepage canonical flows into the SSR shell (`shellRoute` injects into the built `index.html`), guarded by a new `indexHtml.test` assertion. **Operational note for future sessions:** Gemini's browsing tool confabulates plausible audits from fetch-failure states — treat its "findings" as unreliable unless the fetch demonstrably succeeded; GSC URL Inspection "Test live URL" is the authoritative crawl-access check. Build clean; sitemap suite 20 green, `indexHtml` 3 green. Cross-ref ADR-080 (original robots.txt/sitemap), ADR-078 (the `/about` canonical/JSON-LD pattern), ADR-102 (GSC/Bing submission this validates).

---

### ADR-104: Open Graph + Twitter Card social meta on every page
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-07-26
**Context**: Social-preview meta were the last open Phase-1 SEO item. The homepage and all four SSR surfaces (`/about`, `/compare`, `/compare/:category`, `/store/:slug`) emitted `<title>`/`description`/canonical but **no `og:*` or `twitter:*` tags**, so a link shared to Facebook / LinkedIn / iMessage / Slack / X rendered bare — no title card, no image, no description.
**Decision**: New shared `server/utils/socialMeta.ts` — `socialMetaTags({ title, description, url, type?, image?, imageAlt? })` returns the full OG + Twitter block and **self-escapes** so untrusted input (e.g. a store name in `og:title`) can't break out of the attribute. Wired into `aboutRoute`, `compareRoute`'s shared `page()`, and `storeRoute`'s `renderStoreHtml`. The homepage (`client/index.html`, static) carries an equivalent hardcoded block. **og:image** = the branded banner `client/public/og-image.png` served at a stable `/og-image.png`; card type is **`summary_large_image`**. (Initially shipped as `summary` off a placeholder `hero.png`; replaced 2026-07-26 with Erik's real 2400×1260 branded image — "gma's list. / Is it worth the drive?" — and the orphan `hero.png` deleted.)
**Rationale**: One generator = an identical tag set + identical escaping on every surface (the homepage's static copy is the only deliberate duplicate, guarded by `indexHtml.test`). Reused Erik's existing hero art rather than inventing new art (the deal-icon-style precedent: use his art, don't redraw). `summary` not `summary_large_image` because the only asset is square/small — honest to what exists; a wide asset upgrades the card later. No compliance change: the OG copy reuses the same WA-scoped, not-a-seller description already shipped.
**Consequences**: New `socialMeta.ts` (+`socialMeta.test.ts`, 5 cases incl. attribute-escaping + type override); OG/Twitter presence asserted in `aboutRoute`/`compareRoute`/`storeRoute`/`indexHtml` tests. `og:image` is a static `public/` asset (served by `express.static` + Vite's public copy → verified in `dist/`). Closes the Phase-1 OG/Twitter item — the last of the SEO plan's page-level meta work. **Deferred:** per-page distinct OG images (all pages share the one branded banner). Cross-ref ADR-078 (`/about` entity + JSON-LD), ADR-099/100 (store pages), ADR-103 (homepage canonical + robots groups, same reach pass).

---

### ADR-105: Three fixes from the 2026-07-26 technical-SEO audit (llms.txt stores, asset immutable cache, trailing-slash 301)
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-07-26
**Context**: An external technical-SEO / AI-search audit of gmaslist.com was verified against live headers + source (all claims true; two flagged items — sitemap `lastmod` omission and homepage `no-cache` — confirmed *deliberate and correct*, not defects). Three findings were worth acting on; scored by reach-impact (the binding constraint) × effort, these three won. Security headers (HSTS/CSP), long meta descriptions, and store `lastmod` were ranked below the cut (~zero reach impact / not worth the CSP breakage risk).
**Decision**: (1) **llms.txt stores** — `buildLlmsTxt` gained a `stores` param and a `## Stores` section listing every `/store/<id>` with its display name; `llmsTxtRoute` feeds it via a new fail-soft `readStores()`. The sitemap already listed store pages; llms.txt omitted them, hiding the richest citable entity surface from LLM agents. Names are `[`/`]`-stripped so a store name can't break the Markdown link. (2) **Asset immutable cache** — `express.static` gained a `setHeaders` that stamps `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` on anything under `/assets/` (Vite content-hashes those filenames, so the bytes are immutable); root files (favicon, og-image) keep the default short cache since their names are stable. Fixes the audit's "fingerprinted assets served `max-age=0`" finding. (3) **Trailing-slash 301** — new `middleware/trailingSlashRedirect.ts`, mounted before all routes, 301s `/about/` → `/about`. Guards: GET/HEAD only (never rewrite the `/api/ingest` POST), never `/api*`, never root `/`, query string preserved, multi-slash collapsed. Kills the duplicate crawlable slash/no-slash URL twins (canonical tags already pointed no-slash; the 301 removes the dupe outright).
**Rationale**: These three move crawl/citation signal or repeat-visit perf at near-zero effort and near-zero risk given the guards; the deferred items (security headers, meta length) are generic best-practice noise against this property's actual constraint (reach, not hardening). Immutable cache scoped to `/assets/` only — a blanket long cache would pin a stale favicon. Trailing-slash redirect implemented as isolated, unit-tested middleware rather than per-route logic so the `/api` + root + method guards live in one auditable place.
**Consequences**: New `middleware/trailingSlashRedirect.ts` (+ 8-case test: page 301, deep path, root pass-through, `/api*` untouched, query preserved, POST pass-through, canonical untouched, multi-slash collapse). `sitemapRoute.test.ts` +3 cases (stores section present/absent, de-dupe+sort+bracket-safety). Server source suite 765/765; full build green. **Verify post-deploy** (301 is browser-sticky): `curl -I` sweep of `/about/`, `/store/<id>/`, `/` (must stay 200), `/api/data/` (must not 301); and confirm `/llms.txt` lists all 18 stores + an `/assets/*.js` returns the immutable header. Cross-ref ADR-080 (robots/sitemap), ADR-081 (llms.txt origin), ADR-104 (same reach pass).

---

### ADR-106: AI-citation monitor — Phase-0 reach instrumentation
**Status**: Accepted
**Date**: 2026-07-27
**Context**: Reach is the binding constraint (STRATEGY.md). A 2026-07-27 brainstorm produced a phased reach plan (`_bmad-output/brainstorming/brainstorming-session-2026-07-26-launch-reach.md`) whose Phase-0 first move Erik named to build: a **citation monitor** — a scheduled job that asks AI answer engines the natural-language questions real WA shoppers ask ("cheapest weed near Marysville") and records whether gmaslist.com is *cited* in the grounded answer. Stand it up FIRST to capture a baseline before the page-machine work, so every later gain is measurable; it doubles as a dead-man's-switch that catches when a page stops being cited.
**Decision**: New **local-only** tooling mirroring the ingest/derive runner split (`server/scripts/*.ts` logic+tests, root `scripts/*.ps1` orchestration). Pure logic in `server/scripts/citationMonitor.ts`: hostname-scoped citation match (apex + subdomain only; lookalike domains like `gmaslist.com.attacker.net` rejected), a hard `cited` signal from cited SOURCE urls vs a soft `mentionedInText`, and a per-engine summary. IO in `server/scripts/aiCitationRun.ts`: pluggable `CitationEngine`s — a keyless `dry-run` (zero cost, always runnable) and `anthropic` (Claude + the server-side `web_search_20260209` tool, US/Washington `user_location`, `pause_turn` resume loop). Target questions in an editable `citation-questions.json` (8 WA-local deal/comparison seeds; later fed by Phase-0 targeting research). Appends one JSONL observation per (engine, question) to a LOCAL log (`~/GmaS-data/citation-log.jsonl`) — never committed, like `products.db`. Runner `scripts/ai-citation-local.ps1` (no git worktree — commits nothing, picks up question edits) + `scripts/setup-ai-citation-task.ps1` (daily 05:00 Scheduled Task).
**Rationale**: Native `fetch` + the documented Anthropic wire format, **not** `@anthropic-ai/sdk` — keeps the load-bearing `server/package.json` (Render auto-deploys on push) free of a prod-installed dependency no served route uses, and adds zero build/deploy surface. Pluggable engines match the "data source is a commodity" instinct (Perplexity/OpenAI adapters drop in as more `CitationEngine`s). Key in `server/.env` (gitignored). Local-only mirrors the moat/reach split (ADR-077): Render serves committed facts; measurement lives on the home machine.
**Consequences**: `citationMonitor.test.ts` (13 cases: hostname edge cases incl. lookalike rejection, cited/mentioned/dedupe, summary roll-up). **Testing**: 13/13 green; dry-run writes JSONL end-to-end; `tsc --noEmit` clean (won't break the Render build). **NOT yet scheduled** — `setup-ai-citation-task.ps1` is written but unrun (Scheduled-Task registration needs Erik's explicit go-ahead). Live (non-dry) runs need `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in `server/.env`; without it the monitor auto-falls-back to dry-run. Anthropic web-search cost applies per live run (~8 questions × search). Cross-ref STRATEGY.md (reach binding), `project_reach-launch-plan` memory (Phase 0), ADR-077 (the local-only home-machine pattern this mirrors).

---

## Technical Constraints

- US only, WA-focused; distance is now user-relative (ADR-057) — measured from the visitor's device GPS or typed WA ZIP, not a fixed origin
- Distance = `haversine(user, store) × 1.3` road factor, computed client-side from committed store `lat`/`lng` (ADR-044) + a committed WA ZCTA centroid table (ADR-057); no routing API. Retires the pre-computed fixed-origin `distanceMiles` (ADR-008/011)
- Deal data sourced by scraping public dispensary sites (Axios + Cheerio for plain HTML; Python Scraper microservice on port 8000 for Dutchie/iFrame sites — see ADR-017)
- Gas-cost calc: MPG comes ONLY from the user's vehicle via fueleconomy.gov (ADR-028); the hardcoded national-average default was removed (ADR-013 retired by ADR-055) — no vehicle → no gas figure (distance still shows)
- Gas price: EIA public API (weekly refresh), stored in data.json meta
- All active-deal time logic uses America/Los_Angeles (Pacific Time), enforced server-side
- Browser-based only; user preferences (distance, vehicle MPG, age confirmation) persisted in localStorage
- Web app must be mobile-responsive (primary use case: checking from a phone before getting in the car)
- fueleconomy.gov API returns XML by default — must send `Accept: application/json` header
- data.json writes must be atomic (tmp file + rename) to prevent serving partial data

---

## Testing Results

- **R&D accuracy bar (6-month):** Small test group confirms displayed deals match real-world deals at stores, and gas-cost math holds up. No traffic/revenue targets at this stage.

## Known Issues

- ~~**DEPLOY BLOCKER — stuck Porkbun `173.245.58.37` ghost record (2026-06-15):**~~ **RESOLVED (confirmed 2026-07-25, ADR-102).** `gmaslist.com` is cleanly live with managed TLS: apex `ALIAS @ → gma-zr94.onrender.com` resolves to Render (`216.24.57.7`/`.15`) on both Porkbun NS and 8.8.8.8, serves HTTP 200 with a valid cert, and `www` 301s to apex. The old ghost `A → 173.245.58.37` is gone. **Standing hazard (do NOT reintroduce the blocker):** never enable Porkbun **URL Forwarding** on this domain — it silently deletes the apex service record and parks the domain (this recurred 2026-07-25 during GSC TXT setup; recovery runbook in ADR-102 + memory `reference_gmaslist-dns-config`). See ADR-032/ADR-102.
- **Live scraping deferred on Render (env-var half done):** the scrape target is now `SCRAPER_URL`-overridable (ADR-033; default `http://localhost:8000/scrape` unchanged), so the code is go-live-ready. What remains is **deploying** the Python Scraper service + leaving Happy's free tier — **deferred pending budget** (Erik 2026-06-15, free-tier spin-down + Playwright RAM/shm make truly-free live scraping non-viable). Until then `/api/data` serves last-known/bundled data. Recommended topology + costs: ADR-033; go-live steps: `docs/deploy-scraper-runbook.md`; parked in `deferred-work.md`.
- **`render.yaml` is non-authoritative:** the live service was created manually and ignores it; the real build logic lives in the root `package.json` `build` script — do not revert it (ADR-032).
- Deal scraping fragility and ToS exposure: unresolved — ToS review required before expanding beyond the R&D dispensary set (PRD OQ-5)
- Discount Display (side-by-side % + gas cost) may be insufficient as a go/no-go signal — validated by SM-3 in R&D (see ADR-009)
- fueleconomy.gov API response format: must send `Accept: application/json` — default is XML
- Initial data.json requires a one-time manual setup (dispensary list + distanceMiles) before first scraper run
- **Dutchie sources not yet live (4.3 live pass pending):** `jet-cannabis-everett` and `kush21-everett-evergreen` ship with an unresolved embed id (`STORE_ID=''`) and report Stale until discovered live; the Dutchie `GetSpecialMenuCards`→`Deal[]` transform is validated only against a synthesized fixture and must be reconciled against a real capture (discount unit fraction-vs-percent, time-window field path). Requires the Python Scraper service (`C:\Users\erikc\Dev\Scraper`, :8000) running. See `epic-4-retro-2026-06-13.md` and `deferred-work.md`.
- **Scraped-data boundary hardening not done:** `useDeals`/ingestion validation of dispensary shape (null/NaN `distanceMiles`, null array elements can crash `DealCard`) and zero-length-window normalization were deferred to "Epic 4" by 2.3/2.5/2.6 reviews but not implemented — promoted to a follow-up story in the Epic 4 retrospective.
- **No real `EIA_API_KEY` in `.env`:** the server warns and keeps the seed gas price until the key is added.

| 2026-07-21 | **ADR-095 added — data-acquisition strategy: build a better information platform, don't buy a licensed feed.** From `investigations/licensed-data-source-options-2026-07-21.md`: the three paid paths (Dutchie/Weedmaps official APIs = structurally ineligible as non-owner; CannMenus licensed aggregator = sales-gated enterprise, "removes ToS risk" claim UNVERIFIED since aggregators likely scrape too; Apify managed scrapers = same ToS posture, no legitimacy gain) are **risk transfer, not better data** — you pay to hand off the ToS/legal bag and the anti-bot pager, and a raw feed can't replace the derivation engine that is the actual moat. **Decision: stay self-hosted + multi-source; treat the data source as a pluggable commodity beneath the engine; keep diversifying so no single C&D is fatal; close four self-inflicted gaps as Layer-2 stories** — (1) `buildMatchReport` staleness gate (half-specced from the Epic-2 retro), (2) get WebFetch out of the honesty-critical path (DOM-verify offers), (3) dead-man's-switch on ingest (silent-outage alerting), (4) formally decouple acquisition from the derivation engine. CannMenus pricing inquiry deferred. Strategic decision — no code artifact; verification is the Layer-2 stories' ACs. |

| 2026-06-22 | **Live-data freshness fixes (two PRs).** (1) **PR #24 (`4f6dac9`) — repopulate prod on deploy:** added `push: branches: [master]` to `scrape-ingest.yml`. Render's disk is ephemeral so every redeploy resets `data.json` to the committed seed; since every master push fires the Render deploy-hook, re-scraping on push refills prod within ~3 min instead of waiting up to an hour for the hourly cron. Proven live — the merge push auto-triggered run `27933398784`. (2) **ADR-034 §6 alert semantics AMENDED — stop alerting on a single empty/stale store** (Erik's call). Root cause of the chronic red runs: `ingestRun` exited non-zero on any non-`ok` per-store result, so one empty scrape (a store with no specials that hour — `stale`, last-known-good kept) reddened the whole workflow; failing stores rotated run-to-run (even no-Python `remedy`), proving transient-empty not infra. Now: **(a)** `ingestRun` treats `stale` as acceptable (`ok` stays true; only `error`/`unknown`/POST-throw/missing-scraper fail); **(b)** the per-store `scrape` matrix job runs `continue-on-error: true` so no single store reds the run; **(c)** a new **`alert-gate`** job (`needs: scrape`, `if: always()`) is the SOLE alert — pure `evaluateAlert` in `server/scripts/alertGate.ts` reads prod `/api/data` and reds only on **total failure** (no store fresh within 3h) or **persistent staleness** (a previously-ingested store stale > `STALE_ALERT_MS` = 6h ≈ 6 runs). **Never-ingested epoch-seed stores (the 18 Dutchie with `lastFetchedAt` 1970) are EXCLUDED from the stale check** so they don't false-alarm after a deploy. A single empty run keeps last-known-good → stays fresh → no alert. Tests: +11 `alertGate.test.ts` (total-failure, persistent-stale, grace band, epoch-excluded, malformed/skew), updated `ingestRun.test.ts` (stale→ok=true + mixed ok/stale). Server suite 167 green; `tsc` + full build clean. |

| 2026-06-22 | **ADR-047 added — commit-back seed (durable live data across Render deploys).** Correct-Course follow-up making Render's ephemeral-disk reset non-destructive: a new `commit-back` job in `scrape-ingest.yml` (`needs: scrape`, `if: always()`, `continue-on-error: true`) downloads per-store scrape artifacts and merges them into the checked-out `data.json` via new `server/scripts/commitBackSeed.ts` → the existing **`applyIngest`** (NOT `GET /api/data`, which `filterActiveDeals` makes lossy — would progressively delete out-of-window/wrong-weekday deals), then commits only `data.json` to master (`chore(data): refresh seed [skip ci]`, `pull --rebase` + 1 push retry). `ingestRun.ts` gains an additive `--emit`/`INGEST_EMIT_DIR` writing each normalized `IngestEntry` before the POST (one scrape feeds both); the upload step is `if: always()` so a POST that loses the deploy-cutover race (exit 1) still ships the seed. Loop-breaker: `paths-ignore: ['server/data/data.json']` on the `push` trigger (+ `[skip ci]`), in-repo, no Render-dashboard dependency; `permissions: contents: write` (master unprotected); `git add` scoped to `data.json` only; `server/out/` gitignored. **Supersedes PR#24's push-re-scrape as the durability mechanism** (push trigger retained only for immediate refresh); `alert-gate` stays the SOLE alert (push failure → `::warning::`, never reds). Tests: new `commitBackSeed.test.ts` (fresh-replace, AC2 no-loss invariant, absent-store untouched, time-inactive-deal-survives-vs-`filterActiveDeals`, empty-dir no-op, malformed-artifact skip) + `ingestRun.test.ts` emit/`parseEmitDir` cases. Server suite green; full `npm run build` clean. |
| 2026-06-22 | **ADR-047 first live run (PR#29 merge, run `27980954922`) + autostash fix.** Validated end-to-end: the `commit-back` job ran, merged 21 stores (16 `ok`, 5 `stale` last-known-good kept) via `applyIngest`, and built a genuinely fresh seed commit (`c4d129e`, +551/−44). **Bug found:** the job's `npm install` rewrites the tracked `server/package-lock.json`, leaving an unstaged change that aborts `git pull --rebase` (`cannot pull with rebase: You have unstaged changes`) → both push retries failed → the `::warning::` fired (non-fatal, as designed; the separate `alert-gate` red was the expected post-deploy `fresh=0` cutover signal). Seed was NOT pushed that run. **Fix:** `git pull --rebase --autostash origin master` parks the incidental lockfile churn across the rebase; only `data.json` is committed. The visible-not-fatal warning path proved its worth — a silent failure would have masked this. |
